第一章 绪论

Chapter 1 Introduction

01. 陕西古建筑的生成背景

Formation Background of Ancient Architecture in Shaanxi
概述

陕西古称秦川，简称陕或秦，又称三秦，是中华民族及华夏文化的重要发源地。从距今115万~110万年前的蓝田人开始，先民们就在这片土地上生息劳作，陕西是史前人类文明最集中的活动区域。历史上，先后有秦、西汉、隋、唐等14个政权在陕西建都，是我国历史上建都朝代最多、时间最长的省份。

它见证了华夏文明的兴衰更迭，留下了极为丰厚的历史文化遗产，陕西古建筑就是其中之一。陕西古建筑类型多样，现存陕西古建筑中有全国重点文物保护单位200余处，陕西省文物保护单位400余处，合计354处；年代始于汉，迄于明清；

类别包括城垣、仓廪、街镇、台榭、园林、堰坝、路桥、古塔、楼阁、牌坊、寺院、宫观、祠庙、民居、会馆、书院、考院等。

陕西古建筑文化灿烂，先民们在漫长岁月中形成成熟独特的建筑体系，在城市规划、建筑群体等宏观层面，园林、祠庙、民居等中观层面以及建筑空间布局、建筑艺术、建筑材料结构、施工技术等微观层面均体现出卓绝的智慧。

陕西古建筑业绩辉煌，西周的都城丰镐、秦都咸阳、西汉长安、隋唐长安，都是当时中国政治、经济、文化的中心，尤其是中国封建社会鼎盛时期的唐长安城，其建筑规模和建筑水平是中国乃至世界古代建设史上前所未有。

一、自然环境：陕西地处中国内陆腹地、黄河中游，东经105度29分至111度15分、北纬31度42分至39度35分之间。

全省南北长约870公里，东西长约200~500公里，总面积20.58万平方公里，约占全国总面积的2.1%。

陕西与山西、河南、湖北、四川、甘肃、宁夏、内蒙古7个省、自治区毗邻，是新亚欧大陆桥和中国西北、西南、华北、华中之间的门户。陕西全省呈南北高、中间低，西北高、东南低的地形特征，北山和秦岭把陕西分为三大自然区域：北部的陕北高原，南部的秦巴山区与中部的关中平原。

其地貌类型可划分为风沙过渡区、黄土高原区、关中平原区、秦岭山地区、汉江盆地区和大巴山地区六个典型区域。其中，黄土高原区面积最大，约占全省面积的45%。

陕西地处我国中纬地带，由于南北狭长，所跨纬度多，兼跨黄河、长江流域，因此气候的地带性差异特征明显：长城沿线以北为温带干旱半干旱气候，陕北其余地区为暖温带半干旱气候，关中平原为暖温带半湿润气候，陕南盆地为北亚热带湿润气候、山地大部分为暖温带湿润气候。陕西受季风影响，冬冷夏热、四季分明。

二、人文环境：秦汉以后，陕西成为中国典型跨越南北的省份之一，由于自然地理条件、文化功能和文化沿革等原因，逐渐形成陕北黄土高原文化区、关中平原文化区和陕南秦巴山地文化区。

1. 陕北地区——黄土风情：陕北地区地处黄土高原丘陵沟壑区，北有毛乌素沙漠、东有九曲黄河、南有子午岭，是典型的黄土塬、梁、峁、沟地貌区，其日照充足，昼夜温差大，冬天气候寒冷干燥，气温低，雨雪稀少。

陕北地广人稀，土壤贫瘠，深厚的黄土层，半干旱的温带气候，人们传统上多采取窑洞式半穴居居住方式和半农半牧的生产方式，民风强悍，崇尚武力。陕北自古为边塞民族融合之地，其地域文化以黄土文化、黄河文化、边塞文化、黄帝文化为主要特征。

2. 关中地区——渭水秦川：关中地区指渭河流域之中西起宝鸡、东至潼关、南抵秦岭、北达北山的区域，自古就有八百里秦川之称，其四季分明、土地平坦、水草肥美、灌溉便利，是西北地区发展农业的重要区域。关中平原有潼关、大散关、武关、萧关四大关隘，分布有郑国渠、白渠、漕渠、成国渠、龙首渠等古代著名水利工程。

关中地区历史文化沉淀厚重，从西周始，先后有秦、西汉、隋、唐等多个王朝建都于关中，历时千余年。其南部为华夏东西文脉的秦岭，是我国佛教、道教的发祥地，渭河是母亲河黄河的最大支流。

区内分布有秦始皇陵、明十三陵、西汉十一陵、唐十八陵等79座古代帝王陵墓，占到全国现存帝王陵的半数之多。3. 陕南地区——巴山楚水：陕南地区北靠秦岭、南倚巴山，汉江自西向东穿流而过，是黄河流域文化与长江流域文化的交融地。

由于自东向西横亘着秦岭山脉，阻挡了来自北方的寒冷气流，造就了陕南气候温暖湿润的地域特色。

陕南地区古为秦楚交兵之地、商鞅封邑之地、汉家发源地，经历了元末明初的几次重大移民，区内现存有青木川、蜀河古镇、旱船码头、船帮会馆等古迹，是两汉三国文化、秦楚交融文化的重要代表区。

— ENGLISH —

Shaanxi, anciently called Qinchuan, abbreviated as Shaan or Qin, also called Sanqin, is an important birthplace of the Chinese nation and Huaxia culture.

From the Lantian Man 1.15-1.1 million years ago, ancestors lived and worked on this land; Shaanxi is the most concentrated active area of prehistoric human civilization.

Historically, 14 regimes including Qin, Western Han, Sui, and Tang established capitals in Shaanxi, making it the province with the most dynasties and longest duration of capital establishment in Chinese history.

It witnessed the rise and fall of Chinese civilization, leaving extremely rich historical and cultural heritage, of which ancient architecture is one part.

Shaanxi ancient architecture has diverse types; among existing ancient buildings, there are over 200 National Key Cultural Heritage Sites and over 400 provincial cultural heritage sites, totaling 354 sites; dating from Han to Ming-Qing; categories include city walls, granaries, towns, terraces, gardens, dams, roads and bridges, ancient pagodas, towers, archways, temples, palaces, ancestral halls, residences, guild halls, academies, and examination halls.

I. Natural Environment: Shaanxi is located in the inland hinterland of China, in the middle reaches of the Yellow River, between 105°29'-111°15'E and 31°42'-39°35'N.

The province is about 870 km long north-south and 200-500 km wide east-west, with a total area of 205,800 square kilometers, accounting for 2.1% of the national total.

Shaanxi borders 7 provinces and autonomous regions: Shanxi, Henan, Hubei, Sichuan, Gansu, Ningxia, and Inner Mongolia, serving as a gateway between northwest, southwest, north, and central China.

The terrain is high in the north and south, low in the middle, high in the northwest and low in the southeast.

The Beishan and Qinling Mountains divide Shaanxi into three major natural regions: the northern Shaanxi Plateau in the north, the Qinba Mountain Area in the south, and the Guanzhong Plain in the center. II.

Cultural Environment: After Qin and Han, Shaanxi became a typical province spanning north and south.

Due to natural geographical conditions, cultural functions, and evolution, it gradually formed three cultural regions: the northern Shaanxi Loess Plateau Cultural Region, the Guanzhong Plain Cultural Region, and the southern Shaanxi Qinba Mountain Cultural Region.

1. northern Shaanxi - Loess Style: Located in the hilly and gully area of the Loess Plateau, with the Mu Us Desert to the north, the Yellow River to the east, and the Ziwuling Mountains to the south.

People traditionally use cave-dwelling semi-subterranean residences and a semi-agricultural semi-pastoral production mode, with a strong and martial folk custom. 2.

Guanzhong - Wei River Qinchuan: The Guanzhong area refers to the Wei River basin from Baoji in the west to Tongguan in the east, from Qinling in the south to Beishan in the north, known as the '800-li Qinchuan' since ancient times, with distinct four seasons, flat land, fertile soil, and convenient irrigation.

It was the capital region for multiple dynasties including Zhou, Qin, Han, Sui, and Tang. 3.

southern Shaanxi - Bashan Chu River: Bordered by Qinling to the north and Bashan to the south, with the Han River flowing through from west to east, it is a cultural fusion area of the Yellow River and Yangtze River basins, with a warm and humid climate.

  • 人文环境
  • 陕北黄土高原
  • 关中平原
  • 陕南秦巴山地
  • 蓝田人

  • 丝绸之路
  • 黄土窑洞
  • 八百里秦川

02. 陕西古建筑的历史沿革

Historical Evolution of Ancient Architecture in Shaanxi
概述

一、原始社会时期：史前时期的陕西建筑代表着中国黄河流域仰韶文化和龙山文化的建筑文明。考古发掘陕西境内的旧石器时代遗址表明，大约在115万~110万年前，蓝田人的足迹已遍及关中和汉水流域，以巢居和穴居为基本居住形式。

新石器时代的仰韶文化时期，陕西已经成为仰韶文化发展的中心地区，现存遗址两千余处，约占全国总量的40%。半坡遗址和姜寨遗址形成以竖向半穴居以及地面建筑为主的聚落，完成了由穴居野处到筑室居的历史过渡，形成多种类型建筑的功能分区。地面房屋建筑已采用木架结构，室内地面、墙面已用细泥抹面，烧烤使之陶化，以防潮湿的技术做法。

半坡较晚的柱基，回填土中掺石灰质材料，对于加固和防潮略有改善；姜寨遗址当中的回填土还有掺加骨料的。龙山文化时期的陕西房屋建造技术进入了一个新的阶段，先民已经普遍在室内地面、墙面涂抹光洁坚硬的白灰面层，以达到防潮、清洁和明亮的效果。例如，长安客省庄遗址中出现了双室相联、平面呈吕字形的套间式半穴居。

龙山文化中晚期聚落遗址以榆林神木县高家堡镇石峁村为代表，是目前所见中国史前时期最大的城址，古城依山而建，形状大致呈东北——西南向的椭圆形，由皇城台、内城、外城三座基本完整，并相对独立的石构城址组成，拥有体量巨大、结构复杂及构筑技术先进的门道、石城墙、墩台、门塾、内外瓮城等重要遗迹。

二、夏商周时期：夏、商、周是我国奴隶社会时期，大量集中的奴隶劳动力和青铜工具的使用，使得这一时期建筑的规模和技术有了巨大发展，出现了原始社会之中不曾有的宫殿、苑囿、陵寝、官署、监狱等许多为统治阶级服务的建筑类型。

公元前11世纪的西周达到了中国奴隶社会建筑发展的鼎盛时期，其宫室、宗庙的建造，前堂后室制度和院落式布局，夯土、木构架和瓦的出现，标志着中国奴隶社会建筑技术的重要进步。

1976年在今岐山凤雏村、扶风召陈村发现了西周早期和中期的大型建筑群基址，尤其是周原凤雏村严整的四合院式庭院，揭示了中国四合院式建筑古老的历史渊源。

该庭院由二进院落组成，中轴线上依次为影壁、大门、前堂、后室，前堂与后室以廊连接，门、堂、室的两侧为通长的厢房，将庭院围成封闭空间，院落四周有檐廊环绕。房屋基址下设有排水陶管和卵石叠筑的暗沟，以排除院内雨水。屋顶已采用了板瓦，墙有夯土墙、土坯墙和木骨泥墙，均采用了三合土抹面，表面平整光洁。

柱础用块石、河卵石分层铺筑和夯土筑成。板瓦的发明，使中国建筑脱离了茅茨土阶的简陋状态。西周时期出现了瓦的雏形，凤雏村遗址出土的西周早期瓦，其制作为泥条盘筑法，仅有板瓦，其凸面或凹面做有瓦环或瓦钉，可知板瓦已作仰覆两用。召陈村遗址出土的西周中期瓦，已有板瓦和筒瓦，瓦瓦型号增多，瓦钉加长，蘑菇状瓦钉改进为环瓦，烧造火候也有提高；

筒瓦不仅型号多，还出现瓦舌、斜口、瓦钉，并有了扣脊瓦，说明西周中期已是瓦的发展时期。西周晚期瓦及瓦形变薄、变小，趋向规格化，烧造火候更高。秦国都城雍城（今宝鸡市凤翔县）的建筑，是春秋时期陕西古代建筑发展的一个重要里程碑。

作为秦国都城，雍城地处南控巴蜀通道、是东西交通枢纽的重要位置，历经19位国君294年，先后建造了4处大型宫廷建筑，城市规模超过当时的东周洛阳王城。三、秦时期：公元前221年，秦始皇嬴政定都咸阳，建立了中国第一个统一的封建王朝。

秦都咸阳的规划设计具有鲜明独创性，它摒弃了传统的城郭制度，在渭水南北的广阔地区建造了大量高台式宫廷建筑。在建筑群布局上，师法天象，使之与太空的星座相对应。离宫别馆，弥山跨谷，辇道相属，木衣绨绣，土被朱紫，宫人不移，乐不改悬，穷年忘归，犹不能遍，充分表现了咸阳城建筑的宏伟。秦统一六国后，最重要的工程为长城、阿房宫和秦始皇陵。

阿房宫遗址在今西安市三桥镇南，夯土台绵延起伏；

秦始皇陵，今尚存封土，呈方锥体，由夯土筑成，东西485米，南北515米，高约76米，是中国古代最大的坟冢。

秦雍城、栎阳、咸阳遗址，阿房宫及始皇陵遗址出土和采集到大量秦代瓦当，其纹样题材十分广泛。战国初期以鸟兽纹为主，战国中、晚期出现了鱼、虫等纹样和植物纹样，还有动植物结合的纹样，以云纹为主题的纹样也随之出现。

秦统一六国后，瓦当图案除继承战国晚期的题材外，以文字作为瓦当纹饰也开始出现。秦瓦当图案摒弃了商周礼器上刻板僵化的程式和神秘恐怖的形象，表现出突出的写实风格和手法，构图严整而明快简练、朴素大方，这是中国建筑史上瓦当图案题材最广泛、变化最丰富的一个时期。

秦代的建筑成就辉煌，尤其在对大型建筑的营建上，如建造阿房宫、长城以及骊山陵，如此浩大的工程，必然在人员调度、运输安排、施工组织，以及材料与构件的预制、加工与装配，甚至在建筑各部尺度的模数方面，都具有目前尚未为世人所知的种种手段。因此，秦代在建筑营建方面对后世也产生了巨大影响，尤以两汉为重。

四、两汉时期：西汉初年，因秦咸阳已被项羽焚烧一空，汉王朝暂以栎阳（今西安市阎良区武屯镇）为都，同时在咸阳城渭河南岸的长安乡建立新都，即汉长安城。汉长安城是中国历史上第一个大规模的城市，当时世界上只有欧洲的罗马城可以与之媲美。

城垣内总面积36平方公里，有8条大街，160个闾里，东市、西市等9个市场。城内中、南部为宫殿、官署和高官显贵居住区，约占全城面积的2/3以上；东部和西部为商业区。

据《汉书·地理志》记载，公元2年，长安城内人口为户八万八百，人口二十四万六千二百。汉承秦制，施行郡县制度，但在宫室的布置中也有汉承秦制的记载。如宫殿的前殿制、帝陵平面的十字轴以及覆斗形方上，即为两汉所完全承袭。西汉建都长安，经历十一朝皇帝。

汉代盛行厚葬，汉天子即位一年而为陵，工程浩大，陵冢至今仍屹立于咸阳塬上。西汉帝陵的形制属于累土象山，即在地面上夯筑高大的坟丘（封土），呈覆斗形，而方锥体和覆斗形在形式上可以说是一种最为简单、稳定、明确的造型。为了保护陵墓，功臣贵戚及富豪需迁居陵区，按陵设置县邑。

如营建茂陵时，改茂乡为茂陵县（今茂陵东窦马村），迁居各地富豪27万多人。两汉时不但宫殿的建设呈现了多功能的趋向，同时也出现了私家园林，著名的有茂陵邑袁广汉园。

两汉时期的大木结构基本定形，并且出现高层木梁柱建筑，从汉代冥器之中的陶楼可见一斑。至于木构之中各具体构件，其发展演变对后世影响最大者，莫过于斗栱。虽然周、秦已经开始使用斗栱，但是仅在铜器中得知少数形象，而从汉代所遗墓阙、画像砖石以及建筑冥器之中都可见其全貌。

在其他建筑材料与结构方面，西汉建筑遗址上遗留的汉砖，质密、体大、量重，有长形、方形砖与空心砖多种类型，尤其在大型空心砖的使用上，秦代时仅见于殿堂之踏跺，到汉代时才用于墓葬。并且在砖的正面多有装饰图案；汉瓦当地优良，一般为圆形，面上装饰有阳纹的图案或文字，图案十分丰富。

五、三国、两晋南北朝时期：汉末至隋近600年间，由于都城东迁和连年战争，陕西的建设基本处于停滞状态，原有城市、建筑遭到重大破坏。以长安城为例，西汉长安经王莽末年战乱破坏已成废墟，东汉只能在洛阳建都。

东汉末年，董卓于公元190年焚烧洛阳宫室，迁都长安，临时以京兆府舍为宫，后稍稍修葺宫殿居住。公元195年，又经战乱，新修葺的宫室又破坏殆尽。至迟在西晋初，在荒废的城市内部建了小城。

可知在三国、两晋阶段，当年盛极一时的汉长安城已经基本不复存在了。南北朝时期，大夏国国王赫连勃勃驱使10多万人日夜施工，在今榆林市靖边县无定河北岸的塬上修建都城——统万城。该城有内外两城，内城夯土极为结实，城墙非常坚固，至今完整无损。

其城垣、隅墩、马面和建筑台基均由苍白色土版筑造成，经化验鉴定，主要成分为石英、黏土和碳酸钙，即为三合土。这是已知在古代建筑工程中使用三合土的最早实证。

现存统万城城高10米，四角有楼，高30米，东城周长2566米，西城周长2470米。

城内钟楼、鼓楼夯土建筑遗址今仍历历在目，显示了当时陕北劳动人民的聪明才智。

值得一提的还有后秦时期，在今西安市户县东南20公里处的圭峰山北麓建有草堂寺，印度僧人鸠摩罗什于此校译梵文经典。唐代以后，寺内建筑多被兵燹烧毁。寺内现存的建筑，多为民国时期和新中国成立后重建。

六、隋唐五代时期：隋朝立国初期，仍以北周都城原汉长安城为都。但历经700余年战乱，汉长安城的宫室街市以及城垣已残缺不全，甚至连井水也已变得苦咸不堪。

隋文帝杨坚在汉长安城东南10公里的龙首原南侧兴建新都大兴城。

大兴城于公元582年6月动工，经过关中及关东百余万民工9个月的日夜施工，次年3月基本完成宫城、皇城和两市、坊里及主要街道、引水工程的建设。其建设速度之快，在中国古代建设史中前所未有。

隋炀帝大业九年（公元613年）修建长安外郭城。大兴城平面布局十分规整，外郭城近方形，东西长9721米，南北宽8651米，周长36.7千米，总面积84平方千米。

外郭城东、西、南三边共设9座城门。唐灭隋后，改大兴城为长安城，唐长安城沿用隋大兴城旧制，不断修建，城市规模空前宏大。

据今实测，唐长安城平面形似正方形，周长约35.5公里，面积约84平方公里，是汉长安城（36平方公里）的2.2倍多，是同期隋、唐东都洛阳城（45平方公里）的1.8倍。

唐都长安规划布局科学先进，全城建筑布局分别由宫城、皇城和外郭三大部分组成。其中宫城为皇帝及其子女嫔妃居住的地方，皇城是中央衙署机关办公的地方，外郭为坊里居民住宅及商业贸易、寺院区。整个城市布局以北部宫城、皇城为中心，向东西南三面展开，全城以南北中央朱雀大街为中轴线，采用东西对称布局，街坊排列极为整齐。

整个唐长安城中，宫城约占全城总面积的3.7%，皇城约占6.3%，居民区110坊约占63.8%，其余为道路、河渠等设施所占。唐长安宫室规模宏大，建筑雄伟壮丽。宫城位于全城正中的北部，唐初只有太极宫一处（原隋大兴宫）。贞观八年（公元634年），唐太宗李世民于龙首原上修永安宫，次年改名大明宫。

龙朔二年（公元662年），唐高宗李治对其加以扩建。开元二年（公元714年），唐玄宗李隆基改旧居为兴庆宫，并几度扩建。彼时太极宫、大明宫和兴庆宫三大宫殿区，殿楼亭阁，锦绣叠翠。

唐朝是我国风景园林形成的重要时期，著名的王维辋川别业位于蓝田县西南10公里辋谷口内。唐朝是我国佛教建筑快速发展的新时期。

唐贞观二十二年（公元648年），太子李治于原唐长安城进昌坊内今西安城南4公里处，隋无漏寺的旧址上建造慈恩寺，寺建成不久，名僧玄奘从弘福寺搬至寺东院译场译经，并创立中国佛教一大宗派唯识宗。

据《大慈恩寺三藏法师传》载：当时寺内重楼复殿，云阁禅房，共有10多个院落，总计1897间。寺内建有闻名遐迩的大雁塔，其造型雄伟稳重，风格简洁古朴，为唐代楼阁式塔的典型。

彼时修建的寺庙还有荐福寺、香积寺、善导寺、兴教寺、昭仁寺、智果寺、罔极寺扶风法门寺塔及地宫等。纵观隋唐时期，在陕西境内进行了空前规模的建设，表现出统一、强盛国家的伟大气魄。

大明宫遗址表明在规划时以方一百步（50丈）的方格为控制网，主体建筑位于几何中心；现存唐代遗构都以材分为模数，以柱高为立面、断面上的扩大模数，这些证明在唐代大至城市，小到单体建筑，都有一套完整的采用模数制的规划与建筑设计方法。七、宋元时期：唐王朝灭亡后，陕西失去了全国政治、经济与文化中心的地位。

特别是五代至两宋的近400年间，由于关中和陕北地区是宋夏、宋金、金夏、金蒙争战的主战场，社会生产力和建设事业都遭到了极大的破坏。陕北与关中地区成为了边缘文化地带。

而地处陕南的汉水流域，由于免遭战乱，社会比较安定，生产力有了较大发展，建设事业也发展较快。如兴元府（今汉中市）、金州（今安康市）、洋州（今洋县）、兴州（今略阳县）、商州（今商州区）等陕南城镇，工商发达，水陆交通便利，成为当时富甲一方的经济文化中心。宋元时期陕西修建的宗教建筑遗存较多。

如北宋大观元年（1107年）于永寿县永平乡武陵山建武陵寺及寺内永寿塔，于蒲城建崇寿寺的诸佛舍利宝塔、于韩城市昝村镇吴村建普照寺，于横山县塔湾乡芦河东岸建鸿门寺。彼时道教建筑遗存亦较多，如西安八仙庵、户县重阳宫、佳县白云观等。

其中，佳县白云观始建于宋、元，后扩建于明。共计有33座庙宇，总建筑面积8.1万平方米，占地80多亩，为陕西现存的最大的一组古建筑群。明万历四十六年（1618年）万历皇帝朱翊钧亲颁白云观圣旨一道，并赐御制《道藏》4726卷，从而使其成为中国西北最大的道教圣地。

北宋嘉祐七年（1062年）苏轼任凤翔府签书判官时，借古饮凤池，扩广疏浚成湖，曰东湖，建有凌虚台、喜雨亭、宛在亭、会景亭等园林建筑，湖总面积已达7公顷左右。其规划布局和建筑反映了西北固原地区古朴的地方风格。元朝统一后，又将长安城改设为安西路城，其城垣和建筑物逐渐得到重修。

至元十年（1273年）在古城东北3公里处浐河西岸修建了东西长603米，南城宽542米，北城宽534米，面积达3平方公里的安西王府。

至元二十二年（1275年），意大利旅行家马可·波罗曾参观这座宫殿，他在游记中写道：宫甚壮丽，在一大平原中，周围川湖泉水不少，高大墙垣环之，周围约五里。墙内即王宫所在，其壮丽之甚，布置之佳，罕有与比。惜此宫在元末战争中又被夷为平地。

陕西关中、陕南地区现存10多座元代建筑，一类继承唐宋以来传统的横梁结构，如韩城普照寺大殿、九郎庙大殿；一类采用大额式构架，前后檐柱不对位，间数不等，造型古拙硕壮，如韩城禹王殿、三圣庙献殿、洋县智果寺，具有古代早期纵架、减柱造结构的历史特征。

八、明清时期：明代改安西路城为西安府城。明洪武三年（1370年），开始扩建西安城垣，历时八年，奠定了现西安古城基本格局。经后世屡次重修、扩建，遂成中国现存规模最大、保存最为完整的古城墙。

明代于西安城内修建了钟楼和鼓楼，两楼高大雄伟，庄严华丽，相互辉映。明代军事重镇和城堡的设置，与明代兵制密不可分。明代兵制是洪武初年建立的，曰都司、卫所。

其任务是对外防止侵略，巩固边防，对内镇压人民的反抗，维护统治阶级的政权。陕北的榆林镇逐渐成为中原及关中北部的边防重镇。明成化至嘉靖间，蒙古南侵加剧，于是明政府加筑鄂尔多斯南边的一段长城，榆林的军事重要性就尤显突出。

因此，在成化七年（1471年），延绥巡抚都御史余子俊大筑边城。榆林城初建时为土城，嘉靖、隆庆、万历时，渐用瓷瓷，形成顶宽9.6米、底宽16米、高11.5米、包砌厚1米余的坚固城墙。

陕西明代建筑的结构和造型多数采用规矩的传统横梁结构，但也有少数仍沿袭元代大额式纵架结构的做法，如韩城文庙大成殿；还有采用大内额和斜袱、减柱造的做法，如华县禅修寺等。明代即已注意道教建筑药王庙，重修元代寺庙。良马寺。

万历年间于泾阳东南建铁佛寺，后被毁，仅留五间门楼。寺内有崇文宝塔一座，为八角形13层仿木楼阁式砖塔，塔高79.19米，塔门向正南，宽1.3米，上下两层有塔檐。

万历年间于三原县安乐乡中王堡内建寺，寺内有木塔，故名木塔寺，为陕西现存的唯一木塔。李自成于大顺年间建米脂真武宫，是依托嘉靖年间建的真武庙扩建而成的行宫。其布局紧凑、配置得体，为陕西现存一组较完整的古建筑群。清康熙年间在榆林城内建有万佛楼，后焚毁又重建。

该楼为一座建于过街楼台上的佛寺，台用砖砌成，长29.6米，宽18.4米，有十字券洞与街道相通。券洞高、宽各约5米，台内有阶梯可上台面。万佛楼主体建筑为观音殿，面阔三间，高两层，位

— ENGLISH —

I. Primitive Society: Prehistoric Shaanxi architecture represents the architectural civilization of the Yangshao and Longshan cultures in the Yellow River basin.

Archaeological excavations show that about 1.15-1.1 million years ago, Lantian Man's footprints spread throughout Guanzhong and the Han River basin, with nest dwelling and cave dwelling as basic residential forms.

During the Neolithic Yangshao culture period, Shaanxi became the center of Yangshao culture development, with over 2,000 existing sites, accounting for about 40% of the national total.

The Banpo and Jiangzhai sites formed settlements dominated by vertical semi-subterranean and ground-level buildings, completing the historical transition from cave dwelling to built housing. II.

Xia, Shang, Zhou Periods: The Western Zhou reached the peak of Chinese slave society architecture. The palace and temple construction, the front-hall rear-bedroom system and courtyard layout, rammed earth, timber frame, and the appearance of tiles marked important progress.

In 1976, large building complex foundations from the early and middle Western Zhou were discovered at Fengchu Village in Qishan and Zhaochen Village in Fufeng, especially the rigorous siheyuan (courtyard) at Fengchu, revealing the ancient origins of Chinese courtyard architecture.

The Qin capital Yongcheng (present-day Fengxiang County, Baoji) was an important milestone in Spring and Autumn period architecture. III. Qin Period: In 221 BC, Qin Shi Huang established the capital at Xianyang, creating China's first unified feudal dynasty.

Qin Xianyang's planning was highly original, abandoning the traditional city-wall system and building numerous high-platform palace buildings across the Wei River.

The most important projects after unification were the Great Wall, Epang Palace, and Qin Shi Huang's Mausoleum. IV.

Han Period: In the early Western Han, since Qin Xianyang was burned by Xiang Yu, the Han dynasty temporarily made Yueyang the capital and then built the new capital Chang'an City on the south bank of the Wei River.

Han Chang'an was the first large-scale city in Chinese history, with a total area of 36 square kilometers, 8 main streets, 160 li (neighborhoods), and 9 markets.

The Han dynasty practiced elaborate burials, with imperial Mausoleums still standing on the Xianyang Plateau. V.

Three Kingdoms, Jin, Northern and Southern Dynasties: During nearly 600 years from late Han to Sui, due to capital relocation and continuous wars, Shaanxi's construction was basically stagnant.

The Northern Xia king Helian Bobo built the capital Tongwan City on the plateau north of the Wuding River in present-day Jingbian County, Yulin, with extremely solid rammed earth walls that remain intact today.

This is the earliest known evidence of using sanhetu (three-part compound earth) in ancient Chinese architecture. VI. Sui, Tang, Five Dynasties: Emperor Wen of Sui built the new capital Daxing City southeast of Han Chang'an, with construction starting in 582 AD and basically completed in 9 months.

After Tang conquered Sui, Daxing was renamed Chang'an, with continuous expansion reaching an unprecedented scale. Tang Chang'an had an area of about 84 square kilometers, 2.2 times that of Han Chang'an.

The city was scientifically planned with the palace city, imperial city, and outer city, centered on the north-south central Zhuque Avenue with east-west symmetry. The Tang dynasty was an important period for Chinese landscape gardens and Buddhist architecture.

The Giant Wild Goose Pagoda in Ci'en Temple is a typical Tang pavilion-style pagoda. VII. Song and Yuan Periods: After the Tang dynasty fell, Shaanxi lost its position as the national political, economic, and cultural center.

The Han River basin in southern Shaanxi, spared from war, developed relatively rapidly. Many religious buildings from the Song and Yuan periods survive, including the Baiyun Temple in Jiaxian, the largest existing ancient building complex in Shaanxi.

The Yuan dynasty rebuilt Chang'an as Anxi Road City and built the Anxi Royal Palace. VIII. Ming and Qing Periods: The Ming dynasty renamed Anxi Road City as Xi'an Prefecture City.

In 1370, expansion of the Xi'an city wall began, taking 8 years and establishing the basic layout of the present Xi'an ancient city, which became the largest and best-preserved ancient city wall in China.

The Ming dynasty built the bell tower and drum tower in Xi'an. The Ming dynasty also established military towns and fortresses, with Yulin becoming an important frontier defense town.

Most Ming architecture in Shaanxi adopted traditional beam structures, with a few continuing the Yuan dynasty's large-eave longitudinal frame style.

关键特征 / Key Features

第二章 城镇聚落

Chapter 2 Urban Settlements

03. 石峁遗址

Shimao Site
新石器时代龙山晚期至夏代早期 古代都邑 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市神木县高家堡镇洞川沟附近的山梁上

石峁遗址位于榆林市神木县高家堡镇洞川沟附近的山梁上，属新石器时代龙山晚期至夏代早期超大型聚落遗存，是目前所见中国史前时期面积最大的城址。2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位。

石峁遗址距今约4000年，面积约425万平方米。其大型石城使用寿命超过300年。遗址地处黄河支流秃尾河及其支流洞川沟交汇处，所在区域属于低山丘陵区，以黄土梁峁、剥蚀山丘、沙漠滩地为主。

地貌沟壑纵横，支离破碎。城址分为皇城台、内城、外城三个部分，形成以皇城台为中心、内外城包围环绕的环套结构，开启了中国古代都城建筑格局的先河。皇城台位于内城偏西，大致呈方形，四面包砌护坡石墙。内城将皇城台包围其中，依山势而建，平面大致呈东北——西南向的椭圆形，内墙残长2公里，面积约210万平方米。

外城利用了内城东南部墙体，向东南方向继续扩筑了一道弧形石墙，面积约190余万平方米。墙体的加工方法主要有嵌山砌石、夯基砌石以及利用断崖等。同时还发现了角楼、疑似马面、城门等附属建筑遗址。

外城东门址位于外城东北部，处在遗址区域内最高点，门道东北向，由U形石砌照壁、包石夯土墩台、曲尺形瓮城、门塾等部分组成，这些设施以宽约9米的曲尺形门道连接。在照壁的倒塌堆中，发现了玉铲、玉璜以及阴刻人头像的大石块。

在龙山晚期修筑外城东门时有一层黑褐色地基铺垫层，在曲尺形瓮城的一段墙体的地面上，发现了100余块壁画残块，部分壁画还附着在墙体上，这些壁画以白灰面为底，绘有红、黄、黑、绿等色彩，均为几何形图案。

2015年9月，考古人员在石峁外城东门附近清理出一处规模较大、保存较好、错落有致的院落，其窑洞式住房、高处库房、礼仪性厅房及石铺地坪和院落门址等结构基本清晰。

— ENGLISH —

The Shimao Site is located on a mountain ridge near Dongchuangou, Gaojiabao Town, Shenmu County, Yulin City. It is an extra-large settlement remnant from the late Neolithic Longshan period to the early Xia Dynasty, and is currently the largest prehistoric city site found in China.

It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006. The Shimao Site dates back about 4,000 years, with an area of about 4.25 million square meters. Its large stone city had a service life of over 300 years.

The site is located at the confluence of the Tuwei River (a tributary of the Yellow River) and its tributary Dongchuangou, in a low hilly area dominated by loess ridges, eroded hills, and desert flats. The landform is crisscrossed with gullies and fragmented.

The city site is divided into three parts: Huangchengtai (Imperial City Platform), Inner City, and Outer City, forming a nested structure centered on Huangchengtai and surrounded by inner and outer cities, pioneering the architectural layout of ancient Chinese capitals.

Huangchengtai is located slightly west of the inner city, roughly square, with slope-protecting stone walls on all four sides.

The inner city encloses Huangchengtai, built according to the mountain terrain, with a plane roughly oval in the northeast-southwest direction; the inner wall has a remaining length of 2 km and an area of about 2.1 million square meters.

The outer city uses the southeastern wall of the inner city and continues to build an arc-shaped stone wall southeastward, with an area of over 1.9 million square meters. The wall processing methods mainly include stone masonry on hillsides, stone masonry on rammed foundations, and utilizing cliffs.

Attached building remains such as corner towers, suspected horse-face defenses, and city gates have also been found.

The outer city east gate is located in the northeastern part of the outer city, at the highest point within the site area, with the gate facing northeast; it consists of a U-shaped stone screen wall, stone-clad rammed earth gate towers, a zigzag barbican, and gate guard rooms, connected by a zigzag passage about 9 meters wide.

In the collapsed debris of the screen wall, jade shovels, jade huang (semicircular jade), and large stone blocks with incised human head portraits were found.

When building the outer city east gate in the late Longshan period, there was a layer of dark brown foundation bedding; on the ground of a section of the zigzag barbican wall, over 100 mural fragments were found, some murals still attached to the wall; these murals use white plaster as the base, painted with red, yellow, black, green and other colors, all in geometric patterns.

In September 2015, archaeologists cleared a well-preserved, well-arranged courtyard near the outer city east gate, with cave-style dwellings, elevated storerooms, ceremonial halls, stone-paved floors, and courtyard gate structures basically clear.

  • 距今约4000年
  • 面积约425万平方米
  • 石城使用寿命超过300年
  • 皇城台
  • 内城
  • 外城
  • 外城东门址
  • U形石砌照壁
  • 曲尺形瓮城
  • 壁画残块

  • 玉铲

04. 丰镐遗址

Feng-Hao Site
西周 古代都邑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市长安区沣河两岸

西周都城丰镐是中国历史上第一个规模宏大、布局整齐的古代都邑。它开创了中国城市平面布局方整、宽敞、宏伟之先河，成为后来城市规划的模板。1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位。周人在岐山、周原建都后，由于政治、经济、军事需要，遂迁至沣河流域，在沣河以西建立丰京。

后因扩都受到地理条件限制，又在沣河以东建立镐京，但丰京仍然保留。丰京位置在今西安市长安区沣河西岸的马王村、客省庄一带，镐京在沣河以东的落水村、上泉北村、普渡村、花园村、斗门镇一带。两京相距，近在咫尺（仅五华里之遥），仅以沣水相隔，名虽不同，实为一京的两部分。丰京和镐京并称丰镐，是历史上最早称为京的城市。

丰镐作为西周都城沿用近300年，又被称为宗周。西周末年，丰镐为犬戎族所攻入，周幽王被杀，都城遭战火焚毁。秦始皇修阿房宫与汉武帝修昆明池时，镐京遗址遭到毁灭性破坏，整个城址规模、布局已难考据。

经近现代考古发掘与研究，在沣水两岸约15平方公里的遗址范围内，发现了多座宫殿、宗庙、贵族与平民居址、车马坑、青铜器窖藏、大型墓葬、手工业作坊。此外，据《诗经》等文献记载，当时丰镐尚有辟雍、灵台、灵沼等礼仪和游乐性设施，但今已无任何建筑遗存。丰镐是沿河谷阶地发展起来的城市，城市设计因地制宜、朴素自然，强调实用功能。

广义的丰镐地区西起涝河，东至橘水，北达渭水，南及秦岭北麓，又可划分为都邑区、王陵区、农业种植区、游乐渔猎区和放牧区等不同功能区域。例如，灵沼、海子、彪池、镐池和丰水，既是两京区域内的渔猎区，也是天子、贵族习礼、习射和举行各种大礼、游乐活动的重要场所。都邑区内各居民点间有农作区，而都邑以外的细柳塬、高阳塬、毕塬、神禾塬和渭水、涝水、新河阶地可能被辟为农作区，供应都城用粮。

— ENGLISH —

The Western Zhou capital Feng-Hao was the first ancient capital in Chinese history with a large scale and neat layout. It pioneered the square, spacious, and grand urban plane layout in China, becoming a model for later urban planning.

It was designated as the first batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1961.

After the Zhou people established their capital at Qishan and Zhouyuan, due to political, economic, and military needs, they moved to the Feng River basin and established Fengjing west of the Feng River.

Later, due to geographical limitations for expansion, they established Haojing east of the Feng River, but Fengjing was still retained.

Fengjing was located around Mawang Village and Kesheng Village on the west bank of the Feng River in present-day Chang'an District, Xi'an; Haojing was around Luoshui Village, Shangquanbei Village, Pudu Village, Huayuan Village, and Doumen Town east of the Feng River.

The two capitals were close together (only about 2.5 km apart), separated only by the Feng River; although named differently, they were actually two parts of one capital. Fengjing and Haojing were collectively called Feng-Hao, the earliest city called 'Jing' (capital) in history.

Feng-Hao served as the Western Zhou capital for nearly 300 years and was also called Zongzhou. In the late Western Zhou, Feng-Hao was attacked by the Quanrong tribe, King You of Zhou was killed, and the capital was destroyed by war.

When Qin Shi Huang built Epang Palace and Emperor Wu of Han built Kunming Lake, the Haojing site suffered devastating destruction, making the scale and layout of the entire city site difficult to verify.

Through modern archaeological excavations and research, within the 15-square-kilometer site area on both banks of the Feng River, multiple palaces, ancestral temples, aristocratic and civilian residences, chariot pits, bronze hoards, large tombs, and handicraft workshops have been found.

In addition, according to documents such as the 'Book of Songs,' Feng-Hao had ceremonial and recreational facilities such as Piyong (academy), Lingtai (spirit platform), and Lingzhao (spirit pond), but no architectural remains exist today.

Feng-Hao was a city developed along river valley terraces, with urban design adapted to local conditions, simple and natural, emphasizing practical functions.

The broad Feng-Hao area extends west to the Lao River, east to the Jue River, north to the Wei River, and south to the northern foot of the Qinling Mountains; it can be divided into different functional areas such as capital area, royal Mausoleum area, agricultural planting area, recreational fishing and hunting area, and grazing area.

For example, Lingzhao, Haizi, Biao Pond, Hao Pond, and Feng River were both fishing and hunting areas within the two capital regions and important places for the Son of Heaven and nobles to practice rituals, archery, and hold various ceremonies and recreational activities.

There were agricultural areas between residential points within the capital area, while the terraces of Xiliu, Gaoyang, Bi, Shenhe and the terraces of Wei, Lao, and Xin rivers outside the capital may have been opened as agricultural areas to supply grain for the capital.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 中国历史上第一个规模宏大、布局整齐的古代都邑
  • 开创了中国城市平面布局方整、宽敞、宏伟之先河
  • 丰京
  • 镐京
  • 丰镐并称
  • 历史上最早称为京的城市
  • 沿沣河阶地发展
  • 因地制宜
  • 朴素自然
  • 强调实用功能
  • 都邑区
  • 王陵区
  • 农业种植区
  • 游乐渔猎区
  • 放牧区

05. 秦都咸阳城

Qin Capital Xianyang City
战国后期至秦 古代都邑 全国重点文物保护单位 陕西省咸阳市以东约15公里处的窑店镇牛羊村一带

秦咸阳遗址是战国后期秦国都城遗址。其中心地处陕西省咸阳市以东约15公里处的窑店镇牛羊村一带。遗址北至高干渠，南到渭河北岸，东到柏家嘴，西到任家嘴，东西长6公里，南北宽3公里，面积18平方公里。

1988年公布为第三批全国重点文物保护单位。秦孝公十二年（公元前350年），秦都城自栎阳迁至咸阳，面积约100平方公里。

惠文王时（公元前337~公元前310年），都城规模随着宫室增多而扩大，面积约200平方公里。

昭襄王时（公元前306~公元前250年），先后在渭河南修建了兴乐宫、章台宫，并在渭河上架横桥，以联系咸阳宫与兴乐宫，整个城域面积达460平方公里。秦始皇统一中国后，在渭北因北陵营殿，扩建先王宫室，营建风格各异的六国宫殿。

在渭河以南，新建信宫，扩建横桥，宫与宫之间，殿屋复道周阁相属，增加了都城境内的建筑密度。秦始皇三十五年（公元前212年），营造成阳最大宫殿——朝宫，其前殿阿房宫面积约87万平方米，现遗存总面积约65万平方米。

在此基础上，将咸阳周围100公里内的270个宫观，用复道和甬道互相沟通，形成了以咸阳宫为中心，横亘东西400公里、南北200公里的大都会区。

秦咸阳的城市营建不仅受到王畿区域规划体制的影响，而且还受到天人合一理念的重要影响，体现了中国封建社会早期城市规划之中法天象地的指导思想。秦都咸阳城将星宿天象与建筑实体对应起来进行都城营建，既形成了自身的显著特色，又对包括汉长安在内的后世都城规划产生了深刻影响。《三辅黄图》曰：始皇穷奢侈，筑咸阳宫，因北陵营殿，端门四达，以则紫宫，象帝居。

渭水贯都，以象天汉，横桥南渡，以法牵牛。《史记·天官书》则曰：中宫天极星，其一明者，太一常居也；旁三星三公，或曰子属。后句四星，末大星正妃，余三星后宫之属也。环之匡卫十二星，藩臣。皆曰紫宫。秦都咸阳城营建将渭河与天上的银河相对应，将拟建的宫城与天上的星空相对应。

咸阳宫象征天极（北极星），信宫对奎宿、娄宿，上林苑对银河天苑星，阿房宫则对营室宿，各宫殿以咸阳宫为中心，环列周围，形成拱卫之势，滔滔渭水穿流于宫殿群之间，就像是银河亘空，十分壮观，充分实现了为政以德，譬如北辰，居其所而众星拱之的规划意图。秦都咸阳规划布局，以渭河为纬向轴线，以咸阳宫为经向轴线，以两线交点横桥为中心向四周扩展。

其中，秦孝公至惠文王时期，大体以今渭城区窑店镇为界，东部为宫殿区，西南为居住区，西北为陵墓区。始皇时期，都城北部修建郑国渠，形成农业灌溉区。随着国力渐强，秦都经济日益繁荣，市域范围不断扩大，市场数量不断增加，周边地区分布繁市、槐里市、社市、栎市等综合及专业市场。自秦昭王始，秦都咸阳城市功能即以发展经济为主，其市区不断扩大，城郭却未增筑。

秦始皇统一中国后，仍然没有扩大城郭，但是十分注重道路交通。修筑了以咸阳为中心，以驰道、直道为干线的交通网络。宫殿分布大体可分为中心区与外围区。中心区形成高大的建筑形象，同时利用夯土围合房间。夯土台基周围布以围廊，既是遮阴避雨之通道，又为夯土台基之防护。建筑有露台、阁、榭等室外或半室外过渡空间。

由此，证明当时的宫殿建筑群布局、建筑形制、结构与工程技术已有相当成就。宫殿建筑出土文物以砖瓦、瓦当等建筑材料为大宗，另有铁器、铜器、兵器、货币和陶器等。砖有两种规格，一种是用于踏步的大型长方形空心砖，砖面多数饰以多种内容的龙凤纹及回纹；另一种是用于铺地或镶砌廊边的小型长方形和方形的扁砖，砖面多模印菱形方格纹、菱形纹、太阳纹和回纹。瓦分板瓦和筒瓦，体形较大。

瓦背饰绳纹。瓦当大多为卷云纹圆瓦当，也出土少量的半圆形和圆形素瓦当。在一些板瓦、筒瓦和少数砖面上，戳记文字印鉴，款式有正方形、圆形、倒梯形。

— ENGLISH —

The Qin Xianyang Site is the capital site of the State of Qin in the late Warring States period. Its center is located around Yangyang Village, Yaodian Town, about 15 km east of Xianyang City, Shaanxi Province.

The site extends north to the Gaogan Canal, south to the north bank of the Wei River, east to Baijiazui, west to Renjiazui, 6 km east-west and 3 km north-south, with an area of 18 square kilometers. It was designated as the third batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1988.

In the 12th year of Duke Xiao of Qin (350 BC), the Qin capital moved from Yueyang to Xianyang, with an area of about 100 square kilometers. During King Huiwen (337-310 BC), the capital scale expanded with the increase of palaces, with an area of about 200 square kilometers.

During King Zhaoxiang (306-250 BC), Xingle Palace and Zhangtai Palace were built south of the Wei River, and a horizontal bridge was built over the Wei River to connect Xianyang Palace and Xingle Palace, with the entire city area reaching 460 square kilometers.

After Qin Shi Huang unified China, he built palaces on the northern Mausoleum area north of the Wei River, expanded former royal palaces, and built Six-State palaces with different styles.

South of the Wei River, he built Xin Palace and expanded the horizontal bridge; between palaces, covered walkways and surrounding pavilions connected each other, increasing the building density within the capital.

In the 35th year of Qin Shi Huang (212 BC), the largest palace in Xianyang was built - the Chaogong (Court Palace), whose front hall Epang Palace had an area of about 870,000 square meters, with existing remains totaling about 650,000 square meters.

On this basis, 270 palaces within 100 km around Xianyang were connected by covered walkways and walled passages, forming a metropolitan area centered on Xianyang Palace, spanning 400 km east-west and 200 km north-south.

The urban construction of Qin Xianyang was not only influenced by the royal domain planning system but also importantly influenced by the concept of unity of heaven and humanity, reflecting the guiding ideology of modeling the city on heaven and earth in early feudal Chinese urban planning.

The Qin capital Xianyang City corresponded constellations and celestial phenomena with building entities for capital construction, both forming its own distinctive features and profoundly influencing later capital planning including Han Chang'an.

The 'Sanfu Huangtu' says: Qin Shi Huang, pursuing extravagance, built Xianyang Palace, constructing halls on the northern Mausoleum, with gates reaching in all directions, modeling the Purple Palace (heavenly palace), representing the imperial residence.

The Wei River flows through the capital, representing the Milky Way; the horizontal bridge crosses southward, representing the Cowherd Star.

The 'Records of the Grand Historian: Heavenly Officials' says: The Central Palace Tianji Star, the brightest one, is where Taiyi (Great One) constantly resides; the three adjacent stars are the Three Dukes, or said to be sons; the four stars behind, the largest is the principal consort, the remaining three are the harem; the twelve surrounding stars are the vassal ministers.

All are called the Purple Palace. The Qin capital Xianyang City construction corresponded the Wei River with the Milky Way in the sky, and the planned palace city with the starry sky.

Xianyang Palace symbolized the Celestial Pole (North Star), Xin Palace corresponded to the Kui and Lou constellations, Shanglin Park corresponded to the Tianyuan star in the Milky Way, and Epang Palace corresponded to the Yingshi constellation; each palace was centered on Xianyang Palace, arranged around it, forming a guard formation; the surging Wei River flowed through the palace groups, like the Milky Way spanning the sky, very spectacular, fully realizing the planning intention of 'governing with virtue, like the North Star, staying in its place while all stars surround it.' The planning layout of Qin capital Xianyang took the Wei River as the latitudinal axis, Xianyang Palace as the longitudinal axis, and the horizontal bridge at the intersection of the two lines as the center expanding in all directions.

During Duke Xiao to King Huiwen, roughly bounded by Yaodian Town in present-day Weicheng District, the east was the palace area, the southwest was the residential area, and the northwest was the Mausoleum area.

During the First Emperor period, the Zhengguo Canal was built in the northern part of the capital, forming an agricultural irrigation area.

As national strength grew, the Qin capital economy became increasingly prosperous, the urban scope continued to expand, the number of markets increased, and surrounding areas had comprehensive and specialized markets such as Fan City, Huaili City, She City, and Yue City.

From King Zhaoxiang onward, the urban function of Qin capital Xianyang focused on economic development, with the urban area continuously expanding but the city walls not expanded.

After Qin Shi Huang unified China, he still did not expand the city walls but attached great importance to road transportation. He built a transportation network centered on Xianyang, with Chi Dao (imperial highways) and Zhi Dao (straight roads) as trunk lines.

Palace distribution can be roughly divided into central area and peripheral area. The central area formed tall building images, while using rammed earth to enclose rooms.

rammed earth platform bases were surrounded by corridors, serving both as passages for shade and rain protection and as protection for the rammed earth platforms. Buildings had outdoor or semi-outdoor transition spaces such as terraces, pavilions, and open pavilions.

This proves that the palace building complex layout, building forms, structures, and engineering technology at that time had considerable achievements.

Unearthed cultural relics from palace buildings are mainly building materials such as bricks, tiles, and tile-ends, plus ironware, bronze, weapons, currency, and pottery.

Bricks have two specifications: one is large rectangular hollow bricks for steps, with surfaces mostly decorated with various dragon-phoenix patterns and key-fret patterns; the other is small rectangular and square flat bricks for paving or edging corridors, with surfaces mostly molded with diamond grid patterns, diamond patterns, sun patterns, and key-fret patterns.

Tiles are divided into flat tiles and tube tiles, with large bodies. Tile backs are decorated with rope patterns. Tile-ends are mostly circular tile-ends with scrolling cloud patterns, with a small number of semicircular and circular plain tile-ends also unearthed.

On some flat tiles, tube tiles, and a few brick surfaces, stamped text seals are found, with styles including square, circular, and inverted trapezoid.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 战国后期秦国都城遗址
  • 中心处于窑店镇牛羊村
  • 遗址北至高干渠
  • 南到渭河北岸
  • 东到柏家嘴
  • 西到任家嘴
  • 东西长6公里
  • 南北宽3公里
  • 面积18平方公里
  • 秦孝公十二年迁都咸阳
  • 面积约100平方公里
  • 惠文王时面积约200平方公里
  • 昭襄王时面积达460平方公里
  • 秦始皇三十五年营建最大宫殿朝宫
  • 前殿阿房宫面积约87万平方米

06. 汉长安城

Han Chang'an City
西汉 古代都邑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安城西北郊约10公里处的未央区汉城乡

汉长安城遗址是西汉时期的都城遗址，位于西安城西北郊约10公里处的未央区汉城乡，面积约34.39平方公里。1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位。

西汉长安城是在秦咸阳兴乐宫基础上建立起来的。汉高祖五年（公元前202年）刘邦决定修复兴乐宫，并改名为长乐宫，并以此为基础兴建都城，名为长安城。汉高祖七年（公元前200年）二月，长乐宫建成。

汉高祖八年（公元前199年），又在长乐宫西侧建未央宫，作为西汉长安的主要宫殿，由兴乐宫改成的长乐宫则供太后居住。

汉武帝太初元年（公元前104年）兴建建章宫，太初四年（公元前101年）营建桂宫、明光宫，扩建北宫，开凿昆明池和上林苑，扩建宫殿、苑囿、明堂、宗庙等建筑，使汉长安城的建设达到极盛。

（一）规划设计思想：汉长安在城址、营建时期乃至规划传统发展上，有明显的延续性。汉长安借鉴了秦咸阳的设计手法，利用天象来规划城市。汉长安城在形状上类似南斗和北斗，这是汉长安城称为斗城的基础。在天文学意义上，南斗、北斗蕴涵着十分丰富的文化内涵。

南斗和北斗很早就被人们所崇拜，殷商时代，人们就已经为北斗举行隆重的祀典，并且殷人将北斗星与王相联，认为祀北斗可以使王受佑。秦统一后，秦始皇命建南斗庙、北斗庙。北斗七星，由于明确的标志性特征，在表达天物，北斗七星，所谓璇玑玉衡，以齐七政。斗为帝车，运于中央，临制四乡，分阴阳，建四时，均五行，移节度，定诸纪，皆系于斗。筑斗城的意义也在于齐七政，它意味着稳定与秩序，象征着国家体制完备，政通人和。

晋代葛洪在《西京杂记》中记述了西汉初人们崇拜北斗的习俗。因此，在汉长安的规划中，代表紫微宫的未央宫、代表北斗的北城墙西段和代表南斗的南城墙东段，恰好组成了一幅周天星图。（二）功能布局：汉长安城利用原有宫殿基础逐步扩建而成，城墙建于长乐宫和未央宫修成之后，都城平面略呈不规则的正方形，城墙除东面平直以外，其他三面均凹凸曲折。

城西北角，东西墙南部和南墙西部向外折曲，称之为南为南斗形，北为北斗形，或称为斗城。汉长安城布局充分体现了《周礼·考工记》关于古代都城建制的特点。汉长安城的皇宫是未央宫，位于城的西南部，此外在东、北、东北还分别有后妃居住的长乐宫、桂宫、北宫和明光宫。未央宫以北，长安城北部，是首都的主要市场——东市和西市。

宗庙与社稷分别位于未央宫东南和西南。上述布局，反映了都城的前朝后市、左祖右社特点。都城百姓的里居位于长安城东北部，贵族的宅第则大多在皇宫附近。长安城之西和西南部是著名的建章宫与上林苑。长安城北郊是皇室避暑离宫——甘泉宫。城的南面安门外设明堂、辟雍和祖庙、社稷坛等祭祀用建筑物。由于宫殿陆续建造且较为分散，因此每座宫殿都有宫城环绕。

汉长安城城墙采用黄土版筑，东、南、西、北城墙分别长5917米、7453米、4766米、6878米，总长25014米。

地面现存长度分别为4184米、5873米、1795米、1399米，现存最高约10米，基宽12~16米。

城墙四周各开三座城门，四面有渠水或河水环绕。南面由东向西依次为：覆盎门（杜门）、安门、西安门；西面由南向北依次为章城门、直城门、雍门；北面由西向东依次为：横门、厨城门、洛城门；东面由北向南依次为：宣平门、清明门、霸城门。每座城门有3个门道，各宽约8米，可容4辆车并行，与城门相连的街道也分成3股，中间为皇帝专用的御道。

汉长安城有8条大街，街道均为土路，街道宽约45米，中间有两条排水沟将街道分为三股。城内有160个闾里，居民约24.6万人。

汉武帝元狩三年（公元前120年）在大幅度扩展秦时的上林苑后，其占地约300余顷，苑内建有离宫30多处，大量亭台楼阁，布满珍禽奇兽，名木异草，汉朝皇帝秋冬季节都要在苑中射猎。

元狩四年（公元前119年），在长安西南上林苑中开挖周长20公里的昆明池，作为城市供水和漕运用的水源，还可在池中训练水军的船战。池水一方面由西南入城经未央宫中的沧池后再经明渠屈曲向东出城；

一方面分支注入沿城的漕渠而再向东注入郑渠，和黄河相通，既方便漕运，又可供农业灌溉，是一举数得的城市蓄水、引水工程。王莽九庙遗址共发现12座建筑基址。1~11号建筑基址为一组，外边围绕每边长1400米的方形夯土墙。12号建筑基址另为一组，位于南墙外中部。

这12座建筑基址的形制基本相同，中心是平面呈亚字形的主体建筑，外有近方形的围墙，墙的四面辟门。出土的石础上有始建年号，其位置和规模，都与《汉书·王莽传》所载的王莽九庙相符。

— ENGLISH —

The Han Chang'an City Site is the capital site of the Western Han dynasty, located in Hancheng Township, Weiyang District, about 10 km northwest of Xi'an city, with an area of about 34.39 square kilometers.

It was designated as the first batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1961. Western Han Chang'an City was built on the basis of Qin Xianyang's Xingle Palace.

In the 5th year of Emperor Gaozu of Han (202 BC), Liu Bang decided to restore Xingle Palace and rename it Changle Palace, and build the capital on this basis, named Chang'an City. In the 7th year of Emperor Gaozu (200 BC), Changle Palace was completed.

In the 8th year of Emperor Gaozu (199 BC), Weiyang Palace was built west of Changle Palace as the main palace of Western Han Chang'an, while Changle Palace (converted from Xingle Palace) was for the Empress Dowager to live in.

In the 1st year of Taichu of Emperor Wu (104 BC), Jianzhang Palace was built; in the 4th year of Taichu (101 BC), Gui Palace and Mingguang Palace were built, North Palace was expanded, Kunming Lake and Shanglin Park were dug, and palaces, gardens, halls, and ancestral temples were expanded, bringing the construction of Han Chang'an City to its peak.

(1) Planning and Design Philosophy: Han Chang'an had obvious continuity in city site, construction period, and even planning tradition. Han Chang'an borrowed the design techniques of Qin Xianyang, using celestial phenomena to plan the city.

Han Chang'an City was similar in shape to the Southern Dipper and Northern Dipper, which was the basis for Han Chang'an being called the 'Dipper City.' In astronomical sense, the Southern Dipper and Northern Dipper contain very rich cultural connotations.

The Southern Dipper and Northern Dipper were worshipped by people very early; in the Shang Dynasty, people already held grand ceremonies for the Northern Dipper, and the Shang people associated the Northern Dipper with the king, believing that worshipping the Northern Dipper could bless the king.

After Qin unification, Qin Shi Huang ordered the building of Southern Dipper Temple and Northern Dipper Temple.

The Northern Dipper's seven stars, due to their clear landmark features, in expressing celestial objects, the Northern Dipper seven stars, so-called 'the Xuanji jade balance, to align the seven governments.' The Dipper is the emperor's chariot, moving in the center, controlling the four directions, dividing yin and yang, establishing the four seasons, balancing the five elements, shifting seasons, and determining all calendars, all tied to the Dipper.

The significance of building a Dipper City also lay in 'aligning the seven governments,' meaning stability and order, symbolizing a complete national system and smooth governance.

Ge Hong of the Jin Dynasty recorded in 'Xijing Zaji' the custom of people worshipping the Northern Dipper in the early Western Han.

Therefore, in the planning of Han Chang'an, Weiyang Palace representing the Purple Palace, the western section of the north city wall representing the Northern Dipper, and the eastern section of the south city wall representing the Southern Dipper恰好组成了一幅周天星图。 (2) Functional Layout: Han Chang'an City was gradually expanded using existing palace foundations; the city wall was built after Changle Palace and Weiyang Palace were completed, and the capital plane was slightly irregular square, with the city wall concave-convex and winding on three sides except the straight east side.

The northwest corner of the city, the southern part of the west wall, and the western part of the south wall bend outward, called 'south in Southern Dipper shape, north in Northern Dipper shape,' or 'Dipper City.' The layout of Han Chang'an City fully reflected the characteristics of ancient capital construction in the 'Rites of Zhou: Artificers' Record.' The imperial palace of Han Chang'an was Weiyang Palace, located in the southwest of the city; in addition, Changle Palace, Gui Palace, North Palace, and Mingguang Palace for empresses and consorts were located in the east, north, and northeast respectively.

North of Weiyang Palace, in the northern part of Chang'an City, were the capital's main markets - East Market and West Market. The ancestral temple and altar of soil and grain were located southeast and southwest of Weiyang Palace respectively.

The above layout reflected the capital's characteristics of 'front court, rear market' and 'left ancestral temple, right altar.' The residential areas of common people were located in the northeast of Chang'an City, while aristocratic residences were mostly near the imperial palace.

West and southwest of Chang'an City were the famous Jianzhang Palace and Shanglin Park. North of Chang'an City was the imperial summer palace - Ganquan Palace.

South of the city, outside the Anmen Gate, were sacrificial buildings such as the Mingtang (hall of light), Piyong (academy), ancestral temple, and altar of soil and grain.

Since palaces were built successively and were relatively scattered, each palace was surrounded by its own palace wall.

The city wall of Han Chang'an was built with rammed loess; the east, south, west, and north walls were 5,917 m, 7,453 m, 4,766 m, and 6,878 m long respectively, with a total length of 25,014 m.

The existing ground lengths are 4,184 m, 5,873 m, 1,795 m, and 1,399 m respectively, with the highest existing about 10 m and base width 12-16 m. Three city gates were opened on each side of the wall, surrounded by canals or rivers on all four sides.

From east to west on the south side: Fu'ang Gate (Du Gate), An Gate, Xi'an Gate; from south to north on the west side: Zhangcheng Gate, Zhicheng Gate, Yong Gate; from west to east on the north side: Heng Gate, Chu Gate, Luo Gate; from north to south on the east side: Xuanping Gate, Qingming Gate, Bacheng Gate.

Each city gate had 3 gateways, each about 8 m wide, accommodating 4 carriages side by side; the streets connected to the gates were also divided into 3 lanes, with the middle being the emperor's exclusive imperial road.

Han Chang'an City had 8 main streets, all dirt roads, about 45 m wide, with two drainage ditches in the middle dividing the street into 3 lanes. There were 160 li (neighborhoods) in the city, with about 246,000 residents.

In the 3rd year of Yuanshou of Emperor Wu (120 BC), after greatly expanding the Qin-era Shanglin Park, it covered about 300+ qing, with more than 30 detached palaces, numerous pavilions and towers, filled with rare birds and animals, famous trees and exotic plants; Han emperors hunted in the park in autumn and winter.

In the 4th year of Yuanshou (119 BC), Kunming Lake with a circumference of 20 km was dug in Shanglin Park southwest of Chang'an, serving as a water source for urban water supply and grain transport, and also for training naval warfare in the lake.

The lake water一方面 entered the city from the southwest, passed through Cang Pool in Weiyang Palace, then flowed east out of the city through the Ming Canal; on the other hand, branches injected into the grain transport canal along the city and then east into the Zheng Canal, connecting with the Yellow River, facilitating both grain transport and agricultural irrigation, a multi-purpose urban water storage and diversion project.

A total of 12 building foundations were found at the Wang Mang Nine Temples site. Foundations 1-11 formed one group, surrounded by a square rammed earth wall 1,400 m on each side. Foundation 12 formed another group, located in the middle outside the south wall.

The forms of these 12 building foundations were basically the same, with a main building in the center with a plane in the shape of the character 'ya' (亚), surrounded by a nearly square wall with gates on all four sides.

The unearthed stone pillar bases bear the 'Shijian' (first construction) reign mark, and their positions and scales match the 'Wang Mang Nine Temples' recorded in the 'Book of Han: Biography of Wang Mang.'

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 西汉时期的都城遗址
  • 面积约34.39平方公里
  • 在秦咸阳兴乐宫基础上建立
  • 汉高祖五年决定修复兴乐宫改名长乐宫
  • 汉高祖七年长乐宫建成
  • 汉高祖八年建未央宫
  • 汉武帝太初元年兴建建章宫
  • 斗城
  • 南为南斗形北为北斗形
  • 前朝后市
  • 左祖右社
  • 城墙用黄土版筑
  • 周长25014米
  • 12座城门

07. 隋唐长安城

Sui-Tang Chang'an City
隋、唐 古代都邑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市

隋立国之初，仍以汉长安城为都。由于此城历经800余年，旧城在功能、文化等各方面已经不能适应一个新时代都城的需求。

自开皇二年（公元582年）正月开始，隋文帝下诏命高颎、李询、宇文恺、刘龙等人于汉长安城之东南建造新都大兴，整个都城规划方案都是由宇文恺主持设计。大兴城的规划先筑城墙，后辟街道里坊，到次年三月初步完成，其总面积约36平方公里。

大业九年（公元613年），又动用10余万人在宫城和皇城以外建造了外郭城，大兴城总体格局至此基本形成。公元618年，唐代隋，大兴城更名为长安城，都城建设最为显著的就是大明宫、兴庆宫等重大工程，格局在延续大兴城的基础上又有了新的发展。

经数次大规模增建，都城面积达84平方公里。城市规划日臻完善，建筑宏伟、百业兴旺，最多时人口超过100万，成为当时中国乃至世界上最伟大的城市之一。

1996年，隋大兴、唐长安城遗址公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位。1. 规划设计思想：大兴城的规划继承了曹魏邺城、北魏洛阳等前代都城规划经验，创造了新的都城制度与规划结构。

在政治理想和社会理想上，继承了祖社关系，创新了朝市关系，并将魏晋以来的佛、道信仰纳入城市整体构架。并在都城东南西北四方设计了圜丘、日坛、月坛等，形成新的宇宙秩序。大兴城是以太极宫为基本模数单位，皇城（包括宫城）和外郭城分别是太极宫面积的5倍和9倍，形成一种九五空间关系。与此同时，外郭城、皇城（包括宫城）和太极宫的平面都是一个内含等边三角形的矩形。

此后，武廷海（2009）又进一步从形势论的角度认为，举势以立形和聚形而展势是宇文恺规划大兴城的两个重要方面。2. 功能布局：隋唐长安城的宫城、皇城位居都城中央北部，外郭东、西、南三面各置三门，以应左祖右社、旁三门的传统制度。在春明门外规划日坛，开远门外规划月坛，启夏门外规划圜丘、先农坛，形成一种神圣的宇宙秩序。

这些都成为后世都城规划设计的典范。隋唐长安城平面呈东西略长、南北略窄的长方形，周长约35.5公里。据考古实测，从东墙的春明门到西墙的金光门之间，东西宽为9721米（包括两城墙厚度）。

从南墙的明德门到北墙的玄武门偏东处之间，南北长为8651米（包括两城墙厚度）。唐长安城分三大部分：宫城、皇城和外郭城。宫城位于全城北部中心，皇城在宫城之南，外郭城则以宫城、皇城为中心，向东南西三面展开。

城内街道纵横交错，城内南北11条大街，东西14条大街，划分出110座里坊，形似棋盘。唐长安城以宫城承天门、皇城朱雀门和外郭明德门构成南北中轴线，将长安城分成东西对称两部分。为突出北部宫城、延喜殿、延嘉殿和玄武门等高大建筑物布置在中轴线北端，以其雄伟的气势来展现皇权的威严。

3. 建筑特征：（1）宫城：宫城位于城市北部正中，平面为长方形，东西长2830.3米，南北宽1492.1米，周长8.6公里。城四周有围墙，南面正中开承天门（隋称广阳门），东、西为延喜门和安福门，北墙中部开玄武门。

宫城分为三部分，正中为太极宫（隋称大兴宫），是隋朝和初唐时期的皇帝居所和朝会之地，称作大内。东侧是东宫，为太子居所，西侧是掖庭宫，为宫女的住处和犯罪官僚家属属妇女入宫劳动之处。宫内由南向北分为前朝、后寝和苑囿三块区域。

前朝的正殿为太极殿（隋称大兴殿），太极殿东西宽1285米，南北长1492米，面积约1.92平方公里，四周有廊庑围成的巨大宫院，东西两侧建有官署。后寝的主殿是两仪殿，周边有万春殿、千秋殿、甘露殿、神龙殿、安仁殿等殿堂。

苑囿位于宫殿最后部，有亭台池沼等。

大明宫位于太极宫东北方的龙首塬高地上，经考古实测，宫城西墙长2256米，东墙长2614米，北墙长1135米，南墙是外郭城北墙的一部分、长1674米，周围长7.6公里，其形制略呈楔形，总面积3.5平方公里，是三大内中最大的一座，可俯瞰整座长安城。

宫城建于贞观八年（公元634年），自唐高宗起，先后有17位唐朝皇帝在此居住和处理朝政，称为东内。宫城为中轴对称格局，前部由丹凤门、含元殿、宣政殿、紫宸殿等构成前朝的南北中轴线，后部以太液池为中心组成内庭，分布麟德殿、三清殿、大福殿、清思殿等数十座殿宇楼阁。

兴庆宫位于外郭城东部，南北长1250米，东西宽1080米，周长4.6公里，面积约1.35平方公里，规模在三大内中最小。

该宫原是唐玄宗早年任临淄王时的藩邸，开元二年（公元714年）改名为兴庆宫。开元十四年（公元726年）进行扩建，

— ENGLISH —

At the beginning of the Sui dynasty, Han Chang'an City was still used as the capital. Since this city had experienced over 800 years, the old city could no longer meet the needs of a new era capital in terms of function, culture, and other aspects.

Starting from the first month of the 2nd year of Kaihuang (582 AD), Emperor Wen of Sui issued an edict ordering Gao Jiong, Li Xun, Yuwen Kai, Liu Long and others to build the new capital Daxing southeast of Han Chang'an City; the entire capital planning scheme was designed under the leadership of Yuwen Kai.

The planning of Daxing City first built the city walls, then opened streets and neighborhoods, and was initially completed by the third month of the following year, with a total area of about 36 square kilometers.

In the 9th year of Daye (613 AD), more than 100,000 people were mobilized to build the outer guo city outside the palace city and imperial city, and the overall pattern of Daxing City was basically formed by then.

In 618 AD, the Tang dynasty replaced the Sui, and Daxing City was renamed Chang'an City; the most significant capital construction projects were Daming Palace and Xingqing Palace, and the pattern developed further on the basis of Daxing City.

After several large-scale expansions, the capital area reached 84 square kilometers.

Urban planning became increasingly perfect, with magnificent architecture and prosperous industries; at its peak, the population exceeded 1 million, making it one of the greatest cities in China and even the world at that time.

In 1996, the Sui Daxing and Tang Chang'an City sites were designated as the fourth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites. 1.

Planning and Design Philosophy: The planning of Daxing City inherited the planning experience of previous capitals such as Cao Wei Yecheng and Northern Wei Luoyang, creating a new capital system and planning structure.

In political and social ideals, it inherited the ancestral-temple and altar relationship, innovated the court-market relationship, and incorporated Buddhist and Taoist beliefs since the Wei-Jin period into the overall urban framework.

It also designed the Circular Mound Altar, Sun Altar, and Moon Altar in the four directions of southeast, northwest, forming a new cosmic order.

Daxing City used Taiji Palace as the basic modulus unit; the imperial city (including palace city) and outer guo city were respectively 5 times and 9 times the area of Taiji Palace, forming a nine-five spatial relationship.

At the same time, the planes of the outer guo city, imperial city (including palace city), and Taiji Palace were all rectangles containing equilateral triangles.

Later, Wu Tinghai (2009) further from the perspective of situation theory believed that 'using momentum to establish form' and 'gathering form to develop momentum' were two important aspects of Yuwen Kai's planning of Daxing City. 2.

Functional Layout: The palace city and imperial city of Sui-Tang Chang'an were located in the central-northern part of the capital, with three gates each on the east, west, and south sides of the outer guo, corresponding to the traditional system of 'left ancestral temple, right altar' and 'three gates on each side.' Outside the Chunming Gate, the Sun Altar was planned; outside the Kaiyuan Gate, the Moon Altar was planned; outside the Qixia Gate, the Circular Mound Altar and Xiannong Altar were planned, forming a sacred cosmic order.

These all became models for later capital planning and design. Sui-Tang Chang'an City had a plane slightly longer east-west and narrower north-south, with a circumference of about 35.5 km.

According to archaeological measurements, from Chunming Gate on the east wall to Jinguang Gate on the west wall, the east-west width was 9,721 m (including both wall thicknesses).

From Mingde Gate on the south wall to the east of Xuanwu Gate on the north wall, the north-south length was 8,651 m (including both wall thicknesses). Tang Chang'an City was divided into three major parts: palace city, imperial city, and outer guo city.

The palace city was located in the northern center of the whole city, the imperial city was south of the palace city, and the outer guo city expanded to the southeast, south, and west centered on the palace city and imperial city.

Streets crisscrossed inside the city, with 11 north-south streets and 14 east-west streets, dividing into 110 neighborhoods, shaped like a chessboard.

Tang Chang'an City used the Chengtian Gate of the palace city, Zhuque Gate of the imperial city, and Mingde Gate of the outer guo to form a north-south central axis, dividing Chang'an City into two symmetrical east-west parts.

To highlight the northern palace city, tall buildings such as Yanxi Hall, Yanji Hall, and Xuanwu Gate were arranged at the northern end of the central axis, using their majestic momentum to demonstrate imperial authority. 3.

Architectural Features: (1) Palace City: The palace city was located in the northern center of the city, with a rectangular plane, 2,830.3 m east-west and 1,492.1 m north-south, with a circumference of 8.6 km.

It was surrounded by walls, with Chengtian Gate (called Guangyang Gate in Sui) in the center of the south side, Yanxi Gate and Anfu Gate on the east and west, and Xuanwu Gate in the middle of the north wall.

The palace city was divided into three parts: Taiji Palace (called Daxing Palace in Sui) in the center, the emperor's residence and court place during the Sui and early Tang, called the 'Great Interior'; the East Palace on the east side for the crown prince's residence; and the Yeting Palace on the west side for palace maids' residences and female family members of criminal officials to labor in the palace.

Inside, from south to north, it was divided into three areas: the former court, the rear bedchamber, and the garden.

The main hall of the former court was Taiji Hall (called Daxing Hall in Sui), 1,285 m east-west and 1,492 m north-south, with an area of about 1.92 square kilometers, surrounded by corridors forming a huge palace courtyard, with government offices on the east and west sides.

The main hall of the rear bedchamber was Liangyi Hall, surrounded by Wanchun Hall, Qianqiu Hall, Ganlu Hall, Shenlong Hall, Anren Hall and other halls. The garden was located at the rearmost part of the palace, with pavilions, terraces, and ponds.

Daming Palace was located on the high ground of Longshou Plateau northeast of Taiji Palace; according to archaeological measurements, the west wall of the palace city was 2,256 m long, the east wall 2,614 m, the north wall 1,135 m, and the south wall was part of the outer guo north wall, 1,674 m long, with a circumference of 7.6 km, slightly wedge-shaped, with a total area of 3.5 square kilometers, the largest of the 'Three Great Interiors,' overlooking the entire Chang'an City.

The palace city was built in the 8th year of Zhenguan (634 AD); from Emperor Gaozong onward, 17 Tang emperors successively lived and handled government affairs here, called the 'East Interior.' The palace city had a central-axis symmetrical layout; the front part formed the north-south central axis of the former court with Danfeng Gate, Hanyuan Hall, Xuanzheng Hall, Zichen Hall, etc.; the rear part formed the inner court centered on the Taiye Pool, with dozens of halls and pavilions such as Linde Hall, Sanqing Hall, Dafu Hall, and Qingsi Hall distributed.

Xingqing Palace was located in the eastern part of the outer guo city, 1,250 m north-south and 1,080 m east-west, with a circumference of 4.6 km and an area of about 1.35 square kilometers, the smallest of the 'Three Great Interiors.' This palace was originally the mansion of Emperor Xuanzong of Tang when he was the Prince of Linzi; in the 2nd year of Kaiyuan (714 AD), it was renamed Xingqing Palace.

In the 14th year of Kaiyuan (726 AD), it was expanded,

08. 西安古城

Xi'an Ancient City
唐、五代、宋、元、明、清 古代城镇 西安市

唐末唐昭宗于天祐元年（公元904年）东迁洛阳后，长安从此结束了作为国都的历史，但仍为西北重镇。后因城大人少而难于防守，天祐元年（公元904年）佑国军节度使韩建为军事需要，将长安城范围缩小。

元代李好文《长安志图》载：去宫城，又去郭城，重修子城（皇城）。南闭朱雀门，又闭延喜门（东）、安福门（西），北开玄武门，是为新城，即放弃外郭城和宫城，重修皇城，南面留含光门、安上门，东面留景风门，北面留玄武门，西面留顺义门，形成新城。同时筑西郭小城为长安县（今西安市长安区）治，东郭小城为咸宁县治，为今西安城的范围奠定了基础。

五代到元朝，长安城基建变化较多，但城垣却始终未变。长安城元至元十六年（1279年）改为安西路城，皇庆元年（1312年）改为奉元路城，成为元朝统治西北、西南的大本营。

明洪武二年（1369年），明军入陕，改奉元路为西安府，今西安的名称起源于此。洪武三年（1370年），朱元璋封次子朱樉为秦王，遂扩建西安城和修建秦王府。王城于洪武九年（1376年）竣工。

此次府城扩建利用了皇城的东北隅，保留了唐末长安城皇城的西墙和南墙。王府东西408米，南北671米，四面各设一门。北面在皇城北门重明门址建广智门，东面体仁门，西面遵义门，南面端履门。为保卫王府城，循唐长安城皇城向东、北两面扩建，各自延长约四分之一。

扩建后的西安城，东西4256米，南北2708米，周长13.74公里。四面各设一门：东长乐门，西安定门，南永宁门，北安远门。四门通东、西、南、北四街。

清顺治六年（1644年），西安府设满城，将北大街以东、东大街以北约占全城1/3的城区筑墙设防。满城内拆毁秦王府，另置将军府、八旗教场及军营，成为封闭的军事区。清康熙二十二年（1683年），又在今大差市以东从满城南城墙间筑墙，将城市东南部划为南城，作为汉军驻防军。

这样，就将城市的居民中人和经济重心完全挤压到城区的西半部。满城、南城军事区加上陕甘总督、陕西巡抚、陕西布政使治所和府县衙署等，全城面积的一半以上被兵营、官衙占据。全城的四个城门中，对外交往最频繁的东门被满城独占。其封闭与落后在同期全国省会城市中亦为绝无仅有。

— ENGLISH —

After Emperor Zhaozong of Tang moved the capital east to Luoyang in the 1st year of Tianyou (904 AD), Chang'an ended its history as a national capital but remained an important northwestern town.

Later, due to the large city and small population making defense difficult, in the 1st year of Tianyou (904 AD), Han Jian, the military governor of the Youguo Army, for military needs, reduced the scope of Chang'an City.

According to Li Haowen's 'Chang'an Zhi Tu' of the Yuan Dynasty: 'Remove the palace city, also remove the outer guo city, rebuild the sub-city (imperial city).

Close the Zhuque Gate in the south, also close the Yanxi Gate (east) and Anfu Gate (west), open the Xuanwu Gate in the north, this is the new city' - that is, abandon the outer guo city and palace city, rebuild the imperial city, keeping the Hanguang Gate and Anshang Gate in the south, Jingfeng Gate in the east, Xuanwu Gate in the north, and Shunyi Gate in the west, forming the new city.

At the same time, a small western outer city was built as the seat of Chang'an County (present-day Chang'an District, Xi'an), and a small eastern outer city as the seat of Xianning County, laying the foundation for the scope of present-day Xi'an city.

From the Five Dynasties to the Yuan Dynasty, Chang'an City had many infrastructure changes, but the city walls remained unchanged.

Chang'an City was changed to Anxi Road City in the 16th year of Zhiyuan (1279), and to Fengyuan Road City in the 1st year of Huangqing (1312), becoming the Yuan Dynasty's base for governing the northwest and southwest.

In the 2nd year of Hongwu, Ming (1369), the Ming army entered Shaanxi and changed Fengyuan Road to Xi'an Prefecture, from which the name Xi'an originated.

In the 3rd year of Hongwu (1370), Zhu Yuanzhang enfeoffed his second son Zhu Shuang as the Prince of Qin, and then expanded Xi'an City and built the Prince of Qin's mansion. The royal city was completed in the 9th year of Hongwu (1376).

This prefectural city expansion utilized the northeast corner of the imperial city, retaining the west and south walls of the late Tang Chang'an imperial city. The royal city was 408 m east-west and 671 m north-south, with one gate on each side.

In the north, the Guangzhi Gate was built at the site of the Chongming Gate (north gate of the imperial city); in the east, the Tiren Gate; in the west, the Zunyi Gate; in the south, the Duanlv Gate.

To protect the royal city, following the Tang Chang'an imperial city, it was expanded eastward and northward, each extended by about a quarter. The expanded Xi'an City was 4,256 m east-west, 2,708 m north-south, with a circumference of 13.74 km.

One gate was set on each side: Changle Gate in the east, Anding Gate in the west, Yongning Gate in the south, and Anyuan Gate in the north. The four gates connected to the east, west, south, and north streets.

In the 6th year of Shunzhi, Qing (1644), Xi'an Prefecture established the 'Manchu City,' building walls and fortifications in the urban area east of North Street and north of East Street, accounting for about 1/3 of the whole city.

Inside the Manchu City, the Prince of Qin's mansion was demolished, and a general's mansion, Eight Banners parade ground, and military camps were established, becoming a closed military zone.

In the 22nd year of Kangxi (1683), walls were built between the south wall of the Manchu City east of present-day Dachai City, dividing the southeastern part of the city into the 'South City' as a Han Chinese garrison.

Thus, the city's residents and economic center were completely squeezed into the western half of the urban area.

The Manchu City and South City military zones, plus the offices of the Shaanxi-Gansu Governor-General, Shaanxi Governor, Shaanxi Administrative Commissioner, and prefectural/county yamen, occupied more than half of the city's area with military camps and government offices.

Among the four city gates, the East Gate, which had the most frequent external exchanges, was exclusively occupied by the Manchu City. Its closure and backwardness were unique among provincial capital cities of the same period nationwide.

09. 府谷府州城

Fugu Fuzhou City
五代后唐、宋、金、元、明、清 古代城镇 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市府谷县城东约500米处的石山梁上

府谷府州城坐落于榆林市府谷县城东约500米处的石山梁上，背倚大山，面向河谷，是古代陕西的重要军事城堡。

府州城始建于五代后唐时期，北宋庆历年间（1041~1046年）增筑外城，以后历代均有修葺。现存建筑中，除城墙为五代、北宋时期修筑外，其余均为明清建筑。

1996年公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位。城平面呈不规则靴形，东、南、西三面临悬崖，城墙依山势起伏蜿蜒，周长2320米，墙高7.2米，均为石砌。

设四门，东、南、西三门各建瓮城，南门和西门外有小桥。城内主要街道有两条，一条为东西向，一条为南北向，两条街道在城中部丁字相交，将城分为东北、东南、西南、西北四部分。城内建筑有文庙、城隍庙、关帝庙、祖师庙、文昌阁、魁星楼、千佛洞、悬空寺等。南门外半坡上有娘娘庙和悬空寺等古迹。

— ENGLISH —

Fugu Fuzhou City is located on a stone mountain ridge about 500 m east of Fugu County town, Yulin City, backed by a large mountain and facing the river valley, serving as an important military castle in ancient Shaanxi.

Fuzhou City was first built in the Later Tang period of the Five Dynasties; the outer city was added during the Qingli period of the Northern Song (1041-1046), and it was repaired in successive dynasties.

Among the existing buildings, except for the city wall built in the Five Dynasties and Northern Song periods, the rest are Ming and Qing architecture. It was designated as the fourth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1996.

The city plane is irregularly boot-shaped, with cliffs on the east, south, and west sides; the city wall winds along the mountain terrain, with a circumference of 2,320 m and a wall height of 7.2 m, all built of stone.

It has four gates; the east, south, and west gates each have a barbican, with small bridges outside the south and west gates.

There are two main streets inside the city, one east-west and one north-south; the two streets intersect in a T-shape in the middle of the city, dividing the city into four parts: northeast, southeast, southwest, and northwest.

Buildings inside the city include the Confucian Temple, City God Temple, Guandi Temple, Patriarch Temple, Wenchang Pavilion, Kuixing Tower, Thousand-Buddha Cave, and Hanging Temple. On the half-slope outside the south gate, there are historic sites such as the Niangniang Temple and Hanging Temple.

  • 北宋庆历年间增筑外城

10. 神木高家堡古城

Shenmu Gaojiabao Ancient City
明、清 古代城镇 榆林市级重点文物保护单位 榆林市神木县城西南50公里处的秃尾河东岸

神木高家堡古城位于榆林市神木县城西南50公里处的秃尾河东岸，距明长城约5公里，是陕北保存最完好的古城之一。2007年公布为榆林市级重点文物保护单位。

城池始建于明正统四年（1439年），原为夯筑土城，明万历三十六年（1608年）用砖包砌，清乾隆十五年（1750年）、三十三年（1768年）两度重修，后又多次修葺。

古城平面呈长方形，东西墙均长311米，南北墙均长431米，墙高6.5~9.1米，基宽7.52米。城墙上部建有1米高的女儿墙，间有垛口、瞭望洞。

北城头修有三官楼，东南角建有魁星楼。现除魁星楼、女儿墙、垛口、瞭望洞被破坏外，其余均保存完好。东南西三门各建瓮城、箭楼，分别镌刻耸观、永兴、安澜石额。环城女儿墙整饬，垛口齿列，每隔数十米处必有马面突出，俗语戏称城小拐角大，实际为军事防御所需而置。

北城无门，外百余米处建小城垣，东西贯通，长千余米，高近3米许，兼有防洪御寇之功用。城内原有中兴楼、城隍庙、财神庙、五道庙、祖师庙、西门寺、贞节牌坊等古建筑，现仅存中兴楼和财神庙，城内街道以中兴楼为轴心，向东西辐射为东西街，向南北辐射为南街和北巷。

明、清到民国初，南街最为繁华，至今大部分铺面保存完好，东、西、南街各通有巷道。城内民居建筑群为典型的北方构筑风格。既有四合院，如北巷的李家大院；又有前庭大院，如东街的卢家大院、西街的韩家大院；还有楼院，如十字巷的李家楼院，同心巷的刘家楼院等。

— ENGLISH —

Shenmu Gaojiabao Ancient City is located on the east bank of the Tuwei River, 50 km southwest of Shenmu County town, Yulin City, about 5 km from the Ming Great Wall, and is one of the best-preserved ancient cities in northern Shaanxi.

It was designated as a Yulin City-level Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2007.

The city was first built in the 4th year of Zhengtong, Ming (1439), originally a rammed earth city; in the 36th year of Wanli, Ming (1608), it was faced with bricks; in the 15th year (1750) and 33rd year (1768) of Qianlong, Qing, it was twice rebuilt, and later repaired many times.

The ancient city has a rectangular plane, with both east and west walls 311 m long, both north and south walls 431 m long, wall height 6.5-9.1 m, and base width 7.52 m.

On the upper part of the city wall, a 1-m-high parapet wall was built, with crenellations and lookout holes in between. On the north city head, the Sanguan Tower was built; at the southeast corner, the Kuixing Tower was built.

Currently, except for the Kuixing Tower, parapet wall, crenellations, and lookout holes being damaged, the rest are well preserved.

The east, south, and west gates each have a barbican and arrow tower, respectively engraved with stone inscriptions 'Songguan' (Towering View), 'Yongxing' (Eternal Prosperity), and 'Anlan' (Peaceful Waves).

The surrounding parapet walls are neat, with crenellations in rows, and every few tens of meters there must be a horse-face (buttress) protruding; the folk saying jokingly calls it 'small city with big corners,' actually placed for military defense needs.

The north city has no gate; more than 100 m outside, a small city wall was built, running east-west, over 1,000 m long and nearly 3 m high, serving both flood prevention and bandit defense functions.

Inside the city, there were originally ancient buildings such as Zhongxing Tower, City God Temple, God of Wealth Temple, Wudao Temple, Patriarch Temple, West Gate Temple, and Chastity Archway; now only Zhongxing Tower and God of Wealth Temple remain.

The streets inside the city take Zhongxing Tower as the axis, radiating east-west as the East-West Street, and north-south as South Street and North Lane.

From Ming, Qing to early Republican period, South Street was the most prosperous, and most shop fronts are still well preserved today; the east, west, and south streets each connect with lanes. The residential building complex inside the city is of typical northern construction style.

There are courtyard houses (siheyuan), such as the Li Family Compound in North Lane; there are front-courtyard large compounds, such as the Lu Family Compound on East Street and the Han Family Compound on West Street; there are also tower compounds, such as the Li Family Tower Compound in Cross Lane and the Liu Family Tower Compound in Tongxin Lane.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 距明长城约5公里
  • 陕北保存最完好的古城之一
  • 2007年公布为榆林市级重点文物保护单位
  • 城池始建于明正统四年
  • 原为夯筑土城
  • 明万历三十六年用砖包砌
  • 清乾隆十五年、三十三年两度重修
  • 后又多次修葺
  • 古城平面呈长方形
  • 东西墙均长311米
  • 南北墙均长431米
  • 墙高6.5~9.1米
  • 基宽7.52米
  • 城墙上部建有1米高的女儿墙
  • 间有垛口、瞭望洞

11. 榆林卫城

Yulin Guard City
明 古代城镇 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市

榆林卫城为明长城九边重镇之一，现存古城大体为明代建制，是陕西省保存较完整的古城。2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位。

明永乐元年（1403年）在今榆林市设置榆林寨，正统初年（1436年）改建为堡，成化七年（1471年）增立榆林卫，成化九年（1473年），延绥镇迁驻于榆林卫城。

受河水和山势所限，明代曾三拓榆林城：明成化二十二年（1486年）扩北城，弘治五年（1492年）扩南城，明正德十年（1515年）建南关外城，均为向南、北两面发展，因而形成南窄北狭长的布局。

卫城地处半山坡处，东高西低，平面呈不规则矩形，南北较长，近3公里，东西略窄，约1公里多，周围约7公里。因建于长城脚下驼峰山上，又名驼城。古城选址体现防卫需要：驼峰山自东而西渐低，落向榆溪河谷。城南有泥沟河围绕，城北有鸳鸯湖，明长城著名关隘镇北台即雄峙于城北偏东位置。

卫城明代原有城市七座，后东西各废一座为五座。东（振武门）、南（镇远门）、北（广榆门）、西宣威门、小西门新乐门。五门均为拱券式，门洞上额镶有门匾，顶部均建有二层敌楼（均毁），东南两门设瓮城。东城墙上建有红砖砌筑的文昌楼（已毁），为全城的制高点。东南城角建二层高的魁星楼（已残）。

筑城时随地形不同，加设马面，大小不等，相距不一，共十二处。城墙内夯黄土，夯层厚16~20厘米，外墙砌以砖石，上设垛堞。底宽15米，顶宽9米，高12米。

原南城墙长1060.4米、西城墙长2124.5米、北城墙长1168.9米、东城墙长2435.5米，总长6789.3米，现存城墙总长5677.8米。

城内仅有一条大街，贯通南、北门，为榆林城商业和交通中心。横跨南、北大街，前后屹立数座城楼、牌坊，原有文昌楼、万佛楼、新明楼、钟楼、凯歌楼、鼓楼及四座木牌坊，现尚存新明楼、万佛楼和钟楼三座。

古时城内重要建筑，如衙署、文庙（府庙、县庙）、粮仓等均分布在市街两侧，今已不存。城内居住也分布在市街两侧，有30多条东西巷道与市街相通。城东部地处驼峰山山梁，地势高亢，自南至北密布佛寺、道观、宗祠，形成东山古建筑群。

— ENGLISH —

Yulin Guard City was one of the nine important frontier towns of the Ming Great Wall; the existing ancient city is generally of Ming dynasty establishment and is a relatively well-preserved ancient city in Shaanxi Province.

It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006.

In the 1st year of Yongle, Ming (1403), Yulin Stockade was established in present-day Yulin City; in the early Zhengtong period (1436), it was converted into a fort; in the 7th year of Chenghua (1471), Yulin Guard was added; in the 9th year of Chenghua (1473), the Yansui Town was relocated to Yulin Guard City.

Limited by the river and mountain terrain, the Ming dynasty expanded Yulin City three times: in the 22nd year of Chenghua (1486), the north city was expanded; in the 5th year of Hongzhi (1492), the south city was expanded; in the 10th year of Zhengde (1515), the outer city south of the pass was built, all developing toward the south and north, thus forming a layout narrow in the south and long in the north.

The guard city is located on a half-slope, high in the east and low in the west, with an irregular rectangular plane, longer north-south (nearly 3 km) and slightly narrower east-west (about 1+ km), with a circumference of about 7 km.

Because it was built at the foot of the Great Wall on Tuofeng (Camel Peak) Mountain, it is also called Camel City. The ancient city site reflects defense needs: Tuofeng Mountain gradually lowers from east to west, descending to the Yuxi River valley.

The south of the city is surrounded by the Nigou River, and the north has Yuanyang Lake; the famous pass of the Ming Great Wall, Zhenbeitai, stands majestically at the north-east of the city.

The guard city originally had seven city gates in the Ming dynasty, later one each on the east and west was abandoned, leaving five: East (Zhenwu Gate), South (Zhenyuan Gate), North (Guangyu Gate), West Xuanwei Gate, and Small West Gate Xinle Gate.

All five gates are arch-style, with gate plaques inlaid on the upper lintel of the gate openings, and two-story enemy towers built on top (all destroyed); the southeast two gates have barbicans.

On the east city wall, the Wenchang Tower built of red bricks (destroyed) was the highest point of the whole city. At the southeast city corner, a two-story high Kuixing Tower (damaged) was built.

When building the city, according to different terrain, horse-faces (buttresses) were added, of varying sizes and spacing, totaling twelve.

The city wall is rammed with loess inside, with rammed layers 16-20 cm thick, and the outer wall is faced with brick and stone, with crenellations on top. Base width 15 m, top width 9 m, height 12 m.

Originally the south wall was 1,060.4 m, west wall 2,124.5 m, north wall 1,168.9 m, east wall 2,435.5 m, total length 6,789.3 m; the existing city wall total length is 5,677.8 m.

Inside the city there is only one main street running through the south and north gates, serving as Yulin City's commercial and transportation center.

Spanning the south-north main street, several city towers and archways stand front and back; originally there were Wenchang Tower, Wanfo Tower, Xinming Tower, bell tower, Kaige Tower, drum tower, and four wooden archways; now only Xinming Tower, Wanfo Tower, and bell tower remain.

In ancient times, important buildings inside the city, such as government offices, Confucian temples (prefectural and county temples), and granaries, were distributed on both sides of the main street, now no longer existing.

Residential areas inside the city are also distributed on both sides of the main street, with more than 30 east-west lanes connecting to the main street.

The eastern part of the city is located on the Tuofeng Mountain ridge, with high terrain, and from south to north is densely covered with Buddhist temples, Taoist temples, and ancestral halls, forming the Dongshan (East Mountain) ancient building complex.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 明长城九边重镇之一
  • 现存古城大体为明代建制
  • 陕西省保存较完整的古城
  • 2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 明永乐元年在今榆林市设置榆林寨
  • 正统初年改建为堡
  • 成化七年增立榆林卫
  • 成化九年延绥镇迁驻于榆林卫城
  • 受河水和山势所限
  • 明代曾三拓榆林城
  • 明成化二十二年扩北城
  • 弘治五年扩南城
  • 明正德十年建南关外城
  • 均为向南、北两面发展
  • 因而形成南窄北狭长的布局

12. 吴堡石城

Wubao Stone City
五代后周、宋、金、元、明、清 古代城镇 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市吴堡县宋家川镇北2.5公里处的黄河西岸山巅

吴堡石城位于榆林市吴堡县宋家川镇北2.5公里处的黄河西岸山巅，建于后周（公元951~957年）年间，是西北地区迄今保存最完整的千年古县城。

2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位。吴堡石城地处黄河高原之东陲，黄河中游之西滨，扼秦晋交通之要冲，东以黄河为池，西以沟壑为堑，南为通城官道下至河岸，北门外为咽喉要道连接后山，乃一夫当关，万夫莫开之险地，故被誉为铜吴堡。

明弘治《延安府志·卷之八》记载：五代后周广顺元年（公元951年）即在此筑有吴堡水寨，北宋元丰五年（1082年）扩筑吴堡寨，周围二里二十步，东邻黄河为固，门有三。

又据吴堡古城东门口下山崖现存金朝伪齐刘豫阜昌八年（1137年）摩崖石刻题记：这年三月吴堡寨主兼将折彦若因此城之隳弊……水寨之毁陋，恨无力以竖新，今率寨民，共劝修建，工兴土木，不日而成。金正大三年（1226年）置吴堡县，古城即成为本县的治城。

此后，明洪武十四年（1381年）、嘉靖十五年（1536年）、清乾隆三十一年（1766年）吴堡古城屡有修缮。

民国二十五年（1936年）3月，吴堡县政府由古城迁至宋家川后，古城遂为城关镇的一个行政村（现名古城村），古城被逐渐荒置。吴堡石城依山而建，平面呈不规则圆形，城周长1125米，占地面积约10万平方米。

城墙内为黄土夯筑，外为石砌，条石拉筋。城垣残高1.6~11.2米。城垣设东、南、西、北四门，上均建有门楼，今门楼皆毁。大部分为石砌窑洞式，只有大堂、二堂、四座城门楼、魁星阁、文昌阁为砖木结构。

— ENGLISH —

Wubao Stone City is located on the mountaintop on the west bank of the Yellow River, 2.5 km north of Songjiachuan Town, Wubao County, Yulin City. It was built during the Later Zhou period (951-957 AD) and is the best-preserved thousand-year-old ancient county seat in the northwest region to date.

It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006.

Wubao Stone City is located at the eastern edge of the Loess Plateau, on the west bank of the middle Yellow River, controlling the key transportation pass between Shaanxi and Shanxi; with the Yellow River as its moat to the east, gullies as its trenches to the west, the official road to the riverbank to the south, and the throat passage connecting to the back mountain outside the north gate, it is a strategic position where 'one man guards the pass and ten thousand cannot pass,' hence it is praised as 'Copper Wubao.' According to the 'Yan'an Prefecture Chronicle, Volume 8' of the Hongzhi period, Ming: in the 1st year of Guangshun of the Later Zhou (951 AD), Wubao Water Stockade was built here; in the 5th year of Yuanfeng, Northern Song (1082), Wubao Stockade was expanded, with a circumference of two li and twenty steps, bordering the Yellow River to the east for defense, with three gates.

Also according to the existing cliff stone inscription dated the 8th year of Fuchang of the puppet Qi regime of the Jin Dynasty (1137) on the cliff below the east gate of Wubao Ancient City: in March of this year, the Wubao stockade chief and general Zhe Yanruo, due to the dilapidation of this city...

the destruction and shabbiness of the water stockade, hating the lack of strength to build anew, now led the stockade people to jointly encourage construction, starting civil engineering, completed in a few days.

In the 3rd year of Zhengda, Jin (1226), Wubao County was established, and the ancient city became the county seat. Thereafter, in the 14th year of Hongwu, Ming (1381), the 15th year of Jiajing (1536), and the 31st year of Qianlong, Qing (1766), Wubao Ancient City was repeatedly repaired.

In March of the 25th year of the Republic (1936), after the Wubao County Government moved from the ancient city to Songjiachuan, the ancient city became an administrative village of Chengguan Town (now named Gucheng Village), and the ancient city was gradually abandoned.

Wubao Stone City was built according to the mountain, with an irregular circular plane, a city circumference of 1,125 m, and an area of about 100,000 square meters. The city wall is rammed with loess inside and faced with stone outside, with strip stones as reinforcement.

The remaining city wall height is 1.6-11.2 m. The city wall has four gates: east, south, west, and north, each with a gate tower on top, now all destroyed.

Most buildings are stone-built cave-style; only the main hall, second hall, four gate towers, Kuixing Pavilion, and Wenchang Pavilion are of brick-wood structure.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 西北地区迄今保存最完整的千年古县城
  • 2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 地处黄河高原之东陲
  • 黄河中游之西滨
  • 扼秦晋交通之要冲
  • 东以黄河为池
  • 西以沟壑为堑
  • 南为通城官道下至河岸
  • 北门外为咽喉要道连接后山
  • 一夫当关万夫莫开之险地
  • 被誉为铜吴堡
  • 明弘治延安府志记载五代后周广顺元年即在此筑有吴堡水寨
  • 北宋元丰五年扩筑吴堡寨
  • 周围二里二十步
  • 东邻黄河为固

13. 旬阳蜀河古镇

Xunyang Shuhe Ancient Town
明、清 古代城镇 安康市旬阳县太极城以东53公里处

旬阳县蜀河古镇位于安康市旬阳县太极城以东53公里处，东连湘鄂、南达川渝、西通陇海、北抵关中，是鄂、陕、川三地物流交汇的重要中转集散地与商贸重镇。

蜀河古镇东西宽160米，南北长735米，占地面积约0.8平方公里。因其文化积淀深厚、名胜古迹颇多，素有小汉口之美誉。

蜀河历史悠久，周武王以藩屏周时，封蜀国于河南南阳以北，贫瘠积弱的蜀国迫于楚国的扩张，率民西迁，曾在此停留，故此得名。蜀汉时置县，中兴于明代，繁华于清朝中、末期。蜀河古镇的总体格局是中国传统封建社会商贸交通、择址而居意识的一种反映模式。由于商业的繁荣发展，蜀河古镇呈现出一种自然开放的格局特征。

从西晋始建择址到蜀河古镇格局的最终形成，人们都在不断地选择最佳景观，并在古镇建设中不断地改造和修护环境，完善古镇格局。

蜀河古镇以红岩碥（后山）为龙脉，东临蜀河，南接汉江，以远处500米处的太阳包峰为朝山，各居民院的中轴线均以后山为轴线，如自然生长一般，各院住宅稍作调整，形成认同的最佳景观环境。古镇以红岩碥为基础，沿山脚下向东发展，蜀河从北向南穿过蜀河镇，整个古镇呈现出带形布局。

明末清初，四方客商纷纷在此定居经商，建商号、开当铺、设钱庄，多达百余家。为利于聚会和联系，各地客商在此建馆结社，根据不同信仰、不同地域建成了众多会馆、寺庙。至今，古镇仍保持了不同时期、不同地域的建筑风格，成为汉江文化的表现形式和缩影。

如明清时期的黄州帮黄州馆、船帮杨泗馆、回帮清真寺、陕帮三义庙、江西帮万寿宫、八帮火神庙、武昌帮会馆等古建筑。古镇内的家巷、乾益巷、永安巷、沈家楼等古街小巷幽深静谧，而厘金局、电报局、当铺、钱庄、王公馆和古民居等亦别具风韵。

— ENGLISH —

Shuhe Ancient Town in Xunyang County is located 53 km east of Taiji City, Xunyang County, Ankang City, connecting Hunan and Hubei to the east, reaching Sichuan and Chongqing to the south, leading to Longhai to the west, and reaching Guanzhong to the north.

It is an important transit distribution center and commercial town for the logistics intersection of Hubei, Shaanxi, and Sichuan. Shuhe Ancient Town is 160 m wide east-west and 735 m long north-south, covering an area of about 0.8 square kilometers.

Due to its profound cultural heritage and many historic sites, it has long been known as 'Little Hankou.' Shuhe has a long history; when King Wu of Zhou used vassals to screen the Zhou Dynasty, the State of Shu was enfeoffed north of Nanyang, Henan.

The poor and weak State of Shu, pressed by the expansion of Chu State, led its people to migrate westward and once stayed here, hence the name.

In the Shu Han period, a county was established; it revived in the Ming dynasty and flourished in the mid-to-late Qing dynasty.

The overall layout of Shuhe Ancient Town is a reflection model of the commercial transportation and site-selection residence consciousness of traditional Chinese feudal society. Due to the prosperous development of commerce, Shuhe Ancient Town presents a naturally open layout characteristic.

From the initial site selection in the Western Jin Dynasty to the final formation of Shuhe Ancient Town's layout, people continuously selected the best landscapes, and in the construction of the ancient town, continuously transformed and maintained the environment, improving the town layout.

Shuhe Ancient Town takes Hongyanbian (back mountain) as the dragon vein, borders Shuhe River to the east, connects to the Han River to the south, and takes the Sunbaofeng Peak 500 m away as the facing mountain; the central axes of each residential courtyard take the back mountain as the axis, like natural growth, with each courtyard residence slightly adjusted to form a recognized best landscape environment.

The ancient town takes Hongyanbian as the base, developing eastward along the foot of the mountain; Shuhe River passes through Shuhe Town from north to south, and the entire ancient town presents a belt-shaped layout.

In the late Ming and early Qing, merchants from all directions settled here to do business, establishing trading firms, pawnshops, and money shops, as many as over a hundred.

To facilitate gatherings and connections, merchants from various regions built guild halls and associations here, constructing numerous guild halls and temples according to different beliefs and regions.

To this day, the ancient town still maintains architectural styles of different periods and regions, becoming an expression form and microcosm of Han River culture.

For example, ancient buildings from the Ming and Qing periods such as the Huangzhou Gang Huangzhou guild hall, Boat Gang Yangsi Hall, Hui Gang mosque, Shaanxi Gang Sanyi Temple, Jiangxi Gang Wanshou Palace, Eight Gang Huoshen Temple, and Wuchang Gang guild hall.

The ancient streets and lanes inside the town, such as Jia Lane, Qianyi Lane, Yong'an Lane, and Shenjia Building, are deep and quiet; while the Lijin Bureau (tax bureau), Telegraph Office, pawnshop, money shop, Wang Mansion, and ancient residences also have unique charm.
