第四章 宗教建筑

Chapter 4 Religious Architecture

34. 西安大慈恩寺

Xi'an Daci'en Temple
唐、明 佛教寺庙 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市雁塔区雁塔南路北口

西安大慈恩寺位于西安市雁塔区雁塔南路北口，是汉传佛教法相宗的祖庭，唐长安三大译经场之一，也是唐长安城最宏伟壮丽的皇家寺院代表。1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位。

2014年，寺内的大雁塔入选《世界遗产名录》。唐太宗贞观二十二年（公元648年），太子李治为文德皇后祈求冥福，在晋昌坊无漏寺（建于开皇九年）故址营建此寺，重建后改名大慈恩寺。

当时有13个院落，房舍1897间。明成化二年（1466年）起在原寺院西塔院基础上陆续修建，逐步形成今日寺院格局。

大慈恩寺坐北朝南，其南北长约300米，东西宽约150米，全寺总占地面积约为45000平方米。自南向北在中轴线上坐落有山门、大雄宝殿、佛殿、大雁塔、玄奘院。

在山门西北与东北侧坐落着钟鼓二楼，钟鼓楼北侧东西分别坐落着客堂和云水堂，它们与中轴线南北方向的山门以及大雄宝殿构成了寺院的第一进院落，此院南北长（山门之北与大雄宝殿台基南缘的距离）约90米，东西宽（客堂之东与云水堂之西的距离）约36米，为全寺最大的院落。

在大雄宝殿两侧建有面宽较长的东西配殿，东西配殿与南面的大雄宝殿以及北面的在建佛殿共同构成寺院中轴线上的第二进院落，该院落南北长（大雄宝殿之北与在建佛殿之南的距离）约17米，东西宽（两配殿的间距）约33米，其规模小于第一进院落。寺院的功能区主要集中于西侧以及东南，西侧自南向北分别分布着管理院、僧人院以及方丈院三组院落。

东南方向自南向北分别为公共卫生设施建筑以及佛教艺术品展室。山门：面阔三间，坐北朝南，砖木结构，硬山双坡屋顶，布板瓦。脊高7.89米，面宽11.35米，进深9.49米，建筑面积107.71平方米。

门内三间原为金刚殿，后将中间改为通道，东西为传达、接待用房。两侧现建有供游人出入的侧门各一间。门外当心间上方额题大慈恩寺。大雄宝殿：始建于明成化二年（1466年），历代均有维修。

2006年实施重建，2009年春季落成。

整修后的建筑面阔五间，砖木结构，布筒瓦，大殿高9米，宽23.5米，进深12.5米，建筑面积293平方米，为一层歇山式建筑。门楣题匾大雄宝殿。

玄奘三藏院：2000年新建，由3个并列布局的对称式院落、19座仿唐风格建筑物构成。其梁柱、斗拱为钢筋水泥构造，柱础为莲花座，屋面均为宜兴造琉璃瓦，风格高大宽敞。

其中，大遍觉堂居中，庑殿式，脊高13.32米，两层，各五间，地面部分面宽23.4米，进深17米，地下部分面宽22.46米，进深16.06米。大殿台基高1.3米，高于东西两殿。

东、西大殿分别为译经堂和求法堂，歇山式屋面，前抱厦门廊，脊高10.29米，两层各五间，地面部分面宽20.8米，进深14.8米，地下部分面宽19.92米，进深15.92米，台基高于地面61厘米。

东院东侧为东展厅，西院西侧为接待室，悬山式屋面，脊高8.02米，各一层七间，面宽26.3米，进深10.4米，台基高65厘米。三院之间有东西二门相通，悬山式屋面，脊高6.94米。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Daci'en Temple is located at the north entrance of Yanta South Road, Yanta District, Xi'an.

It is the ancestral temple of the Faxiang (Dharma Characteristics) School of Han Chinese Buddhism, one of the three major translation centers in Tang Chang'an, and also the most magnificent representative of royal temples in Tang Chang'an.

It was designated as one of the first batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1961. In 2014, the Giant Wild Goose Pagoda inside the temple was inscribed on the World Heritage List.

In the 22nd year of Zhenguan of Emperor Taizong of Tang (648 AD), Crown Prince Li Zhi, to pray for the posthumous blessings of Empress Wende, built this temple on the former site of Wulou Temple (built in the 9th year of Kaihuang) in Jinchang Ward, and renamed it Daci'en Temple after reconstruction.

At that time, there were 13 courtyards with 1,897 rooms. From the 2nd year of Chenghua, Ming (1466), it was gradually built on the basis of the original West Pagoda Courtyard,逐步 forming today's temple layout.

Daci'en Temple faces south, with a north-south length of about 300 m and an east-west width of about 150 m, with a total area of about 45,000 square meters.

From south to north on the central axis are located the Mountain Gate, Mahavira Hall, Buddha Hall, Giant Wild Goose Pagoda, and Xuanzang Courtyard.

Northwest and northeast of the Mountain Gate are the Bell and drum towers; north of the Bell and drum towers, east and west are respectively the Guest Hall and Yunshui Hall; together with the Mountain Gate and Mahavira Hall along the north-south central axis, they form the first courtyard of the temple, which is about 90 m long north-south (distance from north of Mountain Gate to south edge of Mahavira Hall platform) and about 36 m wide east-west (distance from east of Guest Hall to west of Yunshui Hall), the largest courtyard in the temple.

On both sides of the Mahavira Hall are built east and west side halls with longer widths; the east and west side halls, together with the Mahavira Hall to the south and the under-construction Buddha Hall to the north, form the second courtyard on the temple's central axis, which is about 17 m long north-south (distance from north of Mahavira Hall to south of under-construction Buddha Hall) and about 33 m wide east-west (distance between the two side halls), smaller than the first courtyard.

The functional areas of the temple are mainly concentrated on the west side and southeast; on the west side, from south to north, are respectively distributed the Management Courtyard, Monk Courtyard, and Abbot Courtyard, three groups of courtyards.

In the southeast direction, from south to north, are respectively public health facility buildings and Buddhist art exhibition rooms. Mountain Gate: three bays wide, facing south, brick-wood structure, hip-and-gable double-slope roof, with flat tiles.

Ridge height 7.89 m, width 11.35 m, depth 9.49 m, building area 107.71 square meters. The three rooms inside were originally the Vajra Hall; later the middle was changed to a passage, with the east and west serving as communication and reception rooms.

On both sides, side gates for visitors to enter and exit are now built, one each. Above the central bay outside the gate, the plaque reads 'Daci'en Temple.' Mahavira Hall: first built in the 2nd year of Chenghua, Ming (1466), repaired in successive dynasties.

Reconstruction was implemented in 2006 and completed in spring 2009.

The renovated building has five bays in width, brick-wood structure, with semi-cylindrical tiles; the hall is 9 m high, 23.5 m wide, 12.5 m deep, with a building area of 293 square meters, a one-story hip-and-gable building.

The lintel plaque reads 'Mahavira Hall.' Xuanzang Sanzang Courtyard: newly built in 2000, consisting of 3 symmetrically arranged parallel courtyards and 19 Tang-style buildings.

Its beams, columns, and dougong are of reinforced concrete construction, with lotus-shaped column bases, and the roofs are all made of Yixing glazed tiles, with a tall and spacious style.

Among them, the Dabianjue Hall is in the center, in the hip-roof (wudian) style, with a ridge height of 13.32 m, two stories, five bays each; the above-ground part is 23.4 m wide and 17 m deep, and the underground part is 22.46 m wide and 16.06 m deep.

The hall platform is 1.3 m high, higher than the east and west halls.

The east and west halls are respectively the Sutra Translation Hall and Dharma Seeking Hall, with hip-and-gable roofs and front porticoes, ridge height 10.29 m, two stories of five bays each; the above-ground part is 20.8 m wide and 14.8 m deep, and the underground part is 19.92 m wide and 15.92 m deep, with the platform 61 cm above ground.

The east side of the east courtyard is the East Exhibition Hall, and the west side of the west courtyard is the Reception Room, with overhanging gable roofs, ridge height 8.02 m, one story of seven bays each, 26.3 m wide and 10.4 m deep, with the platform 65 cm high.

Between the three courtyards, there are east and west gates connecting them, with overhanging gable roofs and ridge height 6.94 m.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 汉传佛教法相宗的祖庭
  • 唐长安三大译经场之一
  • 唐长安城最宏伟壮丽的皇家寺院代表
  • 1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 2014年寺内的大雁塔入选世界遗产名录
  • 唐太宗贞观二十二年为文德皇后祈求冥福
  • 在晋昌坊无漏寺故址营建此寺
  • 重建后改名大慈恩寺
  • 当时有13个院落
  • 房舍1897间
  • 明成化二年起在原寺院西塔院基础上陆续修建
  • 大慈恩寺坐北朝南
  • 南北长约300米
  • 东西宽约150米
  • 全寺总占地面积约为45000平方米

35. 长安护国兴教寺

Chang'an Huguo Xingjiao Temple
唐 佛教寺庙 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市长安区樊川北塬少陵塬

长安护国兴教寺位于西安市长安区樊川北塬（少陵塬），为唐代樊川八大寺院之首。1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位。

1983年兴教寺公布为汉族地区全国重点寺院。寺院始建于唐高宗总章二年（公元669年），初为从白鹿原迁葬玄奘法师遗骨，在此建五层砖塔，随即建寺。唐肃宗题塔额曰兴教，寺遂得名。

后玄奘两名高徒窥基、圆测分别于唐永淳元年（公元682年）、唐登封元年（公元696年）葬于塔两侧。建寺约百年之后，即塔无主，寺无僧。唐文宗太和二年（公元828年），重修塔身。

清同治年间（1862~1874年）遭兵灾，除三座舍利塔外，全寺付之一炬，几成废墟。民国年间，寺僧募资修缮大殿、僧房十余间，增建及修葺塔亭、大殿、藏经楼、山门等。

1982年以来，又进行了全面修缮、增建。兴教寺坐北朝南，现由正院、东跨院和西跨院三部分组成。正院由山门、钟鼓楼、大殿、法堂、禅堂等形成中轴线。山门内钟鼓两楼夹道对峙。

大殿正对山门，殿内供奉明代铜佛像和缅甸赠送的白玉石刻弥勒佛像各一尊，并有彩色宗教故事画。东跨院部分以藏经楼为主要建筑，为二层楼：一层陈列有关玄奘大师的画像和书画；二层珍藏《藏经》《大藏经》等数千册经书及用巴利文写的《贝叶经》残片。西跨院又称慈恩塔院，为玄奘及其弟子圆测和窥基遗骨安葬之地。院内建有并列的三座舍利塔。

玄奘法师舍利塔（详见第八章）为现存年代最早的唐代楼阁式塔，又称大遍觉塔，塔体为砖砌仿木结构，共五层，底层拱洞内有玄奘法师塑像。玄奘法师墓塔左右两侧各有一座三层塔，为其弟子窥基大师圆寂塔和西明寺大师圆测舍利塔。

两位弟子的灵塔平面皆为方形，塔身三层，高约7米，塔体为实心砖结构。三座塔之北的慈恩殿内陈列玄奘及其弟子的石刻像等。

— ENGLISH —

Chang'an Huguo Xingjiao Temple is located on Shaoling Plateau (north plateau of Fanchuan), Chang'an District, Xi'an, and was the foremost of the eight major temples in Fanchuan during the Tang dynasty.

It was designated as one of the first batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1961. In 1983, Xingjiao Temple was designated as a national key temple in Han Chinese areas.

The temple was first built in the 2nd year of Zongzhang, Emperor Gaozong of Tang (669 AD), initially to relocate the remains of Master Xuanzang from Bailuyuan, building a five-story brick pagoda here, and then building the temple.

Emperor Suzong of Tang inscribed the pagoda plaque 'Xingjiao' (Prospering Teaching), hence the temple name. Later, Xuanzang's two eminent disciples Kuiji and Yuance were buried on both sides of the pagoda respectively in the 1st year of Yongchun (682 AD) and the 1st year of Dengfeng (696 AD).

About a hundred years after the temple was built, the pagoda had no master and the temple had no monks. In the 2nd year of Taihe, Emperor Wenzong of Tang (828 AD), the pagoda body was rebuilt.

During the Tongzhi period of Qing (1862-1874), it suffered from war disaster; except for the three relic pagodas, the entire temple was burned down, almost in ruins.

During the Republic of China, temple monks raised funds to repair the main hall and over ten monk rooms, and added and repaired pagoda pavilions, main hall, scripture library, and mountain gate. Since 1982, comprehensive repairs and additions have been carried out.

Xingjiao Temple faces south and currently consists of three parts: the main courtyard, east annex courtyard, and west annex courtyard. The main courtyard forms a central axis with the mountain gate, bell and drum towers, main hall, dharma hall, and meditation hall.

Inside the mountain gate, the bell and drum towers face each other across the path.

The main hall faces the mountain gate; inside the hall are enshrined a Ming dynasty bronze Buddha statue and a white jade stone Maitreya Buddha statue presented by Myanmar, one each, with colorful religious story paintings.

The east annex courtyard mainly features the scripture library as its main building, a two-story building: the first floor displays portraits and calligraphy/paintings related to Master Xuanzang; the second floor珍藏 thousands of scripture books such as the 'Tripitaka' and 'Mahaprajnaparamita Sutra,' as well as fragments of the 'Pattra Leaf Sutra' written in Pali.

The west annex courtyard, also called the Ci'en Pagoda Courtyard, is the burial place of Xuanzang and his disciples Yuance and Kuiji. Three parallel relic pagodas are built in the courtyard.

The relic pagoda of Master Xuanzang (see Chapter 8 for details) is the earliest existing Tang dynasty pavilion-style pagoda, also called the Dabianjue Pagoda; the pagoda body is brick-built imitating wooden structure, with five stories, and there is a statue of Master Xuanzang in the ground-floor arch cave.

On the left and right sides of Xuanzang's tomb pagoda are each a three-story pagoda, the relic pagoda of his disciple Master Kuiji and the relic pagoda of Master Yuance of Ximing Temple.

The spirit pagodas of both disciples are square in plane, with three-story pagoda bodies, about 7 m high, and solid brick structures. In the Ci'en Hall north of the three pagodas, stone carvings of Xuanzang and his disciples are displayed.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 唐代樊川八大寺院之首
  • 1961年公布为第一批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 1983年兴教寺公布为汉族地区全国重点寺院
  • 寺院始建于唐高宗总章二年
  • 初为从白鹿原迁葬玄奘法师遗骨
  • 在此建五层砖塔
  • 随即建寺
  • 唐肃宗题塔额曰兴教
  • 寺遂得名
  • 后玄奘两名高徒窥基、圆测分别于唐永淳元年、唐登封元年葬于塔两侧
  • 建寺约百年之后即塔无主寺无僧
  • 唐文宗太和二年遭兵灾
  • 除三座舍利塔外全寺付之一炬
  • 民国年间寺僧募资修缮

36. 西安大兴善寺

Xi'an Daxingshan Temple
晋、隋、唐 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安市兴善寺西街

西安大兴善寺位于西安市兴善寺西街，是汉地佛教密宗祖庭，唐代长安三大译经场之一。1956年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位，1983年被国务院列为全国重点开放寺院之一。

大兴善寺始建于晋武帝太康二年（公元266年），初曰遵善寺。隋开皇二年（公元582年）重修此寺，更名为大兴善寺，为当时京城第一大寺。该寺始建即为国寺，安置于新都大兴城的九五贵位，寺院占城内靖善坊一坊之地，取城名大兴、坊名善字，赐名大兴善寺。

唐神龙年间（公元705~706年），改为酆国寺，景云元年（公元710~711年）复名。

唐武宗会昌年间（公元841~846年），大举灭佛，大兴善寺被拆毁。

以后屡有重修，以清康熙年间（1662~1722年）的修复工程最多，先后重修了山门、方丈殿、钟楼、鼓楼等。清同治年间，寺院建筑再次被毁，仅存钟、鼓楼和前门。

1955年，全面大修。大兴善寺是目前西安地区占地面积最大的寺院，占地面积约8公顷。其南北长330米，东西宽219米。自南向北，沿中轴线方向依次分布着四组大型院落。

第一进院，由轴线南端的山门，北部的天王殿以及东、西仿古商业店面构成。第二进院，由南部的天王殿，北部的大雄宝殿，以及自南向北东西对峙的钟鼓楼和仿唐式配殿共同围合构成，该院落尺度为中轴线上四组院落之最。

第三进院，由南往北方向分别为大雄宝殿和观音殿，它们与东西对峙的禅堂、文殊殿、普贤殿共同构成一组院落，其中的唐代转轮藏经殿的殿基位于这组院落的中心偏北方向。第四进院，南北分别为观音殿、法堂，这两殿之间的东西两侧亦对称布置着一系列的配殿，西侧自南向北为众香寮和卧佛殿，东侧自南向北为花雨轩和玉佛殿。上述四组院落构成了大兴善寺今日格局的主体。

并以第二进院落构成寺院的核心区，此院落在南北方向上的距离自天王殿之北到大雄宝殿之南总长约为70米，东西长度也达到了50米，其空间尺度大大超过其余三组院落，除此之外在大兴善寺北端最后一进院落的紧东边建有金刚殿。大雄宝殿为寺中规模最大的一座建筑，其制度等级与帝王太庙的制度等级一致。

面阔七间，进深两间加前廊，重檐歇山顶，外檐出三踩单昂斗拱。天王殿，面阔五间，单檐硬山顶，外檐不施斗拱。菩萨殿，面阔五间，单檐硬山顶，外檐不施斗拱，形制有如大雄宝殿，东西两侧配有砖砌碑亭。

鼓楼，砖结构，高二层，单檐歇山顶，内置大鼓一面。普同塔，平面六角形，高五层，约18米，砖结构，坐落在六角形的砖砌台基之上。该塔系1990年由附近的小寨西路迁建于此。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Daxingshan Temple is located on Xingshan Temple West Street, Xi'an. It is the ancestral temple of the Esoteric (Mizong) School of Han Chinese Buddhism and one of the three major translation centers in Tang Chang'an.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1956 and listed by the State Council as one of the national key open temples in 1983. Daxingshan Temple was first built in the 2nd year of Taikang, Emperor Wu of Jin (266 AD), initially called Zunshan Temple.

In the 2nd year of Kaihuang, Sui (582 AD), the temple was rebuilt and renamed Daxingshan Temple, becoming the largest temple in the capital at that time.

The temple was built as a state temple from the beginning, placed in the noble 'nine-five' position of the new capital Daxing City; the temple occupied one ward (Jingshan Ward) in the city, taking the city name 'Daxing' and the ward name 'Shan' (good), bestowing the name Daxingshan Temple.

During the Shenlong period of Tang (705-706 AD), it was changed to Fengguo Temple; in the 1st year of Jingyun (710-711 AD), the original name was restored.

During the Huichang period of Emperor Wuzong of Tang (841-846 AD), a large-scale Buddhist persecution occurred, and Daxingshan Temple was demolished.

It was repeatedly rebuilt thereafter, with the most restoration work during the Kangxi period of Qing (1662-1722), successively rebuilding the mountain gate, abbot hall, bell tower, and drum tower.

During the Tongzhi period of Qing, the temple buildings were destroyed again, leaving only the bell and drum towers and front gate. In 1955, a comprehensive major repair was carried out.

Daxingshan Temple is currently the temple with the largest占地面积 in the Xi'an area, covering about 8 hectares. It is 330 m long north-south and 219 m wide east-west. From south to north, along the central axis, four groups of large courtyards are distributed in sequence.

The first courtyard consists of the mountain gate at the southern end of the axis, the Heavenly King Hall to the north, and east and west antique-style commercial storefronts.

The second courtyard is enclosed by the Heavenly King Hall to the south, the Mahavira Hall to the north, and the bell and drum towers and Tang-style side halls facing each other east-west from south to north; this courtyard has the largest scale among the four groups of courtyards on the central axis.

The third courtyard, from south to north, is respectively the Mahavira Hall and Guanyin Hall; together with the meditation hall, Manjusri Hall, and Samantabhadra Hall facing each other east-west, they form a group of courtyards, among which the hall base of the Tang dynasty revolving scripture hall is located slightly north of the center of this group of courtyards.

The fourth courtyard has the Guanyin Hall and Dharma Hall to the north and south respectively; between these two halls, a series of side halls are also symmetrically arranged on the east and west sides; on the west side, from south to north, are Zhongxiang Liao and Reclining Buddha Hall; on the east side, from south to north, are Huayu Xuan and Jade Buddha Hall.

The above four groups of courtyards constitute the main body of today's Daxingshan Temple layout.

The second courtyard forms the core area of the temple; the distance in the north-south direction of this courtyard, from north of the Heavenly King Hall to south of the Mahavira Hall, totals about 70 m, and the east-west length also reaches 50 m; its spatial scale greatly exceeds the other three groups of courtyards.

In addition,紧 east of the last courtyard at the northern end of Daxingshan Temple, the Vajra Hall is built. The Mahavira Hall is the largest building in the temple, and its institutional grade is consistent with that of the imperial ancestral temple.

It has seven bays in width, two bays in depth plus a front corridor, double-eave hip-and-gable roof, with three-cai single ang dougong on the outer eaves.

The Heavenly King Hall has five bays in width, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with no dougong on the outer eaves.

The Bodhisattva Hall has five bays in width, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with no dougong on the outer eaves, similar in form to the Mahavira Hall, with brick stele pavilions on both east and west sides.

The drum tower is of brick structure, two stories high, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with a large drum inside. The Putong Pagoda has a hexagonal plane, five stories high, about 18 m, of brick structure, sitting on a hexagonal brick platform base.

This pagoda was relocated here from nearby Xiaozhai West Road in 1990.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 汉地佛教密宗祖庭
  • 唐代长安三大译经场之一
  • 1956年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 1983年被国务院列为全国重点开放寺院之一
  • 大兴善寺始建于晋武帝太康二年
  • 初曰遵善寺
  • 隋开皇二年重修此寺
  • 更名为大兴善寺
  • 为当时京城第一大寺
  • 该寺始建即为国寺
  • 安置于新都大兴城的九五贵位
  • 寺院占城内靖善坊一坊之地
  • 取城名大兴
  • 坊名善
  • 字赐名大兴善寺

37. 西安香积寺

Xi'an Xiangji Temple
唐 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安市长安区郭杜乡香积寺村

香积寺位于西安市长安区郭杜乡香积寺村，原名光明寺，是汉地佛教净土宗之祖庭。1956年列为陕西省重点文物保护单位，也是国务院确定的佛教地区全国重点寺院之一。

寺内的善导塔于2001年公布为第五批国家级文物保护单位。寺院始建于唐高宗永隆二年（公元681年）。

净土宗创始人之一善导大师圆寂，弟子怀恽为纪念善导功德，修建了香积寺和善导大师供养塔。安史之乱和唐武宗灭佛事件中，香积寺遭到严重破坏。直到宋朝时，又恢复了原名。宋、元期间，长安衰落，寺院年久失修，到明嘉靖年间才进行了大规模的修复。清朝时，香积寺仍保持明朝的规模，并进行了修葺。

直到清末，寺内还保存有许多金石文物，仅历代雕刻就有119件。20世纪50年代初，寺内主要建筑仅有大殿三间、金刚殿三间以及善导、净业二塔，金石文物几乎荡然无存。

1979年，政府为兴建香积寺，拆掉长安县（今西安市长安区）子午镇（今西安市长安区子午镇）的古戏楼，修建大雄宝殿五间，并加固善导古塔。之后相继新建法堂五间，廊房十间，僧房、库房百余间，重建了寺庙围墙和院内通道。

唐香积寺的建筑已毁，现今香积寺由南向北依次为门前牌坊、山门、天王殿、大雄宝殿、佛堂，左右两侧分别有钟楼、鼓楼、善导塔和客堂等建筑。寺内建筑共有大殿五间、佛堂五间、僧房十间等。

寺内遗存唐代两座古塔，其中最大的一座就是为纪念善导大师而建立的，故名善导塔（详见第八章）。另一座塔为善导的门徒净业和尚的灵塔。

— ENGLISH —

Xiangji Temple is located in Xiangjisi Village, Guodu Township, Chang'an District, Xi'an, originally named Guangming Temple. It is the ancestral temple of the Pure Land (Jingtu) School of Han Chinese Buddhism.

It was listed as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1956 and is also one of the national key temples in Buddhist areas designated by the State Council.

The Shandao Pagoda inside the temple was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001. The temple was first built in the 2nd year of Yonglong, Emperor Gaozong of Tang (681 AD).

Master Shandao, one of the founders of the Pure Land School, passed away; his disciple Huaiyun, to commemorate Shandao's merits, built Xiangji Temple and the Shandao Master offering pagoda.

During the An Lushan Rebellion and the Buddhist persecution of Emperor Wuzong of Tang, Xiangji Temple suffered severe damage. It was not until the Song dynasty that its original name was restored.

During the Song and Yuan periods, Chang'an declined and the temple fell into disrepair; it was not until the Jiajing period of Ming that large-scale restoration was carried out.

During the Qing dynasty, Xiangji Temple still maintained the scale of the Ming dynasty and underwent repairs. Until the late Qing, the temple still preserved many gold and stone cultural relics, with 119 carvings from successive dynasties alone.

In the early 1950s, the main buildings in the temple were only three bays of the main hall, three bays of the Vajra Hall, and the two pagodas of Shandao and Jingye, with gold and stone cultural relics almost completely gone.

In 1979, to build Xiangji Temple, the government demolished the ancient opera tower of Ziwu Town, Chang'an County (now Ziwu Town, Chang'an District, Xi'an), built five bays of the Mahavira Hall, and reinforced the ancient Shandao Pagoda.

Subsequently, five bays of the Dharma Hall, ten bays of corridor rooms, over a hundred monk rooms and storehouses were successively built, and the temple walls and院内 passages were rebuilt.

The buildings of Tang Xiangji Temple have been destroyed; today's Xiangji Temple, from south to north, is依次 the front archway, mountain gate, Heavenly King Hall, Mahavira Hall, and Buddha Hall, with the bell tower, drum tower, Shandao Pagoda, and Guest Hall on the left and right sides respectively.

The temple buildings include five bays of the main hall, five bays of the Buddha Hall, ten bays of monk rooms, etc. Two ancient Tang pagodas remain in the temple; the largest one was built to commemorate Master Shandao, hence named Shandao Pagoda (see Chapter 8 for details).

The other pagoda is the spirit pagoda of Monk Jingye, Shandao's disciple.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 原名光明寺
  • 汉地佛教净土宗之祖庭
  • 1956年列为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 也是国务院确定的佛教地区全国重点寺院之一
  • 寺内的善导塔于2001年公布为第五批国家级文物保护单位
  • 寺院始建于唐高宗永隆二年
  • 净土宗创始人之一善导大师圆寂
  • 弟子怀恽为纪念善导功德
  • 修建了香积寺和善导大师供养塔
  • 安史之乱和唐武宗灭佛事件中香积寺遭到严重破坏
  • 直到宋朝时又恢复了原名
  • 宋元期间长安衰落寺院年久失修
  • 到明嘉靖年间才进行了大规模的修复
  • 清朝时香积寺仍保持明朝的规模并进行了修葺
  • 直到清末寺内还保存有许多金石文物

38. 韩城普照寺

Hancheng Puzhao Temple
元 佛教寺庙 全国重点文物保护单位 韩城市昝村镇吴村

韩城普照寺位于韩城市昝村镇吴村，是韩城元代佛寺的标志性建筑，佛教文化气息浓厚。2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位。

普照寺始建于元延祐三年（1316年），据寺内保存下来的《金桩神像并理观音洞碑记》和《重修普照寺大佛殿碑记》记载，清康熙十六年（1677年）、三十六年（1697年），清道光三年（1823年）曾多次对该寺进行过较大规模的维修。

普照寺建于砖砌的高台上，北靠古寨，面南而居，居高临下，颇有虎踞高山之势。寺院原占地面积4453平方米，后陆续迁建韩城境内濒危的元代建筑十座，与原有殿堂一起形成颇具规模的古建筑群，但其各自依旧保持原状格局与特征，被誉为元代建筑博物馆，寺院面积也扩大至30余亩。

现保存下来的建筑有大佛殿、土地庙、关公庙、伽蓝殿、护法庙、观音洞等。大佛殿：元代典型建筑，面阔五间，21米；进深三间六椽，14米，建筑面积约294平方米。

该殿为单檐歇山顶，灰布筒板瓦屋面，前檐当心间设双开板门，次间在墙的上半部设直棂高窗，梢间不开窗或门。殿身山墙的厚度将近1米，且收分比较明显。大殿在大木结构做法上，继承了宋代以来所形成的传统制度，在斗拱、柱额、举折方面具有宋元时期建筑的特征。檐下一周置有斗拱，设有柱头铺作和转角铺作，无补间铺作。

前檐及两际斗拱式样均为五铺作双下昂，里转出双抄；后檐斗拱式样为五铺作，里外出双抄。另外前檐仅当心间的栌斗为圆形，其余则为方形，昂身做成琴面。厢拱上承托替木，替木之上为挑檐檩。转角铺作中设有一种特殊的斗拱——鸳鸯交手拱。

殿内保存有塑于元泰定三年（1326年）的释迦牟尼佛和文殊、普贤二菩萨，阿难、伽叶二弟子五尊彩塑像及明清藻井绘画130余幅。大佛殿左右为土地庙与关公庙，其面阔、进深各一间，硬山顶，灰瓦屋面。

殿与庙之间为拱洞，殿前东西两侧为伽蓝殿和护法庙，面阔均为三间，进深均为一间，硬山顶，灰瓦屋面。大殿后面是观音洞。大佛殿前东、西两侧各建有青砖碑楼，均为硬山仿木构建筑。

— ENGLISH —

Hancheng Puzhao Temple is located in Wucun, Zancun Town, Hancheng City. It is the landmark building of Yuan Dynasty Buddhist temples in Hancheng, with a strong Buddhist cultural atmosphere.

It was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001. Puzhao Temple was first built in the 3rd year of Yanyou, Yuan (1316).

According to the 'Stele Record of Gold-plated Deity Statues and Management of Guanyin Cave' and 'Stele Record of Rebuilding the Great Buddha Hall of Puzhao Temple' preserved in the temple, large-scale repairs were carried out multiple times in the 16th year (1677) and 36th year (1697) of Kangxi, Qing, and the 3rd year of Daoguang, Qing (1823).

Puzhao Temple is built on a high brick platform, backed by an ancient fortress to the north, facing south, commanding a high position, with a momentum like a tiger perched on a high mountain.

The temple originally covered 4,453 square meters; later, ten endangered Yuan Dynasty buildings in Hancheng were successively relocated here, forming a considerable ancient building complex together with the original halls, but each still maintains its original layout and characteristics, known as the 'Yuan Dynasty Architecture Museum,' and the temple area expanded to over 30 mu.

The currently preserved buildings include the Great Buddha Hall, Earth God Temple, Guangong Temple, Qielan Hall, Dharma Protection Temple, Guanyin Cave, etc.

Great Buddha Hall: a typical Yuan Dynasty building, five bays wide (21 m), three bays deep with six rafters (14 m), with a building area of about 294 square meters.

This hall has a single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with gray semi-cylindrical and flat tile roofing; the front eave central bay has double-leaf plank doors, the secondary bays have straight-lattice high windows in the upper half of the wall, and the end bays have no windows or doors.

The gable walls of the hall body are nearly 1 m thick, with relatively obvious entasis.

In the large wooden structure construction method, the hall inherits the traditional system formed since the Song dynasty, with architectural features of the Song-Yuan period in dougong, column lintels, and roof raising.

A ring of dougong is placed under the eaves, with column-head bracket sets and corner bracket sets, but no intermediate bracket sets.

The dougong style on the front eaves and both gable ends are all five-puzuo double xia'ang, with interior turning out double chao; the rear eave dougong style is five-puzuo, with interior and exterior double chao.

In addition, only the lu-dou (block) of the central bay on the front eave is circular, the rest are square, and the ang body is made into a qin-mian (lute-face) shape. The xiang-gong (arm bracket) supports the timu (sub-rafter), above which is the tiaoyan purlin.

A special type of dougong - yuanyang jiaoshou gong (mandarin duck hand-crossing bracket) - is set in the corner bracket set.

Inside the hall are preserved five colored clay statues of Sakyamuni Buddha, Manjusri and Samantabhadra Bodhisattvas, and the two disciples Ananda and Mahakasyapa, molded in the 3rd year of Taiding, Yuan (1326), as well as over 130 Ming-Qing caisson paintings.

To the left and right of the Great Buddha Hall are the Earth God Temple and Guangong Temple, each one bay in width and depth, with hip-and-gable roofs and gray tile roofing.

Between the hall and temples are arch tunnels; on the east and west sides in front of the hall are the Qielan Hall and Dharma Protection Temple, each three bays wide and one bay deep, with hip-and-gable roofs and gray tile roofing. Behind the main hall is the Guanyin Cave.

On the east and west sides in front of the Great Buddha Hall, blue brick stele towers are built, each of hip-and-gable imitation wooden structure.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 韩城元代佛寺的标志性建筑
  • 佛教文化气息浓厚
  • 2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 普照寺始建于元延祐三年
  • 据寺内保存下来的金桩神像并理观音洞碑记和重修普照寺大佛殿碑记记载
  • 清康熙十六年、三十六年
  • 清道光三年曾多次对该寺进行过较大规模的维修
  • 普照寺建于砖砌的高台上
  • 北靠古寨
  • 面南而居
  • 居高临下
  • 颇有虎踞高山之势
  • 寺院原占地面积4453平方米
  • 后陆续迁建韩城境内濒危的元代建筑十座
  • 与原有殿堂一起形成颇具规模的古建筑群

39. 洋县智果寺

Yang County Zhiguo Temple
唐、宋、元、明、清 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 汉中市洋县谢村镇

洋县智果寺位于汉中市洋县谢村镇，是一组规模宽敞、形制完备的敕建寺院，也是陕西唯一的皇家城堡式寺院，素有汉王名刹之称。1962年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

据《洋县志》载：智果寺始建于唐仪凤年间（公元676~679年），宋、元皆重修。

清雍正二年（1724年）《重修智果院六次序》碑记：嗣后万历十四年（1586年）颁施藏经，赐建藏经楼。其规模逐为宏阔。

藏经楼现存碑碣16通，楼前竖有明万历十六年（1588年）圣谕碑，楼内存有明神宗朱翊钧之母慈圣宣文明肃皇太后御赐经卷678函、6780卷，为全国佛经珍品。目前，藏经楼所藏经典按千字文计为4178卷，经书长35厘米，宽13厘米。

其所经年代之久、参与译著人数之多和囊括内容之丰富，实属罕见。藏经楼为寺内主体建筑，砖木结构，二层，重檐歇山顶，灰布筒瓦，前后各有四根较粗檐柱，楼内有四根明柱，东西两侧檐柱各七。楼高15米，顶部屋脊六兽仰首，心间的部分向外挑出平座，以加重立面中心构图。

— ENGLISH —

Yang County Zhiguo Temple is located in Xiecun Town, Yang County, Hanzhong City.

It is a group of imperially-built temples with spacious scale and complete form, and also the only royal castle-style temple in Shaanxi, known as the 'Famous Monastery of the Han King.' It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1962.

According to the 'Yang County Chronicle': Zhiguo Temple was first built during the Yifeng period of Tang (676-679 AD), and was rebuilt in both Song and Yuan dynasties.

The 'Stele Record of the Sixth Sequence of Rebuilding Zhiguo Courtyard' in the 2nd year of Yongzheng, Qing (1724) records: thereafter, in the 14th year of Wanli (1586), scriptures were bestowed and the Scripture Library was ordered to be built. Its scale gradually became vast.

The Scripture Library currently preserves 16 steles; in front of the library stands the Shengyu (Imperial Edict) Stele of the 16th year of Wanli, Ming (1588); inside the library are stored 678 cases and 6,780 volumes of scriptures bestowed by Empress Dowager Cisheng Xuanwen Mingsu, mother of Emperor Wanli Zhu Yijun of Ming, which are national Buddhist scripture treasures.

Currently, the classics stored in the Scripture Library number 4,178 volumes counted by the Thousand-Character Classic; the scriptures are 35 cm long and 13 cm wide.

The long history, the large number of people participating in translation and authorship, and the richness of content included are truly rare.

The Scripture Library is the main building in the temple, of brick-wood structure, two stories, double-eave hip-and-gable roof, with gray semi-cylindrical tiles; there are four thicker eave columns at the front and rear each, four exposed columns inside the library, and seven eave columns on each of the east and west sides.

The library is 15 m high, with six beasts on the top ridge raising their heads; the central bay part projects outward as a flat seat to emphasize the central composition of the facade.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 一组规模宽敞、形制完备的敕建寺院
  • 也是陕西唯一的皇家城堡式寺院
  • 素有汉王名刹之称
  • 1962年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 据洋县志载智果寺始建于唐仪凤年间
  • 宋、元皆重修
  • 清雍正二年重修智果院六次序碑记
  • 嗣后万历十四年颁施藏经
  • 赐建藏经楼
  • 其规模逐为宏阔
  • 藏经楼现存碑碣16通
  • 楼前竖有明万历十六年圣谕碑
  • 楼内存有明神宗朱翊钧之母慈圣宣文明肃皇太后御赐经卷678函、6780卷
  • 为全国佛经珍品
  • 目前藏经楼所藏经典按千字文计为4178卷

40. 宝鸡金台观

Baoji Jintai Temple
元、明、清 道教宫观 陕西省重点文物保护单位 宝鸡市金台区北坡森林公园

宝鸡金台观位于宝鸡市金台区北坡森林公园。宫观整体布局、神祇及神殿布列、装饰艺术带有明显道家思想的影响，是我国北方特有的窑洞式道观建筑群。2003年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

道观始建于元朝末年，因明代道士张三丰在此修道，因而闻名。《明史·方伎》记曰：太祖敬闻其名，洪武二十四年（1392年）遣使觅之不得，后居宝鸡之金台观。明嘉靖四十三年（1564年）修缮玉皇阁，创建娘娘殿。

清道光二十二年（1843年）捐资新建吕祖洞。清光绪年间重修。后金台观屡次毁于战火，屡毁屡建。现观内大部分古建筑保留了明清建筑风格。金台观占地面积约5.9公顷，建筑群依地形、地势灵活展开。布局包括中院和东、西偏院三部分，中部主体的神殿区、东北部窑院的膳堂、宿舍区与西北部的园林。

中院采用中国传统的院落式，严谨的轴线对称，轴线上设玉皇阁、三清殿、三丰洞，两侧设配殿。另辟偏院诸洞设修炼、生活等用房。三清殿与玉皇阁构成一个四面围合的院落空间。而最南的三丰洞，为面阔五开间的靠崖窑洞大殿。规格、装饰等级较高。山门呈东西朝向，三开间重檐歇山，为新中国成立后重建之物。在三丰洞的左右两侧更有飞天、姜嫄、药王诸祠洞。

前院另有祖师殿、玄帝殿。东北部的窑院依地形筑窑洞两迭，一层洞窟14孔，二层洞窟10孔，供奉药王、周公、王母、文昌诸神。娘娘殿、太皇宫形成西北部院落。

— ENGLISH —

Baoji Jintai Temple is located in Beipo Forest Park, Jintai District, Baoji City.

The overall layout of the temple, the arrangement of deities and divine halls, and decorative art are clearly influenced by Taoist thought, and it is a cave-style Taoist temple complex unique to northern China.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2003. The temple was first built in the late Yuan Dynasty, and became famous because the Ming dynasty Taoist Zhang Sanfeng practiced Taoism here.

The 'History of Ming: Biographies of Masters' records: 'The Taizu Emperor respectfully heard of his name, and in the 24th year of Hongwu (1392), sent envoys to seek him but could not find him; later he resided at Jintai Temple in Baoji.' In the 43rd year of Jiajing, Ming (1564), the Jade Emperor Pavilion was repaired and the Goddess Hall was built.

In the 22nd year of Daoguang, Qing (1843), donations were made to build the Lüzu Cave. It was rebuilt during the Guangxu period of Qing. Later, Jintai Temple was repeatedly destroyed by war, rebuilt after each destruction.

Most of the ancient buildings in the current temple retain the Ming-Qing architectural style. Jintai Temple covers about 5.9 hectares, with the building complex flexibly展开 according to terrain and landform.

The layout includes three parts: the central courtyard and east and west side courtyards; the central main divine hall area, the dining hall and dormitory area of the cave courtyard in the northeast, and the garden in the northwest.

The central courtyard adopts the traditional Chinese courtyard style with rigorous axis symmetry; on the axis are set the Jade Emperor Pavilion, Sanqing Hall, and Sanfeng Cave, with side halls on both sides. Separate side courtyards with various caves are set for cultivation and living use.

The Sanqing Hall and Jade Emperor Pavilion form a four-sided enclosed courtyard space. The southernmost Sanfeng Cave is a cliff-side cave hall with five bays in width, with high specifications and decorative grade.

The mountain gate faces east-west, with three bays and double-eave hip-and-gable roof, rebuilt after the founding of New China. On the left and right sides of Sanfeng Cave, there are the Feitian, Jiangyuan, and Yaowang (Medicine King) temple caves.

The front courtyard also has the Patriarch Hall and Xuandi Hall. The cave courtyard in the northeast builds two layers of caves according to terrain, with 14 caves on the first layer and 10 caves on the second layer, enshrining the Medicine King, Duke of Zhou, Queen Mother, and Wenchang deities.

The Goddess Hall and Taihuang Palace form the northwest courtyard.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 宫观整体布局、神祇及神殿布列、装饰艺术带有明显道家思想的影响
  • 是我国北方特有的窑洞式道观建筑群
  • 2003年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 道观始建于元朝末年
  • 因明代道士张三丰在此修道因而闻名
  • 明史方伎记曰太祖敬闻其名洪武二十四年遣使觅之不得后居宝鸡之金台观
  • 明嘉靖四十三年修缮玉皇阁
  • 创建娘娘殿
  • 清道光二十二年捐资新建吕祖洞
  • 清光绪年间重修
  • 后金台观屡次毁于战火屡毁屡建
  • 现观内大部分古建筑保留了明清建筑风格
  • 金台观占地面积约5.9公顷
  • 建筑群依地形地势灵活展开
  • 布局包括中院和东西偏院三部分

41. 宝鸡钓鱼台

Baoji Diaoyutai
唐、明、清 道教宫观 宝鸡市陈仓区天王镇

钓鱼台在唐代时就建庙塑像，之后历代皆有修整及重建。

占地面积约200平方公里，现有一石、二柏、三间庙，一石为璜石玄立：太公庙前溪水中，一巨石，此即璜石，高6.66米，顶平广，上面直径11.2米，根部仅4米，恰似莲座。

璜石上刻一米见方楷字孕璜遗璞，系清乾隆三十八年（1773年）宝鸡知县徐文博所书。在璜石右上方的河边矗立着雄伟壮观的姜太公庙。三间庙为唐建明修周文王庙、姜太公庙、三清庙等。盘山而上的周文王庙，重瓦危檐，布局严谨。姜太公庙：为唐贞观初年所建，明嘉靖年间整修，清乾隆二十六年重修。

坐西朝东，东西10.15米，南北40米。门前有古柏4棵，传为唐代所植。北侧有僧房5间，相传始建于唐贞观年间，历代重修。

面阔三间，进深两间，柱径0.36米，柱高4.2米，台基高0.60米。砖包土坯墙，前檐有斗拱，一斗十六升，明间、补间和次间各一朵。

五架梁带单架梁，硬山顶，布灰板瓦，部分琉璃瓦串沟，琉璃勾头滴水，镂空造花脊，兽边脊，琉璃小五兽，前檐有槅扇门15扇。周文王庙：位于太公庙以西半山腰台地上。建筑群坐南向北。

东西29米，南北39米。初建于汉代，现存建筑为明代风格。主要建筑以中轴线东西对称，南北排列，主要建筑有悬山门楼一座，文王殿一座三间，姜嫄圣母殿一孔接檐三间，东西僧房各三间。

1983年按原貌进行了翻修，庙柱径0.4米，柱高3.62米，砖包土坯墙，四面都有斗拱，共有斗拱25朵。七架梁，歇山顶，屋面施灰布板筒瓦，勾头滴水，正脊镂空龙纹花草。

明间有雕花门4扇，两次间有格子扇2副，后檐明间有刻花门4扇，次间有2圆窗。三清庙：位于钓鱼台伐鱼河东岸半山坡台地上。坐南向北，为群体建筑。东西13.5米，南北39米。有门楼一座，玉皇楼一间，三清、三宫庙各一间，三清殿三间，门楼东侧有僧房两间。始建年代不详，据重修龙王山神碑庙记，清乾隆已有，后有过重修。

目前建筑仍保留清代风格。

— ENGLISH —

Diaoyutai (Fishing Terrace) had temples built and statues molded as early as the Tang dynasty, and has been repaired and rebuilt in successive dynasties.

It covers about 200 square kilometers, and currently has 'one stone, two cypresses, three temples.' The one stone is the Huangshi (Jade-in-embryo Stone) standing mysteriously: in the stream in front of the Taigong Temple, there is a huge stone, this is the Huangshi, 6.66 m high, with a flat and broad top, 11.2 m in diameter on top, and only 4 m at the root, just like a lotus seat.

On the Huangshi, one-meter-square regular script characters 'Yunhuang Yipu' (Jade Embryo Remaining Uncut Jade) are carved, written by Xu Wenbo, Magistrate of Baoji County, in the 38th year of Qianlong, Qing (1773).

On the riverbank at the upper right of the Huangshi stands the magnificent Jiang Taigong Temple. The three temples are the King Wen of Zhou Temple, Jiang Taigong Temple, and Sanqing Temple, built in Tang and repaired in Ming.

The King Wen of Zhou Temple, winding up the mountain, has heavy tiles and high eaves, with a rigorous layout. Jiang Taigong Temple: built in the early Zhenguan period of Tang, renovated in the Jiajing period of Ming, and rebuilt in the 26th year of Qianlong, Qing.

Facing east from west, 10.15 m east-west and 40 m north-south. In front of the door are 4 ancient cypresses, said to have been planted in the Tang dynasty.

On the north side are 5 monk rooms, said to have been first built in the Zhenguan period of Tang and rebuilt in successive dynasties. Three bays wide, two bays deep, column diameter 0.36 m, column height 4.2 m, platform height 0.60 m.

Brick-clad adobe walls, with dougong on the front eaves, one dou sixteen sheng, one set each in the central bay, intermediate bay, and secondary bay.

Five-rafter beam with single-rafter beam, hip-and-gable roof, with gray flat tiles, some glazed tile valleys, glazed hook-head drip tiles, openwork flower ridges, beast edge ridges, glazed small five beasts, and 15 lattice doors on the front eave.

King Wen of Zhou Temple: located on the half-mountain terrace west of the Taigong Temple. The building complex faces north from south. 29 m east-west and 39 m north-south. First built in the Han dynasty, the existing buildings are in Ming style.

The main buildings are symmetric east-west on the central axis, arranged north-south; the main buildings include an overhanging mountain gate tower, one King Wen Hall of three bays, one Jiangyuan Holy Mother Hall cave with extended eaves of three bays, and three monk rooms on each east and west side.

It was renovated according to the original appearance in 1983; temple column diameter 0.4 m, column height 3.62 m, brick-clad adobe walls, with dougong on all four sides, totaling 25 dougong sets.

Seven-rafter beam, hip-and-gable roof, with gray flat and semi-cylindrical tiles, hook-head drip tiles, and openwork dragon-pattern flowers on the main ridge.

The central bay has 4 carved doors, the two secondary bays have 2 pairs of lattice windows, the rear eave central bay has 4 carved doors, and the secondary bays have 2 round windows. Sanqing Temple: located on the half-mountain terrace on the east bank of the Fayu River at Diaoyutai.

Facing north from south, it is a group building. 13.5 m east-west and 39 m north-south. There is one gate tower, one Jade Emperor Tower, one each of Sanqing and Sangong temples, three bays of Sanqing Hall, and two monk rooms east of the gate tower.

The construction date is unknown; according to the 'Stele Record of Rebuilding the Dragon King and Mountain God Temple,' it existed in the Qianlong period of Qing and was later rebuilt. The current buildings still retain the Qing style.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 钓鱼台在唐代时就建庙塑像
  • 之后历代皆有修整及重建
  • 占地面积约200平方公里
  • 现有一石、二柏、三间庙
  • 一石为璜石玄立
  • 太公庙前溪水中一巨石此即璜石
  • 高6.66米
  • 顶平广
  • 上面直径11.2米
  • 根部仅4米
  • 恰似莲座
  • 璜石上刻一米见方楷字孕璜遗璞
  • 系清乾隆三十八年宝鸡知县徐文博所书
  • 在璜石右上方的河边矗立着雄伟壮观的姜太公庙
  • 三间庙为唐建明修周文王庙、姜太公庙、三清庙等

42. 华阴玉泉院

Huayin Yuquan Courtyard
宋、明、清 道教宫观 陕西省重点文物保护单位 华阴市玉泉路最南端

玉泉院位于华阴市玉泉路最南端，为道教之全真派圣地，也是陕西道教著名宫观之一，是华麓景区保存最完好的园林式古建筑群。1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。玉泉院修建最早始于宋初。

据传说，北宋皇祐年间（1049~1053年）道士贾得升为纪念其师陈抟在华峪口修建庙宇，因庙院内原有一眼清泉，泉水清冽如玉，故名为华山玉泉院。道观历代几经周折，至明万历三十六年（1608年）重新修葺，扩为今置。

清康熙四十二年（1703年）毁于洪水，后又重建。乾隆四十二年（1777年）建亭树榭，绕以周垣。1949年后曾多次维修并向东扩建了长廊。

1985年华山道教协会集资对其进行修缮，按照古代宫观的基址重建部分建筑，使东西两部分建筑更趋对称。玉泉院坐南向北，布局规整，占地面积约9000平方米，共有大小建筑18栋。道观由左右对称、从前至后随地形逐次升高的三进式院落组成，主要建筑均排列在中轴线上。

由东、中、西三部分建筑组成：中间为主体建筑希夷祠，祠坐南面北，依势而建，为一座四合院式的庭堂建筑。主殿老祖殿，内供奉陈抟老祖塑像，东西分别为客堂与道舍，青砖蓝瓦，古朴无华。院内有陈抟弟子贾得升撰写的建醮碑，是研究宋代斋醮道场的珍贵资料。东西两侧以长廊围合，东长廊随地形高低跌落，起伏有致；西长廊曲折迂绕，联系含清殿和无忧亭。

东部有华佗墓、回廊和十二洞等。西部有回廊、石舫、无忧亭、含清殿、希夷洞、山荪亭等建筑。玉泉院融自然风光和人文景观为一体。

— ENGLISH —

Yuquan Courtyard (Jade Spring Courtyard) is located at the southernmost end of Yuquan Road, Huayin City.

It is a holy land of the Quanzhen School of Taoism and one of the famous Taoist temples in Shaanxi, and is the best-preserved garden-style ancient building complex in the Huashan foothills scenic area. It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992.

The construction of Yuquan Courtyard first began in the early Song dynasty.

According to legend, during the Huangyou period of the Northern Song (1049-1053), the Taoist priest Jia Desheng built a temple at the Huayu Pass to commemorate his master Chen Tuan; because there was originally a clear spring in the temple courtyard with water as clear as jade, it was named Huashan Yuquan Courtyard.

The temple经历 several twists and turns over successive dynasties; in the 36th year of Wanli, Ming (1608), it was renovated and expanded to its current size. In the 42nd year of Kangxi, Qing (1703), it was destroyed by flood and later rebuilt.

In the 42nd year of Qianlong (1777), pavilions and terraces were built, surrounded by walls. After 1949, it was repaired multiple times and the long corridor was expanded eastward.

In 1985, the Huashan Taoist Association raised funds to renovate it, rebuilding some buildings according to the foundations of ancient temples, making the east and west parts more symmetrical.

Yuquan Courtyard faces north from south, with a regular layout, covering about 9,000 square meters, with a total of 18 large and small buildings.

The temple consists of three-entry courtyards that are left-right symmetrical and rise gradually from front to back following the terrain, with main buildings all arranged on the central axis.

It is composed of three parts: east, central, and west: the central part is the main building Xiyi Temple, which faces north from south, built according to the terrain, as a courtyard-style hall building.

The main hall is the Laozu (Ancestor) Hall, enshrining the statue of Ancestor Chen Tuan; the east and west are respectively the guest hall and Taoist quarters, with blue bricks and blue tiles, simple and unadorned.

In the courtyard, there is the Jianjiao (Temple Establishment) Stele written by Chen Tuan's disciple Jia Desheng, which is precious material for studying Song dynasty Taoist ritual sites.

The east and west sides are enclosed by long corridors; the east long corridor rises and falls with the terrain, undulating gracefully; the west long corridor winds and twists, connecting the Hanqing Hall and Wuyou (Worry-Free) Pavilion.

In the east, there are the Hua Tuo Tomb, corridors, and Twelve Caves. In the west, there are corridors, stone boat, Wuyou Pavilion, Hanqing Hall, Xiyi Cave, and Shansun Pavilion. Yuquan Courtyard integrates natural scenery and cultural landscapes.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 为道教之全真派圣地
  • 也是陕西道教著名宫观之一
  • 是华麓景区保存最完好的园林式古建筑群
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 玉泉院修建最早始于宋初
  • 据传说北宋皇祐年间道士贾得升为纪念其师陈抟在华峪口修建庙宇
  • 因庙院内原有一眼清泉泉水清冽如玉故名为华山玉泉院
  • 道观历代几经周折
  • 至明万历三十六年重新修葺
  • 扩为今置
  • 清康熙四十二年毁于洪水
  • 后又重建
  • 乾隆四十二年建亭树榭
  • 绕以周垣
  • 1949年后曾多次维修并向东扩建了长廊

43. 耀县药王山庙

Yaoxian Yaowang Mountain Temple
宋、金、元、明、清 道教宫观 铜川市耀州区药王山

药王山庙位于铜川市耀州区药王山。南庵：即升仙台，相传是孙思邈当年隐栖之处。北宋崇宁二年（1103年）赐额静应庙，清代改称静应宫。占地10余亩，现有建筑16座，建筑面积2500平方米。

其最南端为文昌阁（即魁星楼），现存的七间殿、戏楼等建筑，均为清代建造。另外，保留明嘉靖年间大地震幸存的金、元大殿。院内还有唐太宗拜孙思邈的拜真台、隐居地、药王牡丹园等古迹。

金代殿：建于北宋嘉祐四年（1059年），面阔三间，进深六架，椽带前檐，外檐斗拱五铺作双下昂，构架为前后搭牵对四椽用四柱，前檐廊柱粗短，柱上用通长大额，外檐斗拱五铺作双下昂，里转出双抄，令拱与耍头相交上承橑檐枋，耍头后尾斜上置于老檐柱（金柱）上，压在下平槫下；

补间铺作一朵。后檐无斗拱，可能为后世所改换。元代殿：面阔三间，进深四间，架构也为前后搭牵对四椽用四柱；四椽栿上用驼峰，太平梁上立蜀柱，两边用叉手；前檐斗拱形制同金代殿，用材较金代殿为小；补间铺作一朵，明间出45度斜昂。但梁架经后世翻修，构件改换较多。

大殿坐北面南，用巨石砌其基，面阔五间，单檐歇山顶，两侧为耳殿，殿内中央供奉着孙思邈的彩色塑像，为明代所塑，至今色彩依然绚丽异常。献亭方形平面，各三间，重檐歇山顶。碑亭东侧室内陈列着药王画像和各种版本的《千金翼方》《备急千金要方》等著作。一通刻写的是《千金宝要》。

另一通刻写的是《海上仙方》，两面刻字。

— ENGLISH —

Yaowang (Medicine King) Mountain Temple is located on Yaowang Mountain, Yaozhou District, Tongchuan City. South Nunnery: also known as the Shengxian (Immortal Ascension) Terrace, said to be the place where Sun Simiao once lived in seclusion.

In the 2nd year of Chongning, Northern Song (1103), it was granted the plaque 'Jingying Temple'; in the Qing dynasty, it was renamed Jingying Palace. It covers over 10 mu, with 16 existing buildings and a building area of 2,500 square meters.

Its southernmost part is the Wenchang Pavilion (i.e., Kuixing Tower); the existing seven-bay hall, opera tower, and other buildings were all built in the Qing dynasty.

In addition, the Jin and Yuan main halls that survived the great earthquake in the Jiajing period of Ming are preserved.

In the courtyard, there are also historic sites such as the Baizhen Terrace where Emperor Taizong of Tang paid homage to Sun Simiao, the hermitage, and the Medicine King Peony Garden.

Jin Dynasty Hall: built in the 4th year of Jiayou, Northern Song (1059), three bays wide, six rafters deep, with rafters and front eaves; the outer eave dougong is five-puzuo double xia'ang; the frame is front-rear daqian to four-rafter with four columns; the front eave corridor columns are thick and short, with through-length large lintels on the columns; the outer eave dougong is five-puzuo double xia'ang, with interior turning out double chao; the ling-gong intersects with the shua-tou to support the liaoyan fang; the rear tail of the shua-tou slopes upward onto the old eave column (gold column), pressed under the lower flat purlin; one intermediate bracket set.

The rear eave has no dougong, possibly changed by later generations.

Yuan Dynasty Hall: three bays wide, four bays deep; the frame is also front-rear daqian to four-rafter with four columns; humpback blocks are used on the four-rafter beam, short columns are erected on the Taiping beam, and diagonal braces are used on both sides; the front eave dougong form is the same as the Jin Dynasty hall, with smaller materials than the Jin Dynasty hall; one intermediate bracket set, with 45-degree diagonal ang in the central bay.

However, the beam frame has been renovated by later generations, with many components replaced.

The main hall faces south from north, with its foundation built of huge stones, five bays wide, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with side halls on both sides; in the center of the hall, the colored statue of Sun Simiao is enshrined, molded in the Ming dynasty, with colors still brilliantly vivid to this day.

The offering pavilion has a square plane, three bays each, with double-eave hip-and-gable roof.

In the room east of the stele pavilion, portraits of the Medicine King and various editions of works such as 'Qianjin Yifang' (Supplement to the Thousand Golden Prescriptions) and 'Beiji Qianjin Yaofang' (Essential Prescriptions Worth a Thousand Gold for Emergencies) are displayed.

One stele is carved with 'Qianjin Baoyao' (Essentials of the Thousand Gold). The other stele is carved with 'Haishang Xianfang' (Immortal Prescriptions from the Sea), with characters carved on both sides.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 南庵即升仙台
  • 相传是孙思邈当年隐栖之处
  • 北宋崇宁二年赐额静应庙
  • 清代改称静应宫
  • 占地10余亩
  • 现有建筑16座
  • 建筑面积2500平方米
  • 其最南端为文昌阁即魁星楼
  • 现存的七间殿、戏楼等建筑均为清代建造
  • 另外保留明嘉靖年间大地震幸存的金、元大殿
  • 院内还有唐太宗拜孙思邈的拜真台、隐居地、药王牡丹园等古迹
  • 金代殿建于北宋嘉祐四年
  • 面阔三间
  • 进深六架
  • 椽带前檐

44. 西安小皮院清真寺

Xi'an Xiaopiyuan Mosque
唐、宋、元、明、清 伊斯兰教建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安市小皮院巷内

小皮院清真寺位于西安市小皮院巷内，因其在化觉巷清真大寺以北，且规模较大，亦称北大寺。1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。该寺创建年代无考。据传，该寺兴建于唐末，时称万寿寺。

宋徽宗大观元年（1107年）时为长安京兆四坊旧有清真寺。元仁宗皇庆元年（1312年）该寺得到敕建，时称真教寺。明洪武元年（1368年）朱元璋敕百字匾于该寺。

明万历三十九年至四十二年（1611~1614年）重修。

小皮院清真寺坐西朝东，占地面积近6000平方米，东西长146米，南北宽41米，总建筑面积2000多平方米，分四进院落。

小皮院清真寺寺门在北侧，面阔三间，单檐歇山顶。上悬小皮院清真寺木匾一面。第一进院落有一座大照壁，照壁的两侧有配壁。配壁为水磨砖雕，壁顶五脊四面坡，青瓦覆面。二进院中有五间厢房，均为单檐硬山顶，带围廊。

正角方有三座门楼，被称为连三门，同样为水磨砖雕。中门面阔七间，单檐歇山顶，两边为单间小门。第三进院落南北两侧各建大小厢房三间，曰南北厅，南为集会议事之处，北侧为讲经堂及阿訇居室。院中间通道两侧各有石碑一通，刻有明万历年间修寺碑记及该寺地契。四进院落即主院落，由月台和礼拜大殿两大部分构成。月台正面为石门（也作牌坊）三座，踏道三路。

山花墙外两侧各辟有一砖雕角门和一月亮门。过角门、月亮门即是礼拜大殿后院，为阿訇墓地。大殿：始建于明代，分为两个部分，为前殿后亭建筑形式，呈凸字形。前殿为单檐歇山顶，后亭为重檐四角攒尖顶，屋顶均覆盖琉璃瓦。

前殿面阔七间，计32米；进深四间带前廊，计20.6米。后亭（后窑殿）面阔与进深各三间，长15.45米，宽15.4米，三面带廊。前殿采用三架梁莲花枕结构，十一檩十椽，雁尾槽挂椽板。后亭内则设天花，做成独特的斗八藻井。

整座大殿全为大木结构，而前殿后亭角门又均为精美砖雕。大殿南北两侧有山花墙迎风马头，下有青石雕刻，上有水磨青砖雕刻。

— ENGLISH —

Xiaopiyuan mosque is located in Xiaopiyuan Lane, Xi'an. Because it is north of the Huajue Lane Great mosque and is larger in scale, it is also called the North Great mosque.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992. The founding date of this mosque is unknown. According to legend, the mosque was built in the late Tang dynasty, then called Wanshou Temple.

In the 1st year of Daguan, Emperor Huizong of Song (1107), it was an existing mosque in the four wards of Jingzhao, Chang'an. In the 1st year of Huangqing, Emperor Renzong of Yuan (1312), the mosque received imperial construction, then called Zhenjiao Temple.

In the 1st year of Hongwu, Ming (1368), Zhu Yuanzhang bestowed a hundred-character plaque on this mosque. It was rebuilt from the 39th to 42nd year of Wanli, Ming (1611-1614).

Xiaopiyuan mosque faces east from west, covering nearly 6,000 square meters, 146 m long east-west and 41 m wide north-south, with a total building area of over 2,000 square meters, divided into four courtyards.

The mosque gate is on the north side, three bays wide, with a single-eave hip-and-gable roof. A wooden plaque reading 'Xiaopiyuan mosque' hangs above. The first courtyard has a large screen wall, with side screen walls on both sides.

The side screen walls are water-polished brick carvings, with five-ridge four-slope wall tops covered with blue tiles. In the second courtyard, there are five-bay wing rooms, all with single-eave hip-and-gable roofs and surrounding corridors.

At the front corner, there are three gate towers, called 'Lian San Men' (Connected Three Gates), also of water-polished brick carvings. The central gate is seven bays wide, with a single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with single-bay small gates on both sides.

In the third courtyard, three large and small wing rooms are built on each of the north and south sides, called the North and South Halls; the south is for gatherings and discussions, and the north is for scripture teaching halls and imam residences.

On both sides of the central passage in the courtyard, there is one stele each, carved with the temple repair stele record of the Wanli period of Ming and the temple's land deed. The fourth courtyard is the main courtyard, consisting of two major parts: the platform and the prayer hall.

On the front of the platform, there are three stone gates (also serving as archways), with three paths of steps. Outside the gable walls, one brick-carved corner gate and one moon gate are opened on each side.

Passing through the corner gate and moon gate is the rear courtyard of the prayer hall, which is the imam cemetery. Prayer Hall: first built in the Ming dynasty, divided into two parts, in the 'front hall, rear pavilion' architectural form,呈 a convex shape.

The front hall has a single-eave hip-and-gable roof, and the rear pavilion has a double-eave four-corner pyramidal roof, both roofs covered with glazed tiles.

The front hall is seven bays wide, totaling 32 m; four bays deep with front corridor, totaling 20.6 m. The rear pavilion (rear窑殿) is three bays in both width and depth, 15.45 m long and 15.4 m wide, with corridors on three sides.

The front hall adopts a three-rafter beam lotus-pillow structure, with eleven purlins and ten rafters, and goose-tail trough hanging rafter boards. Inside the rear pavilion, a ceiling is set, made into a unique dou-eight caisson.

The entire prayer hall is entirely of large wooden structure, while the corner gates of the front hall and rear pavilion are all exquisite brick carvings.

On the north and south sides of the prayer hall, there are gable walls with wind-facing horse-head walls, with bluestone carvings below and water-polished blue brick carvings above.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 因其在化觉巷清真大寺以北且规模较大亦称北大寺
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 该寺创建年代无考
  • 据传该寺兴建于唐末时称万寿寺
  • 宋徽宗大观元年时为长安京兆四坊旧有清真寺
  • 元仁宗皇庆元年该寺得到敕建
  • 时称真教寺
  • 明洪武元年朱元璋敕百字匾于该寺
  • 明万历三十九年至四十二年重修
  • 小皮院清真寺坐西朝东
  • 占地面积近6000平方米
  • 东西长146米
  • 南北宽41米
  • 总建筑面积2000多平方米
  • 分四进院落

45. 西安大皮院清真寺

Xi'an Dapiyuan Mosque
明、清 伊斯兰教建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市回民街大皮院

西安大皮院清真寺位于西安市回民街大皮院。1988年公布为第三批全国重点文物保护单位。

该寺始建于明永乐九年（1411年），1959年被占作他用，致使年久失修，大殿、南北亭倒塌。

1985年由当地教民捐资，恢复了清真寺的原貌，新修了石刻围栏、牌杭、满拉楼、虎国拜楼等设施。该寺建筑规模较小，布局简单。

占地面积4900平方米，建筑面积1610平方米，为中国古典式建筑风格。前院最东端是影壁，其后是山门。过三间硬山顶的山门进入院内，两侧是满拉楼，院中立着两座碑亭，碑亭后面就是石刻围栏、牌坊。南北两侧厢房为讲堂，西侧为带月台的礼拜殿。

礼拜殿：分前后两个部分。前部面阔五间，进深三间；后部面阔、进深各三间，平面呈凸字形，为两个单檐歇山顶相接而成。其收山做法只是利用山墙土砌作成山花，略有收进。其斗拱形制较古。礼拜殿上悬挂名人牌匾，慈禧手书派衍天方匾，光绪皇帝手书教崇西域匾，以及白崇禧题写的兴教建国匾。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Dapiyuan mosque is located at Dapiyuan, Huimin Street, Xi'an. It was designated as the third batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1988. The mosque was first built in the 9th year of Yongle, Ming (1411).

In 1959, it was occupied for other purposes, resulting in long-term disrepair, with the main hall and north-south pavilions collapsing.

In 1985, local religious believers donated funds to restore the original appearance of the mosque, newly building stone railings, plaques, Manla (religious student) buildings, and Huguo worship tower facilities.

The mosque building scale is small, with a simple layout. It covers 4,900 square meters, with a building area of 1,610 square meters, in the Chinese classical architectural style. The easternmost end of the front courtyard is the screen wall, behind which is the mountain gate.

Passing through the three-bay hip-and-gable roof mountain gate into the courtyard, on both sides are the Manla buildings; in the courtyard stand two stele pavilions; behind the stele pavilions are the stone railings and archway.

The north and south side wing rooms are lecture halls, and the west side is the prayer hall with a platform. Prayer Hall: divided into front and rear parts.

The front part is five bays wide and three bays deep; the rear part is three bays in both width and depth, with a convex plane, formed by two single-eave hip-and-gable roofs joined together.

Its shoushan (gable reduction) method only uses the gable wall rammed with earth to make the gable, with slight recession. Its dougong form is relatively ancient.

On the prayer hall hang famous person plaques: the 'Paiyan Tianfang' (Sect Derived from the Heavenly Land) plaque handwritten by Empress Dowager Cixi, the 'Jiao Chong Xiyu' (Religion Respected in the Western Regions) plaque handwritten by Emperor Guangxu, and the 'Xingjiao Jianguo' (Promote Religion, Build the Nation) plaque inscribed by Bai Chongxi.

  • 该寺始建于明永乐九年
  • 1959年被占作他用
  • 致使年久失修
  • 大殿南北亭倒塌
  • 1985年由当地教民捐资
  • 恢复了清真寺的原貌
  • 新修了石刻围栏、牌杭、满拉楼、虎国拜楼等设施
  • 该寺建筑规模较小
  • 布局简单
  • 占地面积4900平方米
  • 建筑面积1610平方米
  • 为中国古典式建筑风格
  • 前院最东端是影壁
  • 其后是山门

46. 周至楼观台

Zhouzhi Louguantai
周、唐、金、元、明、清 道教宫观 西安市周至县楼观镇

楼观台位于西安市周至县楼观镇，是道教圣地，相传为春秋时期伟大的哲学家老子讲授《道德经》五千言之地。

说经台位于海拔594米的山冈上，始建于公元619年，1236年重新扩建，明、清均有修葺。

说经台位居峰顶，南依秦岭，北瞰渭水，占地9432.5平方米，呈南北纵长182.6米，东西平均宽30米不规则矩形的多进院落。历来都是帝王、道众朝拜的仙都，文人墨客云集的圣地。宋苏东坡诗云：此台一览秦川小，不待传经意已空。

祠院主要殿堂有启玄殿、斗姥殿、救苦殿、灵官殿。此外尚有配殿两座、藏经楼和其他两侧配殿、厢房和展廊。楼观台有文物古迹50多处，现仅存的说经台、炼丹炉、化女泉、上善池、吕祖洞、宗圣宫、老子墓、仰天池、镇仙宝塔、三鹰柏、古银杏树等古迹遗址。楼观台现有建筑均为明清所建，作为单体建筑并无多大历史价值，但建筑与环境的结合颇具特色。

说经台：亦称老子祠、授经台，是春秋时期伟大的哲学家老子讲授《道德经》五千言之地。说经台山门：南向偏西，建在高1.3米的台基上。山门为单檐悬山顶，面阔七间，明间设门。东西两端又增建耳房各一间，单檐悬山顶。魏晋南北朝时形成著名之楼观道。

至唐高祖武德年间（公元618~626年）建规模较大的宗圣宫，楼观被尊为皇家祖庙，致于鼎盛。金代天兴年间（1232~1234年），楼观殿宇屡遭战乱，焚毁殆尽。

宋元以后，楼观台屡遭颓圮和葺缮，盛况渐衰。清末，宗圣宫废毁，唯有说经台保存完好。

— ENGLISH —

Louguantai is located in Louguan Town, Zhouzhi County, Xi'an.

It is a Taoist holy land, said to be the place where Laozi, the great philosopher of the Spring and Autumn period, taught the five-thousand-character 'Tao Te Ching.' The Shuojing Terrace (Scripture Teaching Terrace) is located on a hill at an altitude of 594 m, first built in 619 AD, rebuilt and expanded in 1236, and repaired in both Ming and Qing dynasties.

The Shuojing Terrace sits at the peak, backed by the Qinling Mountains to the south and overlooking the Wei River to the north, covering 9,432.5 square meters,呈 an irregular rectangular multi-courtyard layout 182.6 m long north-south and 30 m wide on average east-west.

It has always been a fairy capital for emperors and Taoists to worship, and a holy land gathering literati and poets.

Su Dongpo of the Song dynasty wrote: 'This terrace overlooks the small Qinchuan plain, before transmitting scriptures the meaning is already empty.' The main halls of the temple courtyard include the Qixuan Hall, Doumu Hall, Jiuku Hall, and Lingguan Hall.

In addition, there are two side halls, the Scripture Library, and other side halls, wing rooms, and exhibition corridors on both sides.

Louguantai has over 50 cultural relics and historic sites; currently only the Shuojing Terrace, Alchemy Furnace, Huanu Spring, Shangshan Pool, Lüzu Cave, Zongsheng Palace, Laozi Tomb, Yangtian Pool, Zhenxian Pagoda, Sanying Cypress, and ancient ginkgo tree等 historic sites remain.

The existing buildings at Louguantai were all built in the Ming and Qing dynasties; as individual buildings they do not have much historical value, but the combination of architecture and environment is quite distinctive.

Shuojing Terrace: also called Laozi Temple or Scripture Teaching Terrace, is the place where Laozi, the great philosopher of the Spring and Autumn period, taught the five-thousand-character 'Tao Te Ching.' Shuojing Terrace Mountain Gate: faces south slightly west, built on a 1.3-m-high platform base.

The mountain gate has a single-eave overhanging gable roof, seven bays wide, with a door in the central bay. At both east and west ends, an additional ear room is built, each with a single-eave overhanging gable roof.

During the Wei, Jin, Southern and Northern Dynasties, the famous Louguan Taoism was formed. By the Wude period of Emperor Gaozu of Tang (618-626 AD), the large-scale Zongsheng Palace was built, and Louguan was revered as the royal ancestral temple, reaching its peak.

During the Tianxing period of Jin (1232-1234), Louguan's halls were repeatedly damaged by war and completely burned down. After the Song and Yuan dynasties, Louguantai was repeatedly ruined and repaired, with its prosperity gradually declining.

At the end of Qing, Zongsheng Palace was destroyed, and only the Shuojing Terrace was preserved intact.

  • 道教圣地
  • 相传为老子讲授道德经五千言之地
  • 说经台位于海拔594米的山冈上
  • 始建于公元619年
  • 1236年重新扩建
  • 明清均有修葺
  • 说经台位居峰顶
  • 南依秦岭
  • 北瞰渭水
  • 占地9432.5平方米
  • 呈南北纵长182.6米
  • 东西平均宽30米不规则矩形的多进院落
  • 祠院主要殿堂有启玄殿、斗姥殿、救苦殿、灵官殿
  • 此外尚有配殿两座、藏经楼和其他两侧配殿、厢房和展廊
  • 楼观台有文物古迹50多处

47. 西安八仙庵

Xi'an Baxian Temple
宋、元、明、清 道教宫观 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安东关长乐坊

八仙庵，又称八仙宫，位于西安东关长乐坊，原为唐代兴庆宫遗址的一部分，现为西安最大的一座道教庙宇。1956年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

八仙庵始建于宋朝，元至正年间（1341~1368年）重修，明正德年间（1506~1521年）增建雷祖殿。

清康熙十四年（1675年）重修，嘉庆十二年（1807年）增建吕祖、太白诸殿，道人董清奇增建西跨院。

道光十二年（1832年）《十方丛林碑记》记载：董清奇以后有韩合义、刘合仑开坛放戒，朱教先创建西花园。同治年间（1862~1874年），部分殿宇古柏毁于兵火。

道光年间（1821~1850年）重修。1900年，光绪皇帝与慈禧太后避难西安时，驻跸于八仙庵西花园内，光绪御书宝箓仙传匾额，慈禧太后敕名为西安东关清门万寿八仙宫。

八仙庵占地110亩，坐北面南，总建筑面积8200多平方米，现存殿堂建筑均系明清以后所建，布局紧凑，殿堂庄严。山门前雄伟壮观，建有砖砌

— ENGLISH —

Baxian Temple, also called Baxian Palace (Eight Immortals Palace), is located at Changle Fang, Dongguan, Xi'an. It was originally part of the site of the Tang dynasty Xingqing Palace and is now the largest Taoist temple in Xi'an.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1956. Baxian Temple was first built in the Song dynasty, rebuilt during the Zhizheng period of Yuan (1341-1368), and the Leizu (Thunder Ancestor) Hall was added during the Zhengde period of Ming (1506-1521).

It was rebuilt in the 14th year of Kangxi, Qing (1675); in the 12th year of Jiaqing (1807), the Lüzu, Taibai and other halls were added, and Taoist Dong Qingqi added the west side courtyard.

The 'Shifang Conglin Stele Record' of the 12th year of Daoguang (1832) records: After Dong Qingqi, Han Heyi and Liu Helun opened altars for ordination, and Zhu Jiaoxian founded the West Garden. During the Tongzhi period (1862-1874), some halls and ancient cypresses were destroyed by war.

It was rebuilt during the Daoguang period (1821-1850).

In 1900, when Emperor Guangxu and Empress Dowager Cixi took refuge in Xi'an, they stayed in the West Garden of Baxian Temple; Emperor Guangxu personally wrote the plaque 'Baolu Xianchuan' (Immortal Transmission of Precious Registers), and Empress Dowager Cixi bestowed the name 'Xi'an Dongguan Qingmen Wanshou Baxian Palace.' Baxian Temple covers 110 mu, facing south from north, with a total building area of over 8,200 square meters.

The existing hall buildings were all built after the Ming and Qing dynasties, with a compact layout and majestic halls. In front of the mountain gate, it is majestic and spectacular, with brick-built...

48. 佳县香炉寺

Jiaxian Xianglu Temple
明 道教宫观 榆林市佳县县城东北的黄河崖畔

香炉寺位于榆林市佳县县城东北的黄河崖畔。寺院由东、西两部分组成：东边有山门、龙王庙、娘娘庙等建筑；西边为一组三合院式的明代建筑，石牌坊位于院中，与寄傲亭对望。寺东南10米处，有直径5米、高20余米的石柱兀自耸立，巨石之上建有寄傲亭，可俯瞰黄河。

寄傲亭与崖畔之间有3米长的木桥相连，名曰悬桥。寺内建筑规模较小，三合院主体建筑为硬山顶，两厢为不规则建筑形式，南向山墙出耳房，作单坡檐。石牌坊为仿木构建筑，为一门两柱，斗拱用三攒，为三踩单翘制式，造型古拙。与县城古城门相通。

— ENGLISH —

Xianglu (Incense Burner) Temple is located on the Yellow River cliff northeast of Jiaxian County town, Yulin City.

The temple consists of two parts, east and west: the east has buildings such as the mountain gate, Dragon King Temple, and Goddess Temple; the west is a group of Ming dynasty buildings in a three-sided courtyard style, with a stone archway located in the courtyard, facing the Ji'ao Pavilion.

10 m southeast of the temple, a stone pillar with a diameter of 5 m and a height of over 20 m stands alone; on top of the huge stone, the Ji'ao Pavilion is built, overlooking the Yellow River.

Between the Ji'ao Pavilion and the cliff, a 3-m-long wooden bridge connects them, called the Suspended Bridge.

The temple buildings are small in scale; the main building of the three-sided courtyard has a hip-and-gable roof, the two wings are in irregular architectural forms, and the south-facing gable wall has ear rooms with single-slope eaves.

The stone archway is an imitation wooden structure, with one gate and two columns, using three sets of dougong in the three-cai single-qiao style, with a simple and ancient form. It connects with the ancient city gate of the county town.

  • 东边有山门、龙王庙、娘娘庙等建筑
  • 西边为一组三合院式的明代建筑
  • 石牌坊位于院中
  • 与寄傲亭对望
