  • 作单坡檐

49. 榆林戴兴寺

Yulin Daixing Temple
明、清 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 榆林城东驼峰山巅

戴兴寺位于榆林城东驼峰山巅，与香云寺、洪济寺、大庵寺和老爷庙等建筑群合称为四寺夹一庙，是榆林八景之一的驼峰拥翠。1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。戴兴寺建于明正德十一年（1516年），原为延绥镇总兵戴钦家祠。

明正德十三年（1518年）武宗巡边抵榆，纳戴钦之女，赐戴钦尚方宝剑，挂征西将军印，戴氏声威显赫一时。后舍为佛寺。

明天启七年（1627年），清康熙二十年（1681年）、四十二年（1703年），乾隆三十年（1765年），光绪三十年（1904年）戴兴寺均有维修。

戴兴寺规划布局因山就势，错落有致，总占地面积2846.1平方米。依地形高低就势，分上下两大院。主要建筑为五佛殿、大雄宝殿、观音殿、罗汉殿、地藏殿、南北禅堂等明代殿宇，建筑形式多为砖木结构。上院北殿供十殿阎君，南殿供十八罗汉。

院中有两座高八九米、下粗3米的千佛铁塔。塔上逐层铸有高约3寸小佛像。下院可分南、北、中三个小院落。北小院北殿祀二十四臂金身观音。东殿供奉弥勒佛。该院南墙有弥足珍贵的砖雕壁画和联语。中小院东殿是念经堂，供释迦佛。

殿前檐下有光绪、宣统年间数方木匾。中小院北房是待客室，其顶部建暮鼓阁，南房是佛教协会用房，其顶部建晨钟阁。其后进入南小院禅居处。五佛大殿：为上院正殿，建筑坐东面西，总高11.2米；一层为砖砌五开间通堂拱券式枕头窑，宽22米、深9.4米、高4.7米，外接飞檐走廊；

南、北两侧各砌砖拱券洞，置砖踏步，拾级而上可达二层；二层为砖木结构，歇山顶5楹36柱环廊，南北19.4米，深10.6米，顶覆仰合灰瓦、脊兽。

— ENGLISH —

Daixing Temple is located at the peak of Tuofeng (Camel Peak) Mountain east of Yulin City.

Together with building complexes such as Xiangyun Temple, Hongji Temple, Da'an Temple, and Laoye Temple, it is collectively called 'Four Temples Sandwiched Around One Temple,' and is one of the Eight Scenes of Yulin, 'Camel Peak Embraced by Green.' It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992.

Daixing Temple was built in the 11th year of Zhengde, Ming (1516), originally the family shrine of Dai Qin, the Commander-in-Chief of the Yansui Town.

In the 13th year of Zhengde, Ming (1518), Emperor Wuzong patrolled the frontier and arrived in Yulin, took Dai Qin's daughter as consort, bestowed Dai Qin with the imperial sword, and appointed him as the General Conquering the West; the Dai family's prestige was prominent for a time.

Later it was donated as a Buddhist temple. In the 7th year of Tianqi, Ming (1627), the 20th year (1681) and 42nd year (1703) of Kangxi, Qing, the 30th year of Qianlong (1765), and the 30th year of Guangxu (1904), Daixing Temple was all repaired.

Daixing Temple's planning layout follows the mountain terrain, well-proportioned and picturesque, with a total area of 2,846.1 square meters. According to the terrain height, it is divided into upper and lower major courtyards.

The main buildings are Ming dynasty halls such as the Five Buddha Hall, Mahavira Hall, Guanyin Hall, Arhat Hall, Ksitigarbha Hall, and north-south meditation halls, mostly in brick-wood structure.

The upper courtyard north hall enshrines the Ten Yama Kings, and the south hall enshrines the Eighteen Arhats. In the courtyard, there are two Thousand-Buddha iron towers 8-9 m high with a bottom thickness of 3 m. Small Buddha statues about 3 inches high are cast layer by layer on the towers.

The lower courtyard can be divided into three small courtyards: south, north, and central. The north small courtyard north hall enshrines the 24-armed golden-body Guanyin. The east hall enshrines Maitreya Buddha.

The south wall of this courtyard has precious brick-carved murals and couplets. The central small courtyard east hall is the scripture chanting hall, enshrining Sakyamuni Buddha. Under the front eave of the hall, there are several wooden plaques from the Guangxu and Xuantong periods.

The central small courtyard north room is the guest room, with a Dusk Drum Pavilion built on its top; the south room is for the Buddhist Association, with a Morning Bell Pavilion built on its top. Behind it enters the south small courtyard meditation quarters.

Five Buddha Hall: the main hall of the upper courtyard, facing west from east, with a total height of 11.2 m; the first floor is a brick-built five-bay通堂 arch-style pillow cave, 22 m wide, 9.4 m deep, 4.7 m high, with an overhanging eave corridor externally; on both south and north sides, brick arch tunnels are built with brick steps, leading up to the second floor; the second floor is of brick-wood structure, with a hip-and-gable roof of 5 bays and 36-column surrounding corridor, 19.4 m north-south and 10.6 m deep, covered with overlapping gray tiles and ridge beasts.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 与香云寺、洪济寺、大庵寺和老爷庙等建筑群合称为四寺夹一庙
  • 是榆林八景之一的驼峰拥翠
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 戴兴寺建于明正德十一年
  • 原为延绥镇总兵戴钦家祠
  • 明正德十三年武宗巡边抵榆
  • 纳戴钦之女
  • 赐戴钦尚方宝剑
  • 挂征西将军印
  • 戴氏声威显赫一时
  • 后舍为佛寺
  • 明天启七年
  • 清康熙二十年、四十二年
  • 乾隆三十年
  • 光绪三十年戴兴寺均有维修

50. 榆林万佛楼

Yulin Wanfo Tower
清 佛教寺庙 榆林市榆林古城内

万佛楼位于榆林市榆林古城内。结构可分为基座和楼阁层两部分。基座部分呈长方形，长29.6米，宽18.4米，高18米余，占地面积534平方米。内夯黄土，外包青砖。四个十字券洞与街道相通。

券洞高、宽各约5米。基座上建有二层木结构楼阁，一层正阁为通殿，南北各有4根径为0.28米的立柱直通二层额枋下，14根廊柱环宇，为一层屋檐及二层走廊承重柱。阁楼大殿分南北两院，北院为观音殿，廊柱卷棚式门厅，东西各设厢房3间；南院为孔雀明王殿，东西也各设厢房3间。

二层大殿呈长方形通殿，东西长12.6米，南北宽8.4米，周设走廊，柱间棂门格窗，外附栏杆。顶部为重檐歇山顶。

— ENGLISH —

Wanfo (Ten Thousand Buddha) Tower is located in the ancient city of Yulin, Yulin City. Its structure can be divided into two parts: the base and the pavilion floors. The base part is rectangular, 29.6 m long, 18.4 m wide, over 18 m high, covering 534 square meters.

It is rammed with loess inside and faced with blue bricks outside. Four cross-shaped arch tunnels connect with the streets. The arch tunnels are each about 5 m high and wide. On the base, a two-story wooden structure pavilion is built.

The first floor main pavilion is a通 hall, with 4 columns of 0.28 m diameter on each north and south side extending directly under the second floor lintels; 14 corridor columns surround it, serving as load-bearing columns for the first floor eaves and second floor corridor.

The pavilion main hall is divided into north and south courtyards; the north courtyard is the Guanyin Hall, with a corridor-column curved-roof entrance hall and 3 wing rooms on each east and west side; the south courtyard is the Peacock Mingwang Hall, also with 3 wing rooms on each east and west side.

The second floor main hall is a rectangular通 hall, 12.6 m long east-west and 8.4 m wide north-south, with surrounding corridors, lattice doors and windows between columns, and railings attached externally. The top is a double-eave hip-and-gable roof.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 结构可分为基座和楼阁层两部分
  • 基座部分呈长方形
  • 长29.6米
  • 宽18.4米
  • 高18米余
  • 占地面积534平方米
  • 内夯黄土
  • 外包青砖
  • 四个十字券洞与街道相通
  • 券洞高宽各约5米
  • 基座上建有二层木结构楼阁
  • 一层正阁为通殿
  • 南北各有4根径为0.28米的立柱直通二层额枋下
  • 14根廊柱环宇
  • 为一层屋檐及二层走廊承重柱

51. 西安化觉巷清真寺

Xi'an Huajue Lane Great Mosque
唐、明、清 伊斯兰教建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市鼓楼大街的化觉巷

化觉巷清真寺又名东大寺，位于西安市鼓楼大街的化觉巷，为国内占地规模最大的清真寺。1988年公布为第三批全国重点文物保护单位。

据《创建清真寺碑记》记载，该寺建于唐天宝元年（公元742年），明洪武二十五年（1392年）敕谕碑记，原称礼拜寺，明成化十八年（1482年），奏请改寺名为敕赐清修寺。

明嘉靖元年（1522年）重修，明万历三十四年（1606年）大修，清乾隆年间（公元736~1795年）的重修和扩建形成当今的格局。

民国三十六年（1947年）重修。

化觉巷清真寺坐西朝东，占地面积约1.3万平方米，建筑面积6000多平方米，东西长245.68米，南北宽47.56米。寺院依中轴呈前后序列，左右对称，布局严整。

全寺分为五进院落，内有殿、楼、亭、台、厅、堂180余间，置墙相隔，设门楼、门厅前后贯通。东端院墙正中的照壁，是全寺中轴线的起点，在这条中轴线上依次排列着木牌楼、五间房（二门）、石牌坊、敕修殿（三门）、省心楼（邦克楼）、连三门（四门）、凤凰亭、月台、礼拜大殿等主要建筑物。

中轴线的两侧，建有各式碑楼、石坊、南北对厅和厢房、门楼等各种附属建筑，左右对称，排列井然。前院有一座三开间木质牌楼，约建于17世纪初，高约9米，为三间四柱三楼，琉璃歇山顶，额书：敕赐礼拜寺。翼角飞檐，做成为中轴线上重要的空间节点。

第二进院有石牌坊一座，三间四柱式，中楣镌刻天监在兹，两侧相刻钦翼昭事和虔诚省礼。牌坊两侧各竖冲天雕龙碑一通，一为明万历三十四年（1606年）重修清修寺碑，碑阴镌刻有宋代书法家米芾手书道法参天地五个大字。

一为清乾隆三十三年（1768年）敕修清真寺碑，碑阴镌刻明代书法家董其昌手书敕赐礼拜寺五个大字。第三进院入口处是敕修殿，董其昌题敕赐礼拜寺的古老匾额悬于前檐。殿内立碑五通，其中一碑是清雍正十年三月十五日立的阿拉伯文月碑，用于推算穆斯林封斋和开斋的时间。

院中心的中轴线上矗立省心楼，即邦克楼。此楼为两层、三檐、八角中国楼阁式建筑。省心楼的左右两侧厢房为经堂、沐浴室及客厅。第四进院内有南北两厅，各面阔七间。院中心建一真亭，又名凤凰亭，中亭呈六角形，两侧夹亭呈三角形，三亭相连，采用中国传统木牌楼手法，形似凤凰展翅，独具风采。亭子正面檐下悬挂一真雕龙匾，为明建文元年兵部尚书铁铉所书。

亭后有鱼池，呈海棠形。西面三座石牌坊，可至月台。第五进院宽大的月台上有隋唐时的长明灯，被称为吉星高照，另有唐宋时期的白莲花座、明代的莲升三级座和古石盆、老鼠石、雪坡石等作为装饰品来点缀空间。北侧有一木制小楼阁，名呼图拜楼，是领拜者诵祷词的地方。大殿两侧设有月洞门，可通至后院，为阿訇墓地。

礼拜大殿：为化觉巷清真寺最大的建筑物，可容纳千人礼拜。

平面呈凸字形，面阔七间，宽为32.95米，进深九间，为38.53米，总面积近1300平方米，为两座单檐歇山顶勾连搭而成，锥形镏金顶，明柱雕花隔扇殿门。殿前两侧内山墙为巨幅砖雕花卉。

相邻檐柱用双柱，上做南北排水天沟。大殿梁架金柱不升高，梁柱交点仍用斗拱，近似宋代殿堂做法，似保持明代原状。梁架下做天花。后部虎尾与前部的搭接，在前部后檐采用大额做法，以大额承受梁架，以适应虎尾与前部梁柱不对缝的情况。外檐斗拱为五踩重昂制式，后部接深广各三间的后窑殿，为拜主所在，屋面为孔雀蓝琉璃瓦。

大殿内的藻井顶棚彩画共600余幅，均为阿拉伯文和蔓草纹组成的团花图案。殿内西墙上有一凹壁（圣龛），前左为宣谕台，供阿訇说教时站立，是教徒朝拜的方向，即圣地麦加方向，这也是清真寺坐西朝东的缘故。

— ENGLISH —

Huajue Lane Great mosque, also called the East Great mosque, is located in Huajue Lane, Gulou Street, Xi'an. It is the largest mosque by land area in China.

It was designated as the third batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1988. According to the 'Stele Record of the Founding of the mosque,' the mosque was built in the 1st year of Tianbao, Tang (742 AD).

In the 25th year of Hongwu, Ming (1392), an imperial edict stele recorded it was originally called the Worship Temple; in the 18th year of Chenghua, Ming (1482), a request was made to rename it the Imperial Bestowed Qingxiu Temple.

It was rebuilt in the 1st year of Jiajing, Ming (1522), majorly repaired in the 34th year of Wanli, Ming (1606), and the repairs and expansions during the Qianlong period of Qing (1736-1795) formed the current layout. It was rebuilt in the 36th year of the Republic (1947).

Huajue Lane Great mosque faces east from west, covering about 13,000 square meters, with a building area of over 6,000 square meters, 245.68 m long east-west and 47.56 m wide north-south.

The temple follows the central axis in a front-rear sequence, left-right symmetrical, with a rigorous layout.

The entire mosque is divided into five courtyards, containing over 180 halls, towers, pavilions, terraces, and rooms, separated by walls, with gate towers and gate halls connecting front and rear.

The screen wall in the center of the east end wall is the starting point of the mosque's central axis; along this axis are arranged in sequence the wooden archway, Five-Room Hall (second gate), stone archway, Chuxiu Hall (third gate), Shengxin Tower (Bangke Tower), Connected Three Gates (fourth gate), Phoenix Pavilion, platform, and Prayer Hall as the main buildings.

On both sides of the central axis, various附属 buildings such as stele towers, stone archways, north-south facing halls, wing rooms, and gate towers are built, left-right symmetrical and orderly arranged.

The front courtyard has a three-bay wooden archway, built around the early 17th century, about 9 m high, with three bays, four columns, and three floors, glazed hip-and-gable roof, with the inscription 'Imperial Bestowed Worship Temple' on the lintel.

With flying eaves at the wing corners, it becomes an important spatial node on the central axis.

The second courtyard has a stone archway, three-bay four-column style, with the central lintel carved 'Heaven Supervises Here,' and the two sides carved 'Reverently Winged Affairs' and 'Devout Ritual Observance.' On both sides of the archway, one sky-reaching dragon-carved stele stands each; one is the 'Stele of Rebuilding Qingxiu Temple' from the 34th year of Wanli, Ming (1606), with the back carved with the five large characters 'Dao Fa Can Tian Di' (The Way Law Participates in Heaven and Earth) handwritten by the Song dynasty calligrapher Mi Fu.

The other is the 'Stele of Imperially Repairing the mosque' from the 33rd year of Qianlong, Qing (1768), with the back carved with the five large characters 'Imperial Bestowed Worship Temple' handwritten by the Ming dynasty calligrapher Dong Qichang.

The entrance of the third courtyard is the Chuxiu Hall; the ancient plaque 'Imperial Bestowed Worship Temple' inscribed by Dong Qichang hangs on the front eave.

Five steles stand in the hall, one of which is an Arabic month stele erected on March 15 of the 10th year of Yongzheng, Qing, used to calculate the times for Muslim fasting and breaking the fast. At the center of the courtyard on the central axis stands the Shengxin Tower, i.e., the Bangke Tower.

This tower is a two-story, three-eave, octagonal Chinese pavilion-style building. The wing rooms on the left and right sides of the Shengxin Tower are scripture halls, bathhouses, and living rooms. The fourth courtyard has north and south halls, each seven bays wide.

At the center of the courtyard, the Yizhen Pavilion, also called the Phoenix Pavilion, is built; the central pavilion is hexagonal, and the two side pavilions are triangular; the three pavilions are connected, using traditional Chinese wooden archway techniques, resembling a phoenix spreading its wings, with unique charm.

Under the front eave of the pavilion hangs a carved dragon plaque 'Yizhen' (One Truth), written by Tie Xuan, Minister of War in the 1st year of Jianwen, Ming. Behind the pavilion is a fish pond in the shape of a crabapple. To the west, three stone archways lead to the platform.

On the wide platform of the fifth courtyard, there is a long-burning lamp from the Sui-Tang period, called 'Auspicious Star High Shining'; in addition, white lotus seats from the Tang-Song period, the 'Lotus Rising Three Levels' seat from the Ming dynasty, and ancient stone basins, mouse stone, and snow slope stone are used as decorations to embellish the space.

On the north side, there is a small wooden pavilion called the Hutubai Tower, where the prayer leader recites prayers. Moon gates are set on both sides of the main hall, leading to the rear courtyard, which is the imam cemetery.

Prayer Hall: the largest building of Huajue Lane Great mosque, can accommodate a thousand people for prayer.

Its plane is convex-shaped, seven bays wide, 32.95 m wide, nine bays deep, 38.53 m deep, with a total area of nearly 1,300 square meters; it is formed by two single-eave hip-and-gable roofs joined together, with a conical gilded top and carved lattice hall doors on exposed columns.

The inner gable walls on both sides in front of the hall are huge brick-carved flower murals. Adjacent eave columns use double columns, with north-south drainage gutters on top.

The beam frame of the main hall does not raise the gold columns, and dougong is still used at beam-column joints, similar to Song dynasty hall practices, seemingly maintaining the Ming dynasty original state. Ceilings are made under the beam frame.

The搭接 of the rear 'tiger tail' and the front part adopts the 'large lintel' method at the front rear eave, with the large lintel bearing the beam frame, to adapt to the situation where the rear tiger tail and front beams and columns do not align.

The outer eave dougong is in the five-cai double-ang style; the rear connects to the rear窑殿 three bays deep and wide, where the Lord is worshipped, with peacock blue glazed tiles on the roof.

There are over 600 caisson ceiling paintings in the main hall, all composed of Arabic script and vine patterns in团花 designs.

On the west wall inside the hall, there is a concave wall (mihrab), and to its front left is the preaching platform, for the imam to stand on when preaching; this is the direction of worship for believers, i.e., the direction of the holy city of Mecca, which is also why the mosque faces east from west.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 又名东大寺
  • 为国内占地规模最大的清真寺
  • 1988年公布为第三批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 据创建清真寺碑记记载该寺建于唐天宝元年
  • 明洪武二十五年敕谕碑记原称礼拜寺
  • 明成化十八年奏请改寺名为敕赐清修寺
  • 明嘉靖元年重修
  • 明万历三十四年大修
  • 清乾隆年间的重修和扩建形成当今的格局
  • 民国三十六年重修
  • 化觉巷清真寺坐西朝东
  • 占地面积约1.3万平方米
  • 建筑面积6000多平方米
  • 东西长245.68米
  • 南北宽47.56米

52. 西安大学习巷清真寺

Xi'an Daxuexi Lane Mosque
明、清 伊斯兰教建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安鼓楼西大街北侧大学习巷中段

大学习巷清真寺位于西安鼓楼西大街北侧大学习巷中段。1956年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

— ENGLISH —

Daxuexi Lane mosque is located in the middle section of Daxuexi Lane, north of West Gulou Street, Xi'an. It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1956.

53. 西乡鹿龄寺

Xixiang Luling Temple
清 伊斯兰教建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 陕西省汉中市西乡县城西

鹿龄寺位于陕西省汉中市西乡县城西，系中国伊斯兰教嘎的林耶道祖祁静一归真后埋葬之地，中国伊斯兰教卡迪林耶派三大圣地之一。2003年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

鹿龄寺始建于清康熙六十年（1721年），祁静一归真后，葬其遗体于悠久亭，后又改为鹿龄寺。清同治元年（1862年），不幸毁于兵事。

同治七年（1868年），重修墓亭，建客厅、住宅等50余间及三棂牌楼一座，至光绪十一年（1885年）才告修竣牌坊工程，恢复了原貌。1960年代期间，鹿龄寺遭受了极大的破坏，建筑群坍塌及荒废。

1980年代，鹿龄寺历时五年，进行了三次维修，始恢复景观和建筑面貌。

鹿龄寺结合陇南和陕南的建筑特点，融合了中国传统样式和伊斯兰的特色，是陕南地区最大的清真寺，其石雕和砖雕艺术堪称一绝，该寺占地面积达100余亩，总体布局为传统寺庙的形制，院落沿南北纵深布置，原大寺区由仙根寺、静思寺、鹿龄寺三寺组成，现为鹿龄寺的主要部分。

鹿龄寺三进院落沿南北中轴线展开，依次布置照壁、牌坊、大门、墓庐等。嵌有鹿龄寺匾额的牌坊处于第一进和第二进院落之间，为三间四柱三楼木牌坊，主楼高约8.9米，两侧次楼高约5.8米，庑殿顶出檐深远，起翘优美，檐下斗拱呈倒金字塔形排列，层层迭出，富有韵律。

大门内为正殿前院，东西墙都有水磨对缝方形砖雕图案，东墙砖刻书斋图，西墙砖刻松月牡丹图。最后院落为月台抱厦和礼拜殿。抱厦房各一间，单檐卷棚顶，与大殿毗连。礼拜殿：平面为方形，坐落在宽大的台基上，面阔与进深均为三间。殿身内四根高大的金柱托起梁架，形成重檐盔顶的屋顶形式。外檐为七踩斗拱，疏密相宜。各层拱翘做直角卷杀并斜向出跳，手法独具特色。额枋多为镂空雕饰，华美富丽。

大殿的后面覆以照壁作为结束。礼拜殿东侧为刷达院，即阿訇墓地。墓庐悠久亭：鹿龄寺最核心的建筑，坐北朝南，建筑平面呈倒凸字形，由卷棚和八卦亭组成。八卦亭为单层重檐盔顶，与前方的拜殿屋顶以勾搭方式相连接，屋顶覆盖灰色筒瓦，屋脊设有精美砖雕，中央设圆形塔式脊刹，整个屋檐曲线优美。

— ENGLISH —

Luling Temple is located west of Xixiang County town, Hanzhong City, Shaanxi Province. It is the burial place of Qi Jingyi, the Daozu (Patriarch) of the Qadiriyya order of Chinese Islam after his passing, and one of the three major holy sites of the Qadiriyya school of Chinese Islam.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2003. Luling Temple was first built in the 60th year of Kangxi, Qing (1721); after Qi Jingyi passed away, his remains were buried in the Youjiu (Eternal) Pavilion, later renamed Luling Temple.

In the 1st year of Tongzhi, Qing (1862), it was unfortunately destroyed by war.

In the 7th year of Tongzhi (1868), the tomb pavilion was rebuilt, with over 50 rooms including living rooms and guest halls, and a three-lintel archway built; it was not until the 11th year of Guangxu (1885) that the archway construction was completed, restoring the original appearance.

During the 1960s, Luling Temple suffered great destruction, with the building complex collapsing and falling into disuse. In the 1980s, Luling Temple underwent three repairs over five years, finally restoring its landscape and architectural appearance.

Luling Temple combines the architectural features of southern Gansu and southern Shaanxi, integrating traditional Chinese styles and Islamic characteristics; it is the largest mosque in southern Shaanxi, with its stone carving and brick carving art renowned as exceptional.

The temple covers over 100 mu, with the overall layout in the traditional temple form, courtyards arranged along the north-south depth; the original large temple area consisted of three temples: Xiangen Temple, Jingsi Temple, and Luling Temple, now the main part of Luling Temple.

Luling Temple's three courtyards展开 along the north-south central axis, sequentially arranging the screen wall, archway, gate, tomb, etc.

The archway embedded with the 'Luling Temple' plaque is between the first and second courtyards; it is a three-bay four-column three-floor wooden archway, with the main tower about 8.9 m high and the side towers about 5.8 m high; the hip-and-gable roof has deep eaves and elegant upturned corners, with dougong under the eaves arranged in an inverted golden pagoda shape,层层 protruding, full of rhythm.

Inside the gate is the front courtyard of the main hall; both east and west walls have water-polished butt-jointed square brick carving patterns, with the east wall carved with a 'Study Room' pattern and the west wall carved with a 'Pine, Moon, and Peony' pattern.

The last courtyard is the platform, front porch, and prayer hall. The front porch rooms are one each, with single-eave curved roof, adjacent to the main hall. Prayer Hall: square in plan, sitting on a wide platform base, with both width and depth of three bays.

Inside the hall, four tall gold columns support the beam frame, forming a double-eave helmet-shaped roof. The outer eaves use seven-cai dougong, appropriately dense and sparse. Each layer of arch-qiao is made with right-angle curved cuts and diagonal jumps, with unique techniques.

The lintels are mostly openwork carvings, gorgeous and rich. The rear of the main hall is covered with a screen wall as the ending. East of the prayer hall is the Shuada Courtyard, i.e., the imam cemetery.

Tomb Youjiu Pavilion: the core building of Luling Temple, facing south from north, with an inverted convex plane, composed of a curved-roof porch and an octagonal pavilion.

The octagonal pavilion has a single-layer double-eave helmet-shaped roof, connected to the front worship hall roof by a搭接 method; the roof is covered with gray semi-cylindrical tiles, with exquisite brick carvings on the ridge and a circular pagoda-shaped ridge finial in the center; the entire eave curve is elegant.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 系中国伊斯兰教嘎的林耶道祖祁静一归真后埋葬之地
  • 中国伊斯兰教卡迪林耶派三大圣地之一
  • 2003年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 鹿龄寺始建于清康熙六十年
  • 祁静一归真后葬其遗体于悠久亭
  • 后又改为鹿龄寺
  • 清同治元年不幸毁于兵事
  • 同治七年重修墓亭
  • 建客厅、住宅等50余间及三棂牌楼一座
  • 至光绪十一年才告修竣牌坊工程
  • 恢复了原貌
  • 1960年代期间鹿龄寺遭受了极大的破坏
  • 建筑群坍塌及荒废
  • 1980年代鹿龄寺历时五年进行了三次维修
  • 始恢复景观和建筑面貌

54. 蓝田水陆庵

Lantian Shilu Temple
隋、唐、明 佛教寺庙 全国重点文物保护单位 西安市蓝田县

水陆庵位于西安市蓝田县，以泥塑闻名，有中国第二敦煌之美誉。1996年公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位。

据《蓝田县志》及碑所记载，水陆庵始建于隋文帝开皇年间（公元581~591年），为隋唐时期著名佛寺悟真寺里的水陆殿。唐代发展成为可容僧千人的佛教寺院。及至明代，秦藩王奉为家祀佛堂，后为了纪念其母，将原名水陆殿改名为水陆庵。

水陆庵山门朝东，中间为三间过殿，后院正中为五开间的大雄宝殿，两侧各有一排13间的厢房，为三进院落的总体布局。寺院占地6800平方米，建筑面积2100平方米，共有房舍46间。

山门殿五开间青瓦歇山顶，中殿为三开间弥勒殿，大殿为新建五开间庑殿顶。诸圣水陆殿：是水陆庵的主殿。建筑平面约为方形，各边长约为16米，格局为中央置佛坛，佛坛背后有中隔壁，南山墙和北山墙向西三分之二处设有南隔壁和北隔壁，南、北、中三个隔壁将大殿分为前后两个部分，即前殿、后殿。

前殿造像以佛为主，后殿造像以菩萨为主。诸圣水陆殿融绘画、圆雕、浮雕、楼刻艺术为一体，在墙、梁、柱上展现出3700多尊人物及自然界万物的塑像。整体布局严整，惟妙惟肖，是国内目前保存最大的壁塑群。

水陆庵大殿内的彩色泥塑相传从明嘉靖四十二年到隆庆元年（1563~1567年），由著名雕塑家杨惠之用五年时间，在原庙宇基础之上重整壁塑制作而成。

— ENGLISH —

Shilu Temple is located in Lantian County, Xi'an, famous for its clay sculptures, with the reputation of 'the Second Dunhuang of China.' It was designated as the fourth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1996.

According to the 'Lantian County Chronicle' and stele records, Shilu Temple was first built during the Kaihuang period of Emperor Wen of Sui (581-591 AD), as the Shilu Hall in the famous Buddhist temple Wuzhen Temple of the Sui-Tang period.

In the Tang dynasty, it developed into a Buddhist monastery that could accommodate a thousand monks.

By the Ming dynasty, the Prince of Qin藩 enshrined it as a family worship Buddhist hall; later, to commemorate his mother, the original name 'Shilu Hall' was changed to 'Shilu Temple' (Shilu Nunnery).

Shilu Temple's mountain gate faces east, with a three-bay passage hall in the middle; in the center of the rear courtyard is the five-bay Mahavira Hall, with a row of 13-bay wing rooms on each side, in a three-courtyard overall layout.

The temple covers 6,800 square meters, with a building area of 2,100 square meters and a total of 46 rooms. The mountain gate hall has five bays with blue tile hip-and-gable roof; the middle hall is a three-bay Maitreya Hall; the main hall is a newly built five-bay hip roof.

All Saints Shilu Hall: the main hall of Shilu Temple.

The building plane is approximately square, with each side about 16 m long; the layout has a Buddhist altar in the center, with a middle partition wall behind the altar; at two-thirds west of the south and north gable walls, south and north partition walls are set; the south, north, and middle partition walls divide the main hall into front and rear parts, i.e., front hall and rear hall.

The front hall statues are mainly Buddhas, and the rear hall statues are mainly Bodhisattvas. The All Saints Shilu Hall integrates painting, round sculpture, relief, and carving arts, displaying over 3,700 statues of figures and all things in nature on the walls, beams, and columns.

The overall layout is rigorous and lifelike, and it is the largest preserved wall sculpture group in China currently.

The colored clay sculptures in the main hall of Shilu Temple are said to have been made from the 42nd year of Jiajing to the 1st year of Longqing, Ming (1563-1567), by the famous sculptor Yang Hui over five years, reorganizing the wall sculptures on the basis of the original temple.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 泥塑闻名
  • 有中国第二敦煌之美誉
  • 1996年公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 据蓝田县志及碑所记载
  • 水陆庵始建于隋文帝开皇年间
  • 为隋唐时期著名佛寺悟真寺里的水陆殿
  • 唐代发展成为可容僧千人的佛教寺院
  • 及至明代秦藩王奉为家祀佛堂
  • 后为了纪念其母
  • 将原名水陆殿改名为水陆庵
  • 水陆庵山门朝东
  • 中间为三间过殿
  • 后院正中为五开间的大雄宝殿
  • 两侧各有一排13间的厢房
  • 为三进院落的总体布局

55. 佳县白云观

Jiaxian Baiyun Temple
宋、明、清 道教宫观 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市佳县城南5公里处的白云山上

白云观位于榆林市佳县城南5公里处的白云山上，是西北最负盛名的道教圣地，也是陕北地区保存完整、规模宏大的明清古建筑群。2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位。

据佳县县志中记载，在宋代时白云山上已经有三清殿存在了。形成规模的时候却是在明万历三十三年（1605年），建成了包括真武大殿、钟鼓楼、玉皇阁、文昌楼等建筑。万历四十五年（1617年）补修《道藏》。

万历四十六年（1618年）皇帝颁圣旨颁赐御制道藏经4726卷，使其成为中国西北最大的道教圣地。清雍正二年（1724年）重修并增建。后经历代续建补葺，逐步建成了以道为主，兼有儒、释庙宇的宏大宫观。

— ENGLISH —

Baiyun (White Cloud) Temple is located on Baiyun Mountain, 5 km south of Jiaxian County town, Yulin City. It is the most prestigious Taoist holy land in the northwest and also a well-preserved, large-scale Ming-Qing ancient building complex in northern Shaanxi.

It was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001. According to the Jiaxian County Chronicle, a Sanqing Hall already existed on Baiyun Mountain in the Song dynasty.

It was when it reached scale in the 33rd year of Wanli, Ming (1605), that buildings including the Zhenwu (True Martial) Main Hall, Bell and drum towers, Jade Emperor Pavilion, and Wenchang Tower were built.

In the 45th year of Wanli (1617), the 'Daozang' (Taoist Canon) was supplemented and repaired. In the 46th year of Wanli (1618), the emperor issued an imperial decree bestowing 4,726 volumes of the imperially commissioned Taoist Canon, making it the largest Taoist holy land in northwest China.

It was rebuilt and expanded in the 2nd year of Yongzheng, Qing (1724). Later, through successive dynasties' continued construction and repairs, it was gradually built into a grand temple complex dominated by Taoism, with Confucian and Buddhist temples as well.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 是西北最负盛名的道教圣地
  • 也是陕北地区保存完整、规模宏大的明清古建筑群
  • 2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 据佳县县志中记载
  • 在宋代时白云山上已经有三清殿存在了
  • 形成规模的时候却是在明万历三十三年
  • 建成了包括真武大殿、钟鼓楼、玉皇阁、文昌楼等建筑
  • 万历四十五年补修道藏
  • 万历四十六年皇帝颁圣旨颁赐御制道藏经4726卷
  • 使其成为中国西北最大的道教圣地
  • 清雍正二年重修并增建
  • 后经历代续建补葺
  • 逐步建成了以道为主兼有儒释庙宇的宏大宫观

56. 榆林盘龙山古建筑群

Yulin Panlong Mountain Ancient Building Complex
明、清 道教宫观 全国重点文物保护单位 榆林市米脂县盘龙山山腰

盘龙山古建筑群位于榆林市米脂县盘龙山山腰，饮马河西北侧，因崇祯十六年（1643年）李自成曾返乡驻扎，故又被称为李自成行宫。2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位。建筑群始建于明朝成化年间。

清乾隆四十三至五十六年（1778~1791年）扩建。光绪十五至二十一年（1889~1895年）复修。

1927年以后，盘龙山建筑被改作中学校舍，文化大革命中局部破坏。1978年复修。1983~1984年在此建李自成纪念馆和文物陈列室。

1988年，彩绘装饰。1989年，局部维修。

兆庆宫：位于顶院末端，砖木阁楼，高11.5米，面阔五间8.95米，进深5.55米，内外双柱支撑，四架梁，雕刻雀替、彩绘枋桁装饰，双昂斗拱挑檐，筒瓦悬山顶，雕花镂脊，驮兽、正吻。主楼两侧各有耳房一间。

— ENGLISH —

Panlong Mountain Ancient Building Complex is located on the mountainside of Panlong Mountain, Mizhi County, Yulin City, on the northwest side of the Yinma River.

Because Li Zicheng once returned to his hometown and stationed here in the 16th year of Chongzhen, Ming (1643), it is also called the Li Zicheng Temporary Palace. It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006.

The building complex was first built in the Chenghua period of the Ming dynasty. It was expanded from the 43rd to 56th year of Qianlong, Qing (1778-1791), and repaired from the 15th to 21st year of Guangxu (1889-1895).

After 1927, the Panlong Mountain buildings were converted into a middle school, with partial damage during the Cultural Revolution. They were repaired in 1978. From 1983 to 1984, the Li Zicheng Memorial Hall and Cultural relics Exhibition Room were built here.

In 1988, painted decoration was done. In 1989, partial repairs were made.

Zhaoqing Palace: located at the end of the top courtyard, a brick-wood pavilion, 11.5 m high, five bays wide (8.95 m), 5.55 m deep, with internal and external double columns supporting, four-rafter beams, carved sparrow braces, painted lintel decorations, double-ang dougong eaves, semi-cylindrical tile overhanging gable roof, carved openwork ridges, carrying beasts, and main ornaments.

There is one ear room on each side of the main building.

  • 因崇祯十六年李自成曾返乡驻扎

  • 2006年公布为第六批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 建筑群始建于明朝成化年间
  • 清乾隆四十三至五十六年扩建
  • 光绪十五至二十一年复修
  • 1927年以后盘龙山建筑被改作中学校舍

  • 1978年复修
  • 1983至1984年在此建李自成纪念馆和文物陈列室
  • 1988年彩绘装饰
  • 1989年局部维修

57. 安康白云寺

Ankang Baiyun Temple
唐、明、清 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 安康市西南约18公里处的天柱山上

白云寺位于安康市西南约18公里处的天柱山上，是陕南佛教圣地四大丛林之一，也是安康著名八景之一。1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

寺庙始建于唐麟德二年（公元665年），明末毁于兵灾，清康熙五十年（1716年）重修，乾隆二十年（1755年）安康总督上山求雨如愿，遂扩寺庙。白云寺占地百余亩，坐北朝南。

寺庙由三进四塔、一炉五殿、九洞三院组成，共有殿宇、厢房60余间。中轴线上依次为天王殿、观音殿、大雄宝殿。大雄宝殿右侧为养心殿，后左侧有禅堂，禅堂左右有厢房，均系清代建筑。真武祖师殿石碑最高处三进佛殿的佛像，均为明代石雕精品。寺后有莲花池，前有方池。山北峭壁处有自然形成的九个洞，洞洞相通。

四座灵骨塔均高两丈余，系该寺宋至清代的灵骨。现寺内存碑三十余块，皆为善信捐资重修内容，唯《天柱山庙公议戒律条规》碑为重要，寺东山坡及山坳存舍利塔四座，是安康迄今所知保留舍利塔最多者，其东山一塔高五层，第三层上浮雕释迦牟尼造像，保存基本完整。

— ENGLISH —

Baiyun (White Cloud) Temple is located on Tianzhu Mountain, about 18 km southwest of Ankang City. It is one of the four major Buddhist holy land monasteries in southern Shaanxi and also one of the famous Eight Scenes of Ankang.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992.

The temple was first built in the 2nd year of Linde, Tang (665 AD), destroyed by war disaster at the end of Ming, rebuilt in the 50th year of Kangxi, Qing (1716), and in the 20th year of Qianlong (1755), the Ankang Governor went up the mountain to pray for rain and his wish was fulfilled, so he expanded the temple.

Baiyun Temple covers over a hundred mu, facing south from north. The temple consists of three courtyards, four pagodas, one furnace, five halls, nine caves, and three institutes, with over 60 halls and wing rooms.

On the central axis are sequentially the Heavenly King Hall, Guanyin Hall, and Mahavira Hall.

To the right of the Mahavira Hall is the Yangxin (Nourishing Heart) Hall, and to the rear left is the meditation hall, with wing rooms on the left and right of the meditation hall, all Qing dynasty buildings.

The Buddha statues in the three-entry Buddha halls at the highest point of the Zhenwu Patriarch Hall stele are all Ming dynasty stone carving masterpieces. Behind the temple is a lotus pond, and in front is a square pond.

At the cliff on the north side of the mountain, there are nine naturally formed caves, connected to each other. The four spirit bone pagodas are all over two zhang high, containing the spirit bones of this temple from Song to Qing.

Currently, over thirty steles are preserved in the temple, all recording donations from believers for rebuilding; only the 'Tianzhu Mountain Temple Publicly Agreed Precepts and Regulations' stele is important.

On the east mountain slope and mountain col, four relic pagodas are preserved, the most known preserved relic pagodas in Ankang to date; one pagoda on the east mountain is five stories high, with a relief sculpture of Sakyamuni on the third story, basically intact.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 是陕南佛教圣地四大丛林之一
  • 也是安康著名八景之一
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 寺庙始建于唐麟德二年
  • 明末毁于兵灾
  • 清康熙五十年重修
  • 乾隆二十年安康总督上山求雨如愿遂扩寺庙
  • 白云寺占地百余亩
  • 坐北朝南
  • 寺庙由三进四塔、一炉五殿、九洞三院组成
  • 共有殿宇、厢房60余间
  • 中轴线上依次为天王殿、观音殿、大雄宝殿
  • 大雄宝殿右侧为养心殿
  • 后左侧有禅堂
  • 禅堂左右有厢房

58. 横山县法云寺

Hengshan Fayun Temple
唐、明、清 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 榆林市横山县殿市镇五龙山村

法云寺位于榆林市横山县殿市镇五龙山村，俗称五龙山庙群。寺院内庙宇楼阁重重、院落错综相连，其规模宏大、布局有序，是陕北地区较为典型的古代建筑群落实例。2008年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

寺院始建于唐代开元二年（公元714年），经历明万历、崇祯、清康熙、乾隆时陆续扩建，遂形成现状。法云寺选址于五龙山余脉，山体高约70米，形似神龟。寺院四面临沟，居高临下、气势雄伟。

法云寺坐东北面西南，占地面积达9600平方米。寺院分前、中、后、左、右五组院落，上、中、下三层。

其中，楼亭台阁7座，前后大殿10座，三合院7处，各种小庙堂舍30多处，禅堂、经窑3处，大小高低塔7座，会窑耳房数间，布局和谐自然，上下左右庙宇浑然一体。寺院前部由山门、五龙照壁、大雄宝殿、真武祖师殿、五佛殿、文昌殿等12座建筑组成。山门为洞式，两侧各踞石狮。

五龙照壁居山门后2米正中，壁面砖石，上雕刻五龙盘踞，装饰着青蓝点金，重檐彩顶，毁于兵革年代。真武祖师殿面阔三间，进深一间，为枕头窑洞式砖木结构，顶为卷棚硬山单檐勾连搭灰瓦顶。大雄宝殿与真武祖师殿相连，内立庙志碑数通。

文昌殿位于建筑群中部，高8米，石结构，正方形，正面有进深4.5米、高3米的窑洞式文庙，洞口上方石雕青云二字，下款乾隆二十六年。

右边设石阶至魁星奇楼，其高3米，砖木结构，柱为8根，屋顶为八角攒尖式。寺院后部经四大天王殿内石台阶进入，是由孔子殿、仙公殿、禅堂等建筑组合的窑洞式四合院。再由石台阶进入三佛殿，该殿为后部分的主体建筑，面阔三间，前有月台，内为拱窑式，屋顶单檐歇山式，飞檐高耸，斗拱密布。

— ENGLISH —

Fayun Temple is located in Wulongshan Village, Dianshi Town, Hengshan County, Yulin City, commonly known as the Wulong Mountain Temple Group.

Within the temple, temple pavilions and towers are numerous, courtyards are intricately connected, with a grand scale and orderly layout; it is a typical example of ancient building complexes in northern Shaanxi.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2008.

The temple was first built in the 2nd year of Kaiyuan, Tang (714 AD), and was successively expanded during the Wanli and Chongzhen periods of Ming, and the Kangxi and Qianlong periods of Qing, gradually forming its current state.

Fayun Temple is located on the remainder of Wulong Mountain, with the mountain body about 70 m high, shaped like a divine turtle. The temple faces gullies on all four sides, commanding a high position, with a majestic momentum.

Fayun Temple faces southwest from northeast, covering an area of 9,600 square meters. The temple is divided into five groups of courtyards: front, middle, rear, left, and right, with three layers: upper, middle, and lower.

Among them, there are 7 pavilions, terraces, and towers, 10 front and rear main halls, 7 three-sided courtyards, over 30 various small temple halls and rooms, 3 meditation halls and scripture caves, 7 pagodas of various sizes and heights, and several meeting cave side rooms; the layout is harmonious and natural, with the upper, lower, left, and right temples integrated as one.

The front part of the temple consists of 12 buildings such as the mountain gate, Wulong screen wall, Mahavira Hall, Zhenwu Patriarch Hall, Five Buddha Hall, and Wenchang Hall. The mountain gate is cave-style, with stone lions on each side.

The Wulong screen wall is located 2 m behind the mountain gate in the center, with brick and stone wall surface, carved with five coiled dragons, decorated with blue and gold dots, and a double-eave colored top, destroyed in wartime.

The Zhenwu Patriarch Hall is three bays wide and one bay deep, a pillow-cave style brick-wood structure, with a curved-roof hard mountain single-eave joined gray tile roof. The Mahavira Hall is connected to the Zhenwu Patriarch Hall, with several temple record steles inside.

The Wenchang Hall is located in the middle of the building complex, 8 m high, stone structure, square, with a cave-style Confucian temple 4.5 m deep and 3 m high on the front; above the cave entrance, the two characters 'Qingyun' (Blue Cloud) are stone-carved, with the lower inscription '26th year of Qianlong.' On the right, stone steps lead to the Kuixing Qilou, 3 m high, brick-wood structure, with 8 columns, and an octagonal pyramidal roof.

The rear part of the temple is entered through stone steps inside the Four Heavenly Kings Hall, and is a cave-style courtyard composed of buildings such as the Confucius Hall, Xiangong Hall, and meditation hall.

Then through stone steps enters the Three Buddha Hall, which is the main building of the rear part, three bays wide, with a platform in front, an arched cave inside, and a single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with high flying eaves and dense dougong.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 俗称五龙山庙群
  • 寺院内庙宇楼阁重重
  • 院落错综相连
  • 其规模宏大
  • 布局有序
  • 是陕北地区较为典型的古代建筑群落实例
  • 2008年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 寺院始建于唐代开元二年
  • 经历明万历、崇祯、清康熙、乾隆时陆续扩建
  • 遂形成现状
  • 法云寺选址于五龙山余脉
  • 山体高约70米
  • 形似神龟
  • 寺院四面临沟
  • 居高临下

59. 榆林梅花楼

Yulin Meihua Tower
唐、明、清 佛教寺庙 陕西省重点文物保护单位 榆林城内普惠泉西原寿宁寺北大院

梅花楼位于榆林城内普惠泉西原寿宁寺北大院，2008年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。寿宁寺相传为唐建，明代亦称天界寺，明正统年间重修后改名寿宁寺。明万历十年（1582年）正殿重修后，又称梅花楼。

原梅花楼内有石雕十八罗汉，以及明成化十年（1471年）铸造的铜鼎等物，现已不存在。梅花楼院内现尚存40余间古庙建筑，大部分改为居室，但其建筑物仍存有许多彩画。梅花楼：为典型的单檐双层歇山顶式砖木结构，上为藏经楼，下为罗汉堂。

一层为三开间通堂砖拱大殿，东西设有小耳室，四周环建外廊，东西17.3米，南北12.8米，高4.5米；12根廊柱各柱距3米，东侧廊道内设门洞，楼梯通达二层。

二层阁楼三开间为木梁架结构，东西10.24米，南北5米，廊深2.8米；12根廊柱环列。上层歇山顶置琉璃兽头，飞檐翘角，檐下彩绘，琉璃兽脊、浮雕木刻基本完好。

楼南院中原铁旗杆一对，铁狮子一对，现已无存。楼东大门上有天桥，通向殿院。整组建筑规模、用材都很小。大木为小式柱梁作，不施斗拱，构架为前后抱头梁对五架梁，平梁上叉手与蜀柱并用。万佛楼的特色，在于它处在过街楼台上特殊的环境和狭小的台面，展示出一组完整的建筑布局。在群体造型上有主有次，轮廓丰富，构成榆林城的显著街景。

— ENGLISH —

Meihua (Plum Blossom) Tower is located in the North Great Courtyard of the former Shouning Temple, west of Puhui Spring in Yulin City. It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2008.

Shouning Temple is said to have been built in the Tang dynasty, also called Tianjie Temple in the Ming dynasty; after being rebuilt during the Zhengtong period of Ming, it was renamed Shouning Temple.

After the main hall was rebuilt in the 10th year of Wanli, Ming (1582), it was also called Meihua Tower. Originally, inside Meihua Tower there were stone-carved Eighteen Arhats and a bronze tripod cast in the 10th year of Chenghua, Ming (1471), etc., which no longer exist.

Over 40 ancient temple buildings still remain in the Meihua Tower courtyard, most converted into living quarters, but their buildings still retain many colored paintings.

Meihua Tower: a typical single-eave double-story hip-and-gable roof brick-wood structure, with the Scripture Library on top and the Arhat Hall below.

The first floor is a three-bay通 hall brick-arch main hall, with small side rooms on the east and west, and surrounding outer corridors on all four sides; 17.3 m east-west, 12.8 m north-south, 4.5 m high; 12 corridor columns with 3 m spacing each, with a door opening in the east corridor, and stairs leading to the second floor.

The second floor attic is three bays of wooden beam-frame structure, 10.24 m east-west, 5 m north-south, with 2.8 m deep corridors; 12 corridor columns surround it.

The upper hip-and-gable roof has glazed beast heads, with flying eaves and upturned corners, colored paintings under the eaves, and glazed beast ridges and relief wood carvings basically intact.

In the south courtyard of the tower, there were originally a pair of iron flagpoles and a pair of iron lions, which no longer exist. On the east gate of the tower, there is a sky bridge leading to the hall courtyard. The entire building group is small in scale and materials.

The large wood is small-style column-beam construction, without dougong; the frame is front-rear baotou beams to five-rafter beams, with both diagonal braces and short columns on the flat beam.

The characteristic of Wanfo Tower lies in its special environment on the street-crossing tower platform and the narrow platform,展示 a complete building layout. In the group modeling, there is primary and secondary, with rich silhouettes, forming a prominent streetscape of Yulin City.

  • 寿宁寺相传为唐建
  • 明代亦称天界寺
  • 明正统年间重修后改名寿宁寺
  • 明万历十年正殿重修后又称梅花楼
  • 原梅花楼内有石雕十八罗汉
  • 以及明成化十年铸造的铜鼎等物
  • 现已不存在
  • 梅花楼院内现尚存40余间古庙建筑

  • 但其建筑物仍存有许多彩画
  • 梅花楼为典型的单檐双层歇山顶式砖木结构

第五章 祠庙建筑

Chapter 5 Ancestral and Temple Architecture

60. 西安东岳庙

Xi'an Dongyue Temple
宋、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安东城门内北侧昌仁里

西安东岳庙位于西安东城门内北侧昌仁里，东邻顺城巷，南接东大街，现为陕西著名道教庙宇之一。1956年公布为陕西省第一批重点文物保护单位。东岳庙建立之初的功能为祈雨，并祭祀东岳泰山神。

据《咸宁长安两县续志》记载：庙建于北宋政和六年（1116年），元代庙宇被毁，明弘治年间（1488~1505年）、万历十年（1582年）及清光绪二十一年（1895年）都有扩充修葺，庙内保存有明清石碑数通为证。

1949年新中国成立后被昌仁里小学占用。文化大革命时期，庙内塑像被毁，石牌坊、石碑被砸。1983年，三教宫毁于火灾。2010年，东岳庙进行维修扩建。

东岳庙坐北朝南，遵从中为尊的观念，以南北向的主轴线组织山门、前殿、中殿、后殿及东西对称的廊庑。由于功能空间布局的需要，同时存在的还有其他几条次要的轴线。正殿与寝殿各自有独立的殿院，而非其他祠庙类建筑使用同一庭院的制式。由于记载西安东岳庙的资料匮乏，近代以来变化也较大。

因此，西安东岳庙正殿与寝殿各自有独立的殿院极有可能是近现代改动时，未按祠庙制度建设而形成。西安东岳庙现存大殿、中殿和后殿及东西廊庑等建筑。大殿前有石牌坊、石狮子和记载庙宇大事记的石碑等。石牌坊为明万历七年（1579年）所建，上有岱岳尊宗四字。

大殿：系明代建筑，清代修复，其大梁上有清康熙乙未腊月建题记。其平面呈长方形，面阔五间，进深三间六步架，建筑面积521平方米。双步周回廊，单檐歇山顶，殿脊、屋面施绿琉璃瓦。

梁架为彻上明造，抬梁式木构架，仍保留叉手、驼峰等做法。前后檐斗拱耍头后尾延长插在内檐垂莲柱上，垂莲柱上承下金檩，在挑檐和下金檩之间起平衡作用。外檐斗拱为七踩单抄双下昂，里转出三抄。檐面部分的斗拱在最上面昂嘴部分雕成龙形，有上下错动之分。东岳庙建筑开间大，因此，平身科在当心间用两朵，其余用一朵。

该殿共有朱色廊柱24根，前檐8根柱子柱础刻有精美的二龙戏珠浮雕图案，其他柱石皆雕花卉。大殿后檐梢间墙壁开有发券棋盘门，门颊底部有鼓形门枕石，券顶东西对称有砖刻泰华真境和东黄紫府匾。

殿内东西两壁、后壁棋盘门两侧之上满布大幅彩色道教壁画，面积达158平方米，绘制于清朝康熙五十四年（1715年），是陕西境内现存宫观壁画中单体面积最大的。

壁画内容主要为山水、楼阁、人物、花卉，题材多取自神话传说和历史故事，画工精巧，色彩斑斓，保存比较完整。中殿：又称寝宫殿。建筑面阔三间，进深两间，分心槽，占地面积196平方米，硬山顶建筑。

殿内墙壁多饰以精美砖雕，有较高的艺术欣赏价值。山墙及后檐墙面上有楼阁仕女壁画，绘制于清朝乾隆二年（1737年），壁画线条遒劲，色彩浓重，有元、明时代绘画气韵，其内容也超出宗教题材范围，反映了当时的园林建筑特色和悠闲享乐的生活情境。

后殿：面阔三间，硬山式建筑，建于2.3米的高台之上，占地面积98平方米。《微言集》记载后殿名曰老母殿，原来内塑东岳大帝父母之像，并有送子娘娘和眼光菩萨分立两侧。后殿今被用作仓库，式样已改观。偏殿：面阔三开间，进深两间，硬山顶。其为供奉神仙的殿堂，现仅存梁架，建筑墙身早已被毁坏。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Dongyue (Eastern Peak) Temple is located in Changrenli, north of the East City Gate of Xi'an, adjacent to Shuncheng Lane to the east and Dongda Street to the south. It is now one of the famous Taoist temples in Shaanxi.

It was designated as one of the first batch of Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1956. The original function of Dongyue Temple when it was established was to pray for rain and to worship the god of Mount Tai (Eastern Peak).

According to the 'Continued Chronicle of Xianning and Chang'an Counties': the temple was built in the 6th year of Zhenghe, Northern Song (1116); the temple was destroyed in the Yuan Dynasty; during the Hongzhi period (1488-1505), the 10th year of Wanli (1582) of Ming, and the 21st year of Guangxu (1895) of Qing, it was expanded and repaired, with several Ming-Qing stone steles preserved in the temple as evidence.

After the founding of New China in 1949, it was occupied by Changrenli Primary School. During the Cultural Revolution, the statues in the temple were destroyed, and the stone archways and stone steles were smashed. In 1983, the Sanjiao Palace was destroyed by fire.

In 2010, Dongyue Temple underwent maintenance and expansion. Dongyue Temple faces south from north, following the concept of 'respecting the center,' organizing the mountain gate, front hall, middle hall, rear hall, and symmetric east-west corridors along the north-south main axis.

Due to the needs of functional spatial layout, several other secondary axes also exist. The main hall and the sleeping hall each have independent temple courtyards, rather than using the same courtyard as other temple-type buildings.

Due to the scarcity of records about Xi'an Dongyue Temple, there have been significant changes in modern times. Therefore, it is very likely that the independent courtyards of the main hall and sleeping hall were formed during modern modifications without following the temple system.

Xi'an Dongyue Temple currently preserves buildings such as the main hall, middle hall, rear hall, and east-west corridors. In front of the main hall, there are stone archways, stone lions, and stone steles recording major events of the temple.

The stone archway was built in the 7th year of Wanli, Ming (1579), with the four characters 'Dai Yue Zun Zong' (Revered Ancestor of the Great Peak) on it.

Main Hall: a Ming dynasty building, repaired in Qing, with an inscription on its main beam reading 'Built in the 12th lunar month of Yiwei year of Kangxi, Qing.' Its plane is rectangular, five bays wide, three bays deep with six rafter frames, with a building area of 521 square meters.

Double-step surrounding corridors, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with green glazed tiles on the ridge and roof.

The beam frame is exposed (cheshang mingzao), with a抬梁-style wooden frame, still retaining practices such as diagonal braces (chashou) and humpback blocks (tuofeng).

The rear tails of the dougong shua-tou on the front and rear eaves extend and insert into the inner eave hanging lotus columns, which support the lower gold purlins, playing a balancing role between the挑檐 and lower gold purlins.

The outer eave dougong is seven-cai single chao double xia'ang, with interior turning out triple chao. The dougong on the eave surface has the top ang mouth carved into dragon shapes, with up-down错位.

Due to the large bay width of Dongyue Temple buildings, the pingjian-ke (intermediate bracket set) uses two sets in the central bay and one set in the rest.

This hall has a total of 24 vermilion corridor columns; the column bases of the 8 front eave columns are carved with exquisite two-dragons-playing-with-pearl relief patterns, and the other column stones are all carved with flowers.

On the rear eave end bay walls of the main hall, arched checkerboard doors are opened, with drum-shaped door sill stones at the bottom of the door jambs, and brick-carved plaques 'Taihua Zhenjing' and 'Donghuang Zifu' symmetrically on the east and west of the arch top.

On the east and west walls inside the hall, and above both sides of the checkerboard door on the rear wall, large-scale colored Taoist murals are fully covered, with an area of 158 square meters, painted in the 54th year of Kangxi, Qing (1715), which is the largest single-area mural among existing temple murals in Shaanxi.

The mural content mainly includes landscapes, pavilions, figures, and flowers, with themes mostly taken from mythological legends and historical stories, with exquisite painting skills and brilliant colors, preserved relatively intact.

Middle Hall: also called the Sleeping Palace Hall. The building is three bays wide, two bays deep, with a分心槽 (central column grid), covering an area of 196 square meters, with a hip-and-gable roof.

The walls inside the hall are mostly decorated with exquisite brick carvings, with high artistic appreciation value.

On the gable walls and rear eave walls, there are pavilion-and-lady murals, painted in the 2nd year of Qianlong, Qing (1737); the mural lines are vigorous, with rich colors, having the painting charm of the Yuan and Ming dynasties, and their content also exceeds religious themes, reflecting the garden architectural features and leisurely lifestyle of the time.

Rear Hall: three bays wide, hip-and-gable style building, built on a 2.3-m-high platform, covering an area of 98 square meters.

The 'Weiyan Ji' records that the rear hall is called the Old Mother Hall, originally containing statues of the parents of the Great Emperor of the Eastern Peak, with the Child-Giving Goddess and Eye-Guang Bodhisattva standing on both sides.

The rear hall is now used as a warehouse, and its style has changed. Side Hall: three bays wide, two bays deep, hip-and-gable roof. It was a hall for worshipping immortals; now only the beam frame remains, and the building walls have long been destroyed.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 为陕西著名道教庙宇之一
  • 1956年公布为陕西省第一批重点文物保护单位
  • 东岳庙建立之初的功能为祈雨
  • 并祭祀东岳泰山神
  • 据咸宁长安两县续志记载庙建于北宋政和六年
  • 元代庙宇被毁
  • 明弘治年间、万历十年及清光绪二十一年都有扩充修葺
  • 庙内保存有明清石碑数通为证
  • 1949年新中国成立后被昌仁里小学占用
  • 文化大革命时期庙内塑像被毁
  • 石牌坊、石碑被砸
  • 1983年三教宫毁于火灾
  • 2010年东岳庙进行维修扩建
  • 东岳庙坐北朝南
  • 遵从中为尊的观念

61. 华阴西岳庙

Huayin Xiyue Temple
汉、北魏、唐、宋、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 华阴市华山北麓5公里处

西岳庙，又称华岳庙，是专为中国古代帝王祭祀华山之神的庙宇，位于华阴市华山北麓5公里处，东距华阴市区2.5公里。1988年公布为全国重点文物保护单位。

西岳庙占地186亩，约12万平方米。历史上有华岳庙、金天王神祠等称呼，俗称为大庙、皇庙。该庙在五岳庙中规模最大，是西北地区现存最大的明清风格皇家祭祀古建园林群落，兼有帝王行宫职能。

西岳庙前身为集灵宫，创建于西汉武帝元光元年（公元前134年），初在华山黄神谷。东汉桓帝延熹八年（公元165年）迁于今址。东汉恒帝延熹八年《西岳华山庙碑》已记载修庙祭山、祈天求雨之事，北周、唐、宋、明历代均有修葺。

清乾隆四十二年（1777年）大规模扩建，历时三年完成，奠定了今日之西岳庙格局。乾隆大修后200年来岳庙灾难不断，先是同治年间回民反清，焚毁大半岳庙；民国时期国民军占驻，破坏加剧；文化大革命期间毁文物、拆古建、伐古柏。

1998年，依据乾隆时代《西岳庙图碑》进行大规模修复，重建五凤楼、御书楼、万寿阁、灵官殿、城墙角楼等建筑，再现当年西岳庙雄伟面貌。西岳庙位于华山北麓，坐北面南，中轴线正对华山主峰。

庙院整体基本沿南北轴线左右对称布局。主轴线在棂星门与望华桥等建筑节点部分有偏转现象。同时，仿照宫禁制度，设内外重城，将各组建筑群围在城中，属于围庙建城的典型实例。

外城长525米，宽225米，高约8米，南端设有瓮城。主要建筑有七龙壁、灏灵门（庙门）、钟鼓楼（已毁）、五凤楼、棂星门、石牌坊、金城门等。

内城长270米，平均宽120米，高约3米，分为前、后两院。主要建筑有水金桥、泮池、灏灵殿、寝殿、御书楼等。

庙后高台之上是万寿阁，原阁1932年毁于大火，1999年复原重建。庙院中轴线空间层次丰富，可分为三段渐高递进式。第一段是影壁、庙门、五凤楼，五凤楼为高点；第二段是棂星门、金城门、灏灵殿，灏灵殿为高点；第三段是寝殿、御书楼、万寿阁，万寿阁为高点。

三段九个节点加上最前端的遥参亭、末端的游岳坊及三座牌楼共十四个节点，在两翼配属建筑的烘托陪衬下，视觉上高低错落，抑扬顿挫。

— ENGLISH —

Xiyue (Western Peak) Temple, also called Huayue Temple, is a temple specially built for ancient Chinese emperors to worship the god of Mount Hua. It is located 5 km north of the foot of Mount Hua, Huayin City, 2.5 km east of Huayin urban area.

It was designated as a National Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1988. Xiyue Temple covers 186 mu, about 120,000 square meters. Historically, it had names such as Huayue Temple and Jin Tianwang Divine Shrine, commonly called the Great Temple or Imperial Temple.

This temple is the largest among the Five Peak Temples, and is the largest existing Ming-Qing style royal sacrificial ancient building garden complex in the northwest region, also serving as an imperial palace function.

The predecessor of Xiyue Temple was the Jiling Palace, founded in the 1st year of Yuanguang, Emperor Wu of Western Han (134 BC), initially in the Huangshen Valley of Mount Hua. In the 8th year of Yanxi, Emperor Huan of Eastern Han (165 AD), it was moved to the current site.

The 'Stele of the Western Peak Mount Hua Temple' from the 8th year of Yanxi, Emperor Huan of Eastern Han already records matters of repairing the temple, sacrificing to the mountain, and praying to heaven for rain; it was repaired in the Northern Zhou, Tang, Song, and Ming dynasties.

In the 42nd year of Qianlong, Qing (1777), a large-scale expansion was carried out, completed over three years, laying the foundation for today's Xiyue Temple layout.

In the 200 years after the Qianlong major repair, the temple suffered continuous disasters: first, during the Tongzhi period, the Hui people's anti-Qing rebellion burned down most of the temple; during the Republic period, the Nationalist Army occupied it, worsening the damage; during the Cultural Revolution, cultural relics were destroyed, ancient buildings were demolished, and ancient cypresses were cut down.

In 1998, based on the 'Stele Map of Xiyue Temple' from the Qianlong era, a large-scale restoration was carried out, rebuilding buildings such as the Five Phoenix Tower, Imperial Library Tower, Longevity Pavilion, Lingguan Hall, and city wall corner towers, reproducing the majestic appearance of Xiyue Temple in its prime.

Xiyue Temple is located at the northern foot of Mount Hua, facing south from north, with its central axis directly facing the main peak of Mount Hua. The temple courtyard overall is basically symmetrically laid out along the north-south axis.

The main axis has deflection phenomena at building nodes such as the Lingxing Gate and Wanghua Bridge.

At the same time, following the palace prohibition system, inner and outer double cities are set, enclosing each group of buildings within the city, which is a typical example of building a city around a temple.

The outer city is 525 m long, 225 m wide, about 8 m high, with an enceinte at the southern end. The main buildings include the Seven Dragon Wall, Haoling Gate (temple gate), Bell and drum towers (destroyed), Five Phoenix Tower, Lingxing Gate, Stone Archway, and Jincheng Gate.

The inner city is 270 m long, averaging 120 m wide, about 3 m high, divided into front and rear courtyards. The main buildings include the Water Gold Bridge, Pan Pool, Haoling Hall, Sleeping Hall, and Imperial Library Tower.

On the high platform behind the temple is the Longevity Pavilion; the original pavilion was destroyed by fire in 1932 and restored and rebuilt in 1999. The central axis of the temple courtyard has rich spatial layers, which can be divided into three sections of gradually rising progressive style.

The first section includes the screen wall, temple gate, and Five Phoenix Tower, with the Five Phoenix Tower as the high point; the second section includes the Lingxing Gate, Jincheng Gate, and Haoling Hall, with the Haoling Hall as the high point; the third section includes the Sleeping Hall, Imperial Library Tower, and Longevity Pavilion, with the Longevity Pavilion as the high point.

The three sections with nine nodes, plus the Yaocan Pavilion at the foremost end, the Youyue Archway at the end, and three archways, total fourteen nodes; set off by the wing附属 buildings, visually they are高低错落, with rhythmic ups and downs.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 又称华岳庙
  • 是专为中国古代帝王祭祀华山之神的庙宇
  • 1988年公布为全国重点文物保护单位
  • 西岳庙占地186亩
  • 约12万平方米
  • 历史上有华岳庙、金天王神祠等称呼
  • 俗称为大庙、皇庙
  • 该庙在五岳庙中规模最大
  • 是西北地区现存最大的明清风格皇家祭祀古建园林群落
  • 兼有帝王行宫职能
  • 西岳庙前身为集灵宫

62. 西安都城隍庙

Xi'an Capital City God Temple
明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安城内西大街与北广济街交叉口以西

西安都城隍庙位于西安城内西大街与北广济街交叉口以西，大学习巷以东，正面为西大街，北侧为庙后街，在清代理事厅署和长安县署之间。2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位。

西安都城隍庙由朱元璋二子朱樉于明洪武二十年（1387年）创建，原址在东城门内九耀街，明宣德八年（1433年）移建于今址。

清顺治年间（1644~1661年）屡次增饰重修，雍正元年（1723年）毁于火。

同年移明秦王府砖瓦木料扩充修建，乾隆、嘉庆、道光年间又屡加修葺，光绪十三年（1887年）二月，戏楼、二门及钟、鼓楼因火灾被毁，同年重建。1942年部分建筑惨遭日寇炸毁。

山门、二殿、寝殿、藏经楼、玉皇阁不同程度被炸坏。1985、2005年，先后进行大规模修复，重现了当年城隍庙的宏伟面貌。

西安都城隍庙，都字意为不仅能管辖关中地区各府、州、县城隍庙，而且西北地区各府、州、县城隍庙也在其管辖范围之内，因此与北京、南京的城隍庙并称，可见等级之高。

西安都城隍庙坐北面南，占地约9230平方米，建筑面积约4466平方米，分为庙院和道院。

庙宇总体布局采用沿轴线纵深发展与对称布局的方式，即以南端西大街五间牌楼为起点，向北经过青石马道穿文昌阁，阁东、西分立钟楼、鼓楼。从文昌阁向北进仪门，仪门两侧设东西配房，迎面乐舞楼，绕乐舞楼，面三间大牌楼，穿牌楼进大殿，仪门与大殿东西两侧设配殿各一间，配殿两侧设两厢各六间，继续向北为二殿（已毁），东西设最乾殿、藏经阁（已毁）。

过二殿即入主轴线最后一座殿堂寝殿。整条主轴线全长350余米，东西道院大致对称布局于主轴线两侧，现已无存。大殿：面阔七间，进深五间，四周回廊，单檐庑殿顶，屋顶蓝色琉璃筒瓦。台明到正脊高13.65米，台基高0.15米。

当心间面阔6.36米，次间面阔5.90米，回廊面阔2.22米。殿身进深11.40米，加上周前后廊深15.84米。七架六椽袱，正脊推山不明显；脊瓜柱设有因袭宋制的叉手，又有明清时期用以起支护作用之角背。

外檐斗拱为七踩三下昂，明间平身科用三攒，攒档较为疏朗；平板枋也较额枋为宽，用材比常见清式建筑为大，可能为移用明秦王府材料建造所致。文昌阁：始建于明洪武二十年（1387年），明宣德八年（1433年）移建于西大街，清乾隆、嘉庆、道光年间屡次重修。

建筑坐南向北，居中而设，二层前卷、后殿式建筑，面积248平方米。底层明间设人行通道，前卷部分台明到正脊高7.10米。

面阔三间，当心间面阔3.78米，次间面阔3.06米，进深两间，加卷棚抱厦三间，共4.30米。抱厦外檐斗拱，外拽出三跳，里拽出两跳，偷心造。梁架为四架，月梁下设随梁枋，彻上露明造。后殿部分台明到正脊高8.15米。

面阔三间，当心间宽度3.78米，次间宽度3.06米，进深三间，共4.88米。梁架为五架前后廊式，金檩之下，脊瓜柱之上有襻间做法。襻间斗拱，形式为一斗二升交麻叶，但与脊瓜柱柱头交接处并无构件尺寸较大的襻间枋，而是用尺寸较小的横枋和木方来代替。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Capital City God Temple is located west of the intersection of West Street and North Guangji Street inside Xi'an city, east of Daxuexi Lane, with West Street on the front and Miaohou Street on the north side, between the Qing dynasty Lishiting Office and Chang'an County Office.

It was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001.

Xi'an Capital City God Temple was founded by Zhu Shuang, the second son of Zhu Yuanzhang, in the 20th year of Hongwu, Ming (1387), originally at Jiuyao Street inside the East City Gate, and moved to the current site in the 8th year of Xuande, Ming (1433).

During the Shunzhi period of Qing (1644-1661), it was repeatedly decorated and rebuilt; in the 1st year of Yongzheng (1723), it was destroyed by fire.

In the same year, bricks, tiles, and timber from the Ming Prince of Qin's mansion were moved to expand and construct it; during the Qianlong, Jiaqing, and Daoguang periods, it was repeatedly repaired; in February of the 13th year of Guangxu (1887), the opera tower, second gate, and bell and drum towers were destroyed by fire and rebuilt in the same year.

In 1942, some buildings were tragically bombed and destroyed by Japanese invaders. The mountain gate, second hall, sleeping hall, scripture library, and Jade Emperor Pavilion were damaged to varying degrees.

In 1985 and 2005, large-scale restorations were carried out successively, reproducing the grand appearance of the City God Temple in its prime.

The character 'Du' (Capital) in Xi'an Capital City God Temple means that it not only governs the city god temples of various prefectures, departments, and counties in the Guanzhong region, but also the city god temples of various prefectures, departments, and counties in the northwest region are within its jurisdiction; therefore, it is ranked alongside the City God Temples of Beijing and Nanjing, showing its high rank.

Xi'an Capital City God Temple faces south from north, covering about 9,230 square meters, with a building area of about 4,466 square meters, divided into temple courtyard and Taoist courtyard.

The overall layout of the temple adopts the method of longitudinal development along the axis and symmetric layout, i.e., starting from the five-bay archway at West Street at the southern end, passing north through the bluestone horse path and through the Wenchang Pavilion, with bell and drum towers standing separately east and west of the pavilion.

From Wenchang Pavilion northward through the ceremonial gate, with east and west side rooms on both sides of the ceremonial gate, facing the music and dance tower; around the music and dance tower, facing the three-bay grand archway; through the archway into the main hall, with side halls one bay each on the east and west sides between the ceremonial gate and main hall, with two wings of six bays each on both sides of the side halls; continuing northward is the second hall (destroyed), with the Zuiqian Hall and Scripture Library (destroyed) on the east and west.

Passing the second hall enters the last hall on the main axis, the sleeping hall. The entire main axis is more than 350 m long; the east and west Taoist courtyards are roughly symmetrically laid out on both sides of the main axis, now no longer existing.

Main Hall: seven bays wide, five bays deep, with surrounding corridors on all sides, single-eave hip roof, with blue glazed tube tiles on the roof. From the platform base to the main ridge is 13.65 m high, and the platform base is 0.15 m high.

The central bay is 6.36 m wide, the secondary bays are 5.90 m wide, and the corridor bays are 2.22 m wide. The hall body depth is 11.40 m, plus the front and rear corridor depth of 15.84 m.

Seven-frame six-rafter beam, with unobvious main ridge push-up; the ridge melon columns have diagonal braces (chashou) inherited from the Song system, and also corner backs (jiaobei) used for support in the Ming and Qing periods.

The outer eave dougong is seven-cai triple xia'ang, with three sets of pingjian-ke in the central bay, with relatively sparse spacing; the flat plate beam is also wider than the额枋, with larger timber than common Qing-style buildings, possibly due to the use of materials moved from the Ming Prince of Qin's mansion.

Wenchang Pavilion: founded in the 20th year of Hongwu, Ming (1387), moved to West Street in the 8th year of Xuande, Ming (1433), and repeatedly repaired during the Qianlong, Jiaqing, and Daoguang periods of Qing.

The building faces north from south, set in the center, a two-story front-rolling, rear-hall style building, with an area of 248 square meters. The ground floor central bay has a pedestrian passage; the front rolling part from platform base to main ridge is 7.10 m high.

Three bays wide, with the central bay 3.78 m wide, secondary bays 3.06 m wide, two bays deep, plus three rolling-roof抱厦 bays, totaling 4.30 m. The抱厦 outer eave dougong has outer拽 triple jumps and inner拽 double jumps, with偷心造 (heart-stealing construction).

The beam frame is four-frame, with a following beam under the moon beam, with exposed construction (cheshang luming zao). The rear hall part from platform base to main ridge is 8.15 m high.

Three bays wide, with the central bay 3.78 m wide, secondary bays 3.06 m wide, three bays deep, totaling 4.88 m. The beam frame is five-frame front-rear corridor style, under the gold purlins, above the ridge melon columns there is a襻间 (inter-column tie) practice.

The襻间 dougong is in the form of one-dou-two-sheng intersecting hemp leaves, but at the junction with the ridge melon column capitals there is no larger-sized襻间 beam, instead smaller-sized transverse beams and wooden squares are used.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 西安都城隍庙由朱元璋二子朱樉于明洪武二十年创建
  • 原址在东城门内九耀街
  • 明宣德八年移建于今址
  • 清顺治年间屡次增饰重修
  • 雍正元年毁于火
  • 同年移明秦王府砖瓦木料扩充修建
  • 乾隆、嘉庆、道光年间又屡加修葺
  • 光绪十三年二月戏楼、二门及钟、鼓楼因火灾被毁
  • 同年重建
  • 1942年部分建筑惨遭日寇炸毁
  • 山门、二殿、寝殿、藏经楼、玉皇阁不同程度被炸坏
  • 1985、2005年先后进行大规模修复
  • 重现了当年城隍庙的宏伟面貌

63. 三原城隍庙

Sanyuan City God Temple
明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 三原县城内东渠街

三原城隍庙，又名威灵昭应祠，位于三原县城内东渠街。2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位。

三原城隍庙始建于明代洪武八年（1375年），成化十二年（1486年）增建寝殿等建筑，嘉靖二十九年（1550年）修建山门外青石砖雕照壁。

清乾隆二年（1757年），修建中院大戏楼、前院石牌坊、木牌坊两座。后经多次修葺和增建，遂形成现在的规模。三原城隍庙现有建筑百余间，其规模宏大，形制完整。

院落坐北朝南，纵长约300米，横宽约70米，占地面积约1.34万平方米，呈基本规则矩形。三原城隍庙建筑群中轴线从最南端开始，依次布置照壁、牌坊门、山门、前院木牌坊、石牌坊、戏楼、中院大木牌坊、拜殿、寝殿。

前院分列东西厢房，中院两侧建有钟鼓楼。其中，钟楼位于中院之西侧，鼓楼位于中院之东侧，与晨（东）钟暮（西）鼓的惯常摆设方位有所不同。传说源于城隍神为阴间的行政官，日落而作、日出而息，与阳间相反，因此方向调转。

庙门：重檐歇山顶，高15米，内檐作斗八藻井，中为阴阳太极图，小木作工艺十分精巧。庙门内为狭长的神路，神路中央分别竖立着两座牌坊。大殿：建于明洪武八年（1375年），面阔五间，进深四间，为双槽平面，单檐歇山顶。

梁架为彻上明造，抬梁式构架，五架梁上用瓜柱、角背，而太平梁上仍保留驼峰、叉手。外檐斗拱为五踩双下昂，平身科仅用两攒，攒档较为疏朗。后檐平身科耍头刻作卷草、三福云式样，后尾向上托在下金檩下，起挑杆作用；歇山顶转角为抹角梁与顺、扒梁并用，均为明代初期建筑特征的做法。

大殿与其前的拜殿呈勾连搭形式，拜殿为卷棚歇山顶。寝殿：建于明成化二十一年（1486年），面阔五间，进深四间，屋顶布琉璃筒瓦，为二层通柱式结构楼阁，重檐歇山顶，高17.25米。

梁架为彻上明造，构造特征同大殿。钟楼、鼓楼：建于明代洪武八年（1375年），面阔一间加周回廊，高二层，内布置木楼梯贯通上下，屋顶为重檐歇山十字脊，一、二层之间加一圈腰檐，琉璃脊饰。

戏楼：建于清代康熙二十二年（1757年），上下两层。下层为架空过道，上层为戏台，面阔三间，台口4.9米，两翼为侧台，单檐歇山顶。

— ENGLISH —

Sanyuan City God Temple, also called Weiling Zhaoying Shrine, is located on Dongqu Street inside Sanyuan County town. It was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001.

Sanyuan City God Temple was first built in the 8th year of Hongwu, Ming (1375); in the 12th year of Chenghua (1486), buildings such as the sleeping hall were added; in the 29th year of Jiajing (1550), a bluestone brick-carved screen wall outside the mountain gate was built.

In the 2nd year of Qianlong, Qing (1757), the grand opera tower in the middle courtyard and two stone archways and wooden archways in the front courtyard were built. After multiple repairs and additions, it eventually formed its current scale.

Sanyuan City God Temple currently has more than a hundred buildings, with a grand scale and complete form. The courtyard faces south from north, about 300 m long longitudinally and about 70 m wide transversely, covering an area of about 13,400 square meters, presenting a basically regular rectangle.

The central axis of the Sanyuan City God Temple building complex starts from the southernmost end, sequentially arranging the screen wall, archway gate, mountain gate, front courtyard wooden archway, stone archway, opera tower, middle courtyard grand wooden archway, worship hall, and sleeping hall.

The front courtyard has east and west wing rooms separately, and bell and drum towers are built on both sides of the middle courtyard.

Among them, the bell tower is located on the west side of the middle courtyard, and the drum tower is located on the east side of the middle courtyard, which differs from the customary arrangement of morning (east) bell and evening (west) drum.

Legend has it that this originates from the City God being the administrative official of the underworld, working at sunset and resting at sunrise, opposite to the yang world, so the directions are reversed.

Temple Gate: double-eave hip-and-gable roof, 15 m high, with a dou-eight caisson ceiling on the inner eave, with a yin-yang taiji diagram in the center, with very exquisite small woodwork craftsmanship.

Inside the temple gate is a narrow divine path, with two archways erected separately in the center of the divine path. Main Hall: built in the 8th year of Hongwu, Ming (1375), five bays wide, four bays deep, with a double-slot plane, single-eave hip-and-gable roof.

The beam frame is exposed (cheshang mingzao), with a抬梁-style frame; on the five-frame beam, melon columns and corner backs are used, while on the taiping beam, humpback blocks (tuofeng) and diagonal braces (chashou) are still retained.

The outer eave dougong is five-cai double xia'ang, with only two sets of pingjian-ke, with relatively sparse spacing.

The rear eave pingjian-ke shua-tou is carved in rolling grass and three-fortune cloud patterns, with the rear tail upward supporting under the lower gold purlin, acting as a挑杆; the hip-and-gable roof corner uses both mitered beams and顺/扒 beams, all of which are characteristic practices of early Ming architecture.

The main hall and the worship hall in front of it present a goudianda (interlocking roof) form, with the worship hall having a rolling-roof hip-and-gable roof.

Sleeping Hall: built in the 21st year of Chenghua, Ming (1486), five bays wide, four bays deep, with glazed tube tiles on the roof, a two-story through-column structure pavilion, double-eave hip-and-gable roof, 17.25 m high.

The beam frame is exposed (cheshang mingzao), with structural features the same as the main hall.

Bell and drum towers: built in the 8th year of Hongwu, Ming (1375), one bay wide plus surrounding corridors, two stories high, with wooden stairs inside connecting up and down, with a double-eave hip-and-gable cross ridge roof, with a一圈 waist eave between the first and second stories, with glazed ridge ornaments.

opera tower: built in the 22nd year of Kangxi, Qing (1757), two stories upper and lower.

The lower story is an elevated passage, the upper story is the stage, three bays wide, with a stage opening of 4.9 m, with side stages on both wings, single-eave hip-and-gable roof.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 又名威灵昭应祠
  • 2001年公布为第五批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 三原城隍庙始建于明代洪武八年
  • 成化十二年增建寝殿等建筑
  • 嘉靖二十九年修建山门外青石砖雕照壁
  • 清乾隆二年修建中院大戏楼、前院石牌坊、木牌坊两座
  • 后经多次修葺和增建
  • 遂形成现在的规模
  • 三原城隍庙现有建筑百余间
  • 其规模宏大
  • 形制完整
  • 院落坐北朝南
  • 纵长约300米
  • 横宽约70米
  • 占地面积约1.34万平方米

64. 韩城城隍庙

Hancheng City God Temple
唐、宋、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 韩城市古城东北隍庙巷东段

韩城城隍庙位于韩城市古城东北隍庙巷东段。寺庙南北纵长200余米，东西横宽70余米，占地1.55万平方米，平面呈不规则矩形。2001年公布为第五批国家重点文物保护单位。

韩城城隍庙创建年代应为唐宋之间，最晚也在元代。据明万历年间《韩城县志》记载，明隆庆辛未五年至壬申六年（1571~1572年），重建戏楼；

明万历五年至六年（1577~1578年）重建大殿。

明万历十八年（1590年）《韩城县志·城隍庙记》记载，彼时韩城城隍庙已有壁屏门、山门、政教坊、威明门、化育坊、广荐殿、德馨殿、灵佑殿、含光殿，配列有监察幽冥和保安黎庶两座牌坊，正殿偏后两侧建有东西道院，四进院落，中轴明显，格局完整。

清代在此基础上屡有修葺。韩城城隍庙本是模仿衙署建筑而建，采用前堂后室的形式，多层次向纵深发展。其南北主轴线长200余米，共布置有五个院落。两侧东西道院建筑大致对称，使得整体建筑布局开阔疏朗，显得尤为宏大。建筑组群中轴线以最南端的琉璃雕花照壁为起点，向北穿越壁屏门。

城隍庙巷东、西各建一木牌坊，东曰监察幽冥，西曰保安黎庶。

再循城隍庙巷北进入山门至政教坊，向北过第二道门即为威明门、化育坊（已毁），化育坊东西两侧为钟鼓楼（已毁），北面正面是广荐殿，广荐殿前曾有东西戏楼对峙，现仅存西戏楼，其北沿主轴线为拜殿（德馨殿）、献殿（灵佑殿），德馨殿前两侧有东西庑，最终抵寝殿（含光殿）为结尾。

西戏楼：主体为重檐十字歇山顶，布筒瓦，琉璃脊。除了西立面下檐中间为如意斗拱外，四面一式。高12.9米，其中台基高1.4米。

平面为单槽式样，平面横长8.3米，纵长8.95米。广荐殿：面阔五间，均为3.55米，进深7.46米，台明长19.26米，宽10.56米。

单檐悬山顶，出檐较深，布琉璃筒瓦，脊吻，次间脊上增饰楼阁。屋身与屋面比例约为1:1，檐口及正脊平直，无升起。山面采用五花山墙的做法，直接暴露山面木构架和角科斗拱。

德馨殿：面阔三间，长为15.54米，宽为10.08米，单檐歇山顶，屋顶布琉璃筒瓦，有方胜心，脊以花卉为主，间作龙，脊上间隔饰以亭阁桥梁。屋顶举折颇高，与屋身之比大于1:1。

台基为石砌，长17.67米，宽12.25米，高0.85米。台基上面墁以方砖。德馨殿前有凸字形月台，月台长12.60米，宽9.77米。

灵佑殿：紧贴于德馨殿之后，与之相隔不到2.5米，原面阔三间，后改为五间，平面为双槽形式。单檐歇山顶，屋顶与屋身的比例略大于1:1。台明长17.48米，宽11.02米。

梁架为七架六椽栿，无补间铺作，柱头斗拱五铺作出双抄，斗拱由大小额枋承托，并没有使用平板枋。含光殿：位于灵佑殿后第四院中轴正北，为寝殿。面阔五间，单檐悬山顶，梁架为五架四椽栿。高10.40米，其中台基高0.30米。

屋顶与屋身的比例略大于0.8:1，屋面布琉璃筒瓦，琉璃脊吻。

— ENGLISH —

Hancheng City God Temple is located at the eastern end of Huangmiao Lane, northeast of the ancient city of Hancheng City. The temple is more than 200 m long north-south and more than 70 m wide east-west, covering 15,500 square meters, with an irregular rectangular plane.

It was designated as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2001. The founding date of Hancheng City God Temple should be between the Tang and Song dynasties, at the latest in the Yuan Dynasty.

According to the 'Hancheng County Chronicle' from the Wanli period of Ming, in the 5th to 6th years of Longqing Xinwei-Renshen (1571-1572), the opera tower was rebuilt; in the 5th to 6th years of Wanli (1577-1578), the main hall was rebuilt.

The 'Record of Hancheng City God Temple' from the 18th year of Wanli, Ming (1590) records that at that time Hancheng City God Temple already had the wall screen gate, mountain gate, Zhengjiao Archway, Weiming Gate, Huayu Archway, Guangjian Hall, Dexin Hall, Lingyou Hall, and Hanguang Hall, with two archways 'Jiancha Youming' (Supervising the Netherworld) and 'Bao'an Lishu' (Protecting the People) arranged on both sides; on both sides behind the main hall, east and west Taoist courtyards were built, with four courtyards, a clear central axis, and a complete layout.

In the Qing dynasty, it was repeatedly repaired on this basis. Hancheng City God Temple was originally built imitating yamen (government office) architecture, adopting the form of 'front hall, rear chamber,' with multi-level longitudinal development.

Its north-south main axis is more than 200 m long, with a total of five courtyards arranged. The east and west Taoist courtyards on both sides are roughly symmetric, making the overall building layout open and spacious, appearing particularly grand.

The central axis of the building complex starts from the glazed carved screen wall at the southernmost end, passing north through the wall screen gate.

On the east and west of Huangmiao Lane, a wooden archway is built each, the east one called 'Jiancha Youming' and the west one called 'Bao'an Lishu.' Then following Huangmiao Lane north into the mountain gate to the Zhengjiao Archway, passing north through the second gate is the Weiming Gate and Huayu Archway (destroyed); on the east and west sides of the Huayu Archway are the bell and drum towers (destroyed); on the north front is the Guangjian Hall; in front of the Guangjian Hall there were once east and west opera towers facing each other, now only the west opera tower remains; north along the main axis are the worship hall (Dexin Hall) and offering hall (Lingyou Hall); on both sides in front of the Dexin Hall are east and west corridors; finally reaching the sleeping hall (Hanguang Hall) as the end.

West opera tower: the main body has a double-eave cross hip-and-gable roof, with tube tiles and glazed ridges. Except for the middle of the lower eave on the west facade being ruyi dougong, all four sides are uniform.

12.9 m high, of which the platform base is 1.4 m high. The plane is in a single-slot style, with a horizontal length of 8.3 m and a longitudinal length of 8.95 m. Guangjian Hall: five bays wide, all 3.55 m, 7.46 m deep, with a platform length of 19.26 m and width of 10.56 m.

Single-eave overhanging gable roof, with relatively deep eaves, glazed tube tiles, ridge ornaments, with pavilions added on the secondary bay ridges. The ratio of building body to roof is about 1:1, with straight eaves and main ridge, no rise.

The gable side adopts the five-flower gable wall practice, directly exposing the gable wooden frame and corner-section dougong.

Dexin Hall: three bays wide, 15.54 m long and 10.08 m wide, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with glazed tube tiles on the roof, with fang-sheng (interlocking squares) patterns, ridges mainly with flowers, interspersed with dragons, with pavilions and bridges decorated at intervals on the ridges.

The roof pitch is quite high, with a ratio greater than 1:1 to the building body. The platform base is stone-built, 17.67 m long, 12.25 m wide, 0.85 m high. The top of the platform base is paved with square bricks. In front of the Dexin Hall is a凸-shaped moon platform, 12.60 m long and 9.77 m wide.

Lingyou Hall: closely attached behind the Dexin Hall, separated by less than 2.5 m, originally three bays wide, later changed to five bays, with a double-slot plane. Single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with the ratio of roof to building body slightly greater than 1:1.

The platform is 17.48 m long and 11.02 m wide. The beam frame is seven-frame six-rafter beam, without intermediate bracket sets, with column-head dougong of five-puzuo double chao, with dougong supported by large and small额枋, without using flat plate beams.

Hanguang Hall: located due north of the central axis in the fourth courtyard behind the Lingyou Hall, serving as the sleeping hall. Five bays wide, single-eave overhanging gable roof, with a five-frame four-rafter beam frame. 10.40 m high, of which the platform base is 0.30 m high.

The ratio of roof to building body is slightly greater than 0.8:1, with glazed tube tiles on the roof and glazed ridge ornaments.

  • 东西横宽70余米
  • 占地1.55万平方米

  • 最晚也在元代
  • 据明万历年间韩城县志记载

  • 明万历五年至六年重建大殿
