  • 配列有监察幽冥和保安黎庶两座牌坊
  • 正殿偏后两侧建有东西道院
  • 四进院落

65. 西安府文庙

Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple
宋、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 西安城内府学巷

西安府文庙也称孔庙，位于西安城内府学巷。文庙建于宋，经元、明、清扩建，形成宏大规模，占地约3.19公顷。明成化年间（1466~1487年），西安府所辖长安和咸宁二县的县学迁移到孔庙西南侧，故有一庙三学（一庙指孔庙；

三学则指西安府学、长安县学、咸宁县学）之称。现为全国重点文物保护单位。《陕西通志》载：文庙在府治东南，建自宋。元至元中（1335~1340年）廉希宪修。

明正统间（1436~1449年）知府孙仁益增拓之，正殿七间，两庑各十七间。庑南为厨舍，东西各两间，前为仪门，稍南为碑亭二，两司府县官厅东西相向；又南为宰牲所，前为棂星门，门前为泮池，跨以石桥，万历庚子（1600年）巡按李思孝建；

桥前为太和元气坊，左右碑亭二，坊前为屏，东西二坊曰贤关，曰圣域。西安府文庙、学宫在空间上采用一庙三学，翼比鹏翔的布局。文庙居于中心位置，府学掖而右，即在文庙之右，紧挨文庙。

长安、咸宁二学，按其邑治所在方位，分别坐落于文庙和府学的东西两侧。西安府文庙与学宫的这种一庙三学的空间布局在全国极其少见。西安府文庙选址遵循环渭理论，择址于西安城东南隅的巽位。其选址时巧妙构思，利用高达12米的西安城南城墙为万仞宫墙，突出表现了儒学博大精深的内涵。

西安府文庙内现存古建筑可分为文庙和碑林两大部分。文庙部分古建筑大多属于明清时期，主要贯穿于中轴线上，如牌坊、泮池、棂星门、东配殿、戟门、碑亭等；碑林部分古建筑大多属于清代和民国时期，如石台孝经、展室等。

仪门：坐落在棂星门以北，面阔三间，进深两间四椽，单檐歇山顶，屋面覆黄绿色琉璃瓦。建立在一个比较低平的台基上，前段踏步三座，后设踏步一座。檐柱比较低矮，柱高仅够三十六个斗口，与清代惯用六十斗口以定柱高的制度不同。屋顶举架平缓，出檐深邃，翼角起翘舒展，有朴素稳健之风。

斗拱，用三踩单昂，明间四朵，次间两朵，攒档相当疏朗。拱嘴扁平，挺秀有劲，大小斗子皆有显著的。柱头科的昂嘴虽较平身科略宽，但座斗并未加宽。大成殿：原建筑坐落在崇台之上，前有宽广的大月台，三面环以石栏，正面设石级三道，两侧各有一道。面阔九间，进深五间，四周环廊，明间特宽，次间特窄，异乎惯例，明、次、梢间为槅扇窗，尽间为槛窗。

重檐庑殿顶，蓝、绿二色琉璃瓦覆盖。从正面看，正脊较短，四条垂脊较长，似未推山。正脊两端螭吻体态庞大，高度约2米。檐下置斗拱，下檐用三踩单昂，上檐用五踩重昂，斗拱用材尺度较大，屋顶出檐较远。可惜于1959年焚于雷火，仅存台座。

1980年代，台座也被拆除，现只在地面用刻画青石标志展示出当时柱础的位置。

— ENGLISH —

Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple, also called the Temple of Confucius, is located on Fuxue Lane inside Xi'an city. The temple was built in the Song dynasty and expanded through the Yuan, Ming, and Qing dynasties, forming a grand scale, covering about 3.19 hectares.

During the Chenghua period of Ming (1466-1487), the county schools of Chang'an and Xianning, which were under the jurisdiction of Xi'an Prefecture, were moved to the southwest side of the Confucian Temple, hence the name 'One Temple, Three Schools' (One Temple refers to the Confucian Temple; Three Schools refer to Xi'an Prefecture School, Chang'an County School, and Xianning County School).

It is now a National Key Cultural Heritage Site. The 'Shaanxi General Chronicle' records: the Confucian Temple is southeast of the prefectural government, built since the Song dynasty. In the Zhiyuan period of Yuan (1335-1340), Lian Xixian repaired it.

During the Zhengtong period of Ming (1436-1449), Prefect Sun Renyi expanded it, with the main hall seven bays wide and two corridors each seventeen bays wide.

South of the corridors are kitchen rooms, two bays each east and west; in front is the ceremonial gate, slightly south are two stele pavilions, with the official offices of the two departments and prefecture facing east-west; further south is the animal-slaughtering house, in front is the Lingxing Gate, in front of the gate is the Pan Pool, crossed by a stone bridge, built by Li Sixiao, the巡按, in the Wengzi year of Wanli (1600); in front of the bridge is the Taihe Yuanqi Archway, with two stele pavilions on the left and right; in front of the archway is a screen, with two archways on the east and west called 'Xian Guan' (Gate of the Worthy) and 'Sheng Yu' (Domain of the Sage).

Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple and the school palace adopt a spatial layout of 'One Temple, Three Schools, wings like soaring peng birds.' The Confucian Temple occupies the central position, with the prefecture school tucked to its right, i.e., to the right of the Confucian Temple, closely adjacent to it.

The Chang'an and Xianning county schools, according to the location of their county seats, are respectively located on the east and west sides of the Confucian Temple and prefecture school.

This spatial layout of 'One Temple, Three Schools' of Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple and school palace is extremely rare nationwide. The site selection of Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple follows the Huanwei theory, choosing the Xun position in the southeast corner of Xi'an city.

Its site selection was ingeniously conceived, using the 12-m-high south city wall of Xi'an as the 'Wall of Ten Thousand Rens,' prominently expressing the profound and broad connotation of Confucianism.

The existing ancient buildings in Xi'an Prefecture Confucian Temple can be divided into two major parts: the Confucian Temple and the Stele Forest.

Most of the ancient buildings in the Confucian Temple part belong to the Ming and Qing periods, mainly running along the central axis, such as archways, Pan Pool, Lingxing Gate, east side hall, halberd gate, and stele pavilions; most of the ancient buildings in the Stele Forest part belong to the Qing and Republican periods, such as the Stone Platform Classic of Filial Piety and exhibition rooms.

Ceremonial Gate: located north of the Lingxing Gate, three bays wide, two bays deep with four rafters, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with yellow-green glazed tiles on the roof.

Built on a relatively low and flat platform base, with three front steps and one rear step. The eave columns are relatively low, with column height only enough for thirty-six dou (bracket) units, different from the Qing system of commonly using sixty dou units to determine column height.

The roof pitch is gentle, with deep eaves and舒展 wing corner rises, having a simple and steady style. The dougong uses three-cai single ang, with four sets in the central bay and two sets in the secondary bays, with relatively sparse spacing.

The arch mouths are flat, straight and vigorous, with significant features on both large and small dou units. Although the ang mouth of the column-head section is slightly wider than that of the intermediate section, the seat dou is not widened.

Main Hall (Dacheng Hall): the original building sat on a high platform, with a broad large moon platform in front, surrounded by stone railings on three sides, with three stone steps on the front and one on each side.

Nine bays wide, five bays deep, with surrounding corridors on all sides; the central bay is particularly wide and the secondary bays particularly narrow, unusual from convention; the central, secondary, and end bays have lattice windows, and the outermost bays have sill windows.

Double-eave hip roof, covered with blue and green glazed tiles. Viewed from the front, the main ridge is shorter and the four diagonal ridges are longer, seemingly without push-up. The chiwen (dragon-like ornaments) at both ends of the main ridge are huge, about 2 m high.

dougong is placed under the eaves, with the lower eave using three-cai single ang and the upper eave using five-cai double ang; the dougong timber scale is relatively large, with relatively far roof eaves.

Unfortunately, it was burned by lightning fire in 1959, leaving only the platform base. In the 1980s, the platform base was also demolished; now only carved bluestone markers on the ground show the positions of the column bases at that time.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 也称孔庙
  • 位于西安城内府学巷
  • 文庙建于宋
  • 经元、明、清扩建
  • 形成宏大规模
  • 占地约3.19公顷
  • 明成化年间西安府所辖长安和咸宁二县的县学迁移到孔庙西南侧
  • 故有一庙三学
  • 一庙指孔庙
  • 三学则指西安府学、长安县学、咸宁县学之称
  • 现为全国重点文物保护单位
  • 陕西通志载文庙在府治东南
  • 建自宋
  • 元至元中廉希宪修
  • 明正统间知府孙仁益增拓之

66. 韩城文庙

Hancheng Confucian Temple
金、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 韩城旧城金城学巷内

韩城文庙位于韩城旧城金城学巷内，是全国第三大孔庙，其规模仅次于山东曲阜和北京国子监街的孔庙，现为韩城市博物馆。2001年公布为全国重点文物保护单位。据《韩城县志（卷一）》记载：学宫初，参错民居而迫隘，勘舆家叹之。

邑民杨福厚以五十金易院二区而广西南，程爱以地五亩而扩东北，学基用是始成正大，而观者俱颂鸿淑云。这是现有对韩城文庙修建历史追溯最早的记录史料，由此可知韩城文庙在金代正大以前建筑，初制简朴，与民居交错相建。

根据《大明一统志》及清代的《韩城县志》记载，文庙为明代洪武四年（1371年）在元代文庙建筑的旧址上修建而成，其后又经历了明代天顺、成化、万历和清代乾隆年间的多次重修，形成现在的规模。

韩城文庙坐北朝南，现存主体和附属建筑共22座，计78间。文庙由棂星门、戟门、大成殿、明伦堂、尊经阁等5座主体建筑和4座紧密相连的院落组成。

文庙南北中轴线长达180米，总面积约为14000平方米。建筑群以现存大成殿主体建筑为中心，自南而北，排列有影壁、棂星门、泮池、戟门、大成殿、正谊明道门、明伦堂、尊经阁。

整个建筑群由左右对称的附属建筑四进院落组成：琉璃照壁（五龙壁）、棂星门、泮池、石桥、致斋所、碑楼、碑亭、戟门为一进，现院西建有仿古式接待室五间；大成殿和东西庑为二进，现分别作为博物馆、陈列室和展厅使用；明伦堂和东西碑林、掌酒司、典库司为三进，其中除明伦堂外，全部作为博物馆展厅使用；尊经阁和东西厢房为四进，其中尊经阁现底层为陈列室，二层为库房；

院西为启圣祠东西耳房各三间。棂星门：建于明万历年间（1573~1615年），为四柱三楼木牌坊，冲天柱上安琉璃乌头。大成殿：建于凸字形的砖砌台基上，殿身面阔五间，进深三间六架椽，前廊面阔三间，单檐歇山顶，六铺作单昂斗拱。

在建筑结构的做法上，前檐柱采用大额形式的阑额，梁架采用明袱草架，歇山草架构成的收山较深。后排的两根金柱采用移柱的做法，仍保留元代建筑手法。

明伦堂：建于明代洪武四年（1371年），是文庙现存年代较早的建筑，面阔五间，进深三间六架椽，单檐硬山顶，梁架做法古拙粗犷。明间前后排金柱均做移柱。梁架为彻上明造，仍保留驼峰、叉手做法。

斗拱为四铺作出单抄，补间用一朵，但经后世翻建，构件更换较多。

尊经阁：建于明代弘治十三年（1550年），阁的基座为砖砌高台，高两层约3米，面阔三间，进深两间，底层回廊，通高两层，重檐歇山顶，单檐歇山二滴水。

梁架为彻上明造，沿用早期驼峰、叉手等构件，前檐斗拱：下檐五铺作双下昂，上檐四铺作单昂；后檐斗拱：下檐五铺作出单抄；歇山转角做法为抹角梁和扒梁并用。尊经阁为整个建筑群的焦点，将庭院空间推向高潮。

— ENGLISH —

Hancheng Confucian Temple is located on Xue Lane in Jincheng, the old city of Hancheng. It is the third largest Confucian Temple in China, with a scale second only to the Confucian Temples in Qufu, Shandong and on Guozijian Street, Beijing. It is now the Hancheng City Museum.

It was designated as a National Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2001. According to the 'Hancheng County Chronicle (Volume 1)': At the beginning, the school palace was interspersed with residential houses and cramped, which geomancers lamented.

A local resident Yang Fuhou used fifty taels of gold to exchange two courtyard areas to expand the southwest, and Cheng Ai used five mu of land to expand the northeast; thus the school foundation became upright and grand, and viewers all praised its greatness.

This is the earliest existing historical record tracing the construction history of Hancheng Confucian Temple, from which it can be known that Hancheng Confucian Temple was built before the Zhengda period of the Jin Dynasty, with a simple initial system, interspersed and built with residential houses.

According to the 'Unified Chronicles of the Great Ming' and the Qing dynasty 'Hancheng County Chronicle,' the Confucian Temple was built in the 4th year of Hongwu, Ming (1371) on the site of the Yuan Dynasty Confucian Temple, and subsequently underwent multiple repairs during the Tianshun, Chenghua, and Wanli periods of Ming and the Qianlong period of Qing, forming its current scale.

Hancheng Confucian Temple faces south from north, with a total of 22 existing main and附属 buildings, totaling 78 bays. The temple consists of 5 main buildings including the Lingxing Gate, Halberd Gate, Dacheng Hall, Minglun Hall, and Zunjing Pavilion, and 4 closely connected courtyards.

The north-south central axis of the temple is 180 m long, with a total area of about 14,000 square meters.

The building complex centers on the existing Dacheng Hall main building, and from south to north arranges the screen wall, Lingxing Gate, Pan Pool, Halberd Gate, Dacheng Hall, Zhengyi Mingdao Gate, Minglun Hall, and Zunjing Pavilion.

The entire building complex consists of four courtyards with symmetric附属 buildings: the glazed screen wall (Five-Dragon Wall), Lingxing Gate, Pan Pool, stone bridge, Zhaisuo (fasting house), stele tower, stele pavilion, and Halberd Gate form the first courtyard, with five antique-style reception rooms now built on the west side of the courtyard; the Dacheng Hall and east-west corridors form the second courtyard, now used respectively as museum, exhibition room, and exhibition hall; the Minglun Hall and east-west stele forest, Zhangjiu Department, and Dianku Department form the third courtyard, of which all except the Minglun Hall are used as museum exhibition halls; the Zunjing Pavilion and east-west wing rooms form the fourth courtyard, of which the Zunjing Pavilion's ground floor is now an exhibition room and the second floor a warehouse; on the west side of the courtyard are the Qisheng Shrine with east and west ear rooms of three bays each.

Lingxing Gate: built during the Wanli period of Ming (1573-1615), a four-column three-story wooden archway, with glazed black heads on the soaring columns.

Dacheng Hall: built on a凸-shaped brick platform base, the hall body is five bays wide, three bays deep with six rafter frames, the front porch is three bays wide, single-eave hip-and-gable roof, with six-puzuo single ang dougong.

In the structural practice, the front eave columns use a large额-style lintel, the beam frame uses exposed beams and hidden frames, with the hip-and-gable hidden frame forming a relatively deep收山.

The two rear gold columns adopt the column-shifting practice, still retaining Yuan Dynasty architectural techniques.

Minglun Hall: built in the 4th year of Hongwu, Ming (1371), it is the earliest existing building in the temple, five bays wide, three bays deep with six rafter frames, single-eave flush gable roof, with a crude and rugged beam frame practice.

The front and rear rows of gold columns in the central bay both adopt column-shifting. The beam frame is exposed (cheshang mingzao), still retaining humpback blocks (tuofeng) and diagonal braces (chashou) practices.

The dougong is four-puzuo single chao, with one set in the intermediate bay, but after later reconstructions, many components were replaced.

Zunjing Pavilion: built in the 13th year of Hongzhi, Ming (1550), the pavilion's base is a brick high platform, two stories high about 3 m, three bays wide, two bays deep, with a ground-floor corridor, two stories in total height, double-eave hip-and-gable roof, single-eave hip-and-gable two-drop water.

The beam frame is exposed (cheshang mingzao),沿用 early components such as humpback blocks and diagonal braces; front eave dougong: lower eave five-puzuo double xia'ang, upper eave four-puzuo single ang; rear eave dougong: lower eave five-puzuo single chao; the hip-and-gable corner practice uses both mitered beams and扒 beams.

The Zunjing Pavilion is the focal point of the entire building complex, pushing the courtyard space to a climax.

67. 咸阳文庙

Xianyang Confucian Temple
明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 咸阳市区中山街中段路北

咸阳文庙位于咸阳市区中山街中段路北，现为咸阳博物馆，是咸阳城区目前保存较为完整的明清古建筑群。文庙始建于明洪武四年（1371年），由县丞孔文郁主持修建。原有大殿五架、两厢各七架、戟门五架，前有杜星门。

戟门里有金水池，上架金水桥，再后有金声、玉振两门分列左右，对称布局。到1949年仅存大殿四架，中厢房十二间。现在戟门是1964年拆迁周四王庙牌楼重建而成，四柱三孔式。

1980年以后，国家拨款修葺大殿及庑房等。2006年公布为全国第六批重点文物保护单位。

咸阳文庙现为咸阳博物馆所在地，总占地12.78亩，加上偏院共有四组院落，整体坐北朝南。文庙建筑布局由南向北，依次为牌楼、前殿（原戟门处）、东西两庑、大成殿（中殿）、后殿（原明伦堂处），中殿西侧有偏院，建筑有小牌楼、偏院正殿等。

前殿，面阔五间，进深三间，歇山顶，现为秦...

— ENGLISH —

Xianyang Confucian Temple is located north of the middle section of Zhongshan Street in Xianyang urban area, now the Xianyang Museum. It is a relatively well-preserved Ming-Qing ancient building complex in Xianyang urban area.

The temple was first built in the 4th year of Hongwu, Ming (1371), presided over by County Assistant Kong Wenyu. Originally there were five main hall frames, seven frames each for the two wings, five frames for the halberd gate, with a Duxing Gate in front.

Inside the halberd gate there was a Golden Water Pool, with a Golden Water Bridge above it, and further back the Jinsheng and Yuzhen gates were arranged separately on the left and right, in a symmetric layout. By 1949, only four main hall frames and twelve middle wing rooms remained.

The current halberd gate was rebuilt in 1964 by relocating the archway of the Thursday King Temple, in a four-column three-opening style. After 1980, the state allocated funds to repair the main hall and wing rooms, etc.

It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006. Xianyang Confucian Temple is now the location of the Xianyang Museum, with a total area of 12.78 mu, plus side courtyards totaling four groups of courtyards, overall facing south from north.

The building layout of the temple from south to north is sequentially: archway, front hall (original halberd gate location), east and west corridors, Dacheng Hall (middle hall), rear hall (original Minglun Hall location); on the west side of the middle hall there is a side courtyard, with buildings such as a small archway and side courtyard main hall.

The front hall is five bays wide, three bays deep, with a hip-and-gable roof, now serving as the Qin...

68. 府谷文庙

Fugu Confucian Temple
明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 府谷县旧县城内

府谷文庙位于府谷县旧县城内，始建于明洪武十四年（1381年）。

经清乾隆三十四年（1769年）重修，光绪二年（1876年）至八年（1882年）续修，逐渐扩充为以大成殿为中心，坐北面南的轴对称群体建筑，占地面积约12000平方米，建筑面积2100平方米，是古县城内一处规模最大、保存最好的名胜古迹。

1996年公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位。府谷文庙形制为前庙后学（南庙北学），建筑空间布局以大成殿院落即庙的祭祀空间为整体建筑群落的核心，与以明伦堂院落即学的内庭空间为次中心所产生的位置关系，构成两组不同功能的院落空间形式。

府谷文庙整体采用中轴对称的布局方式，以现存大成殿主体建筑为中心，自南向北，排列有九龙壁、礼门、泮池、金水桥、棂星门、大成殿、明伦堂。整个建筑群由四进院落组成：第一进院落，前有踏步六级，礼门三间，后有影壁、泮池、金水桥建筑三处，左右各有大型花池各一个；

第二进院落，前有踏步五级，棂星门三间，后有大成殿五间，东西配殿各七间，台前正中为神道，浮雕二龙戏珠，两侧踏步七级；第三进院落，前有踏步十二级，过厅一间，后有明伦堂正殿五间，两庑各五间；第四进院落，位于整体院落的东侧，前有戏台，中有文昌祠，后有崇圣祠各一座，均为硬山式屋顶，砖木结构建筑。大成殿：面阔五间，进深三间，单檐歇山顶；

殿前月台长13米、宽5米、高1.2米，外檐斗拱为五踩双下昂，里转出双跳，耍头后尾起挑斡，上置散斗替木承下金檩，做法古朴，屋顶琉璃正吻花脊造型生动，为明代所建。

— ENGLISH —

Fugu Confucian Temple is located in the old county town of Fugu County, first built in the 14th year of Hongwu, Ming (1381).

It was repaired in the 34th year of Qianlong, Qing (1769), and continued to be repaired from the 2nd year (1876) to the 8th year (1882) of Guangxu, gradually expanding into an axis-symmetric group building centered on the Dacheng Hall, facing south from north, covering an area of about 12,000 square meters, with a building area of 2,100 square meters.

It is the largest and best-preserved scenic spot and historical site in the old county town. It was designated as the fourth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1996. The form of Fugu Confucian Temple is 'front temple, rear school' (south temple, north school).

The architectural spatial layout takes the Dacheng Hall courtyard, i.e., the sacrificial space of the temple, as the core of the overall building complex, and the positional relationship generated with the Minglun Hall courtyard, i.e., the inner courtyard space of the school, as the secondary center, constitutes two groups of courtyard space forms with different functions.

Fugu Confucian Temple overall adopts a central-axis symmetric layout, centered on the existing Dacheng Hall main building, and from south to north arranges the Nine Dragon Wall, Ritual Gate, Pan Pool, Golden Water Bridge, Lingxing Gate, Dacheng Hall, and Minglun Hall.

The entire building complex consists of four courtyards: the first courtyard has six front steps, a three-bay Ritual Gate, and three buildings behind including a screen wall, Pan Pool, and Golden Water Bridge, with a large flower pond on each side; the second courtyard has five front steps, a three-bay Lingxing Gate, and a five-bay Dacheng Hall behind, with seven-bay east and west side halls each; in front of the platform is the divine path in the center, with a relief of two dragons playing with a pearl, and seven steps on both sides; the third courtyard has twelve front steps, a one-bay passage hall, and a five-bay Minglun Hall main hall behind, with five-bay two corridors each; the fourth courtyard is located on the east side of the overall courtyard, with an opera stage in front, a Wenchang Shrine in the middle, and a Chongsheng Shrine at the rear, all with flush gable roofs and brick-wood structure.

Dacheng Hall: five bays wide, three bays deep, single-eave hip-and-gable roof; the moon platform in front of the hall is 13 m long, 5 m wide, and 1.2 m high; the outer eave dougong is five-cai double xia'ang, with interior turning out double jumps; the rear tail of the shua-tou rises as a挑斡, with a scattered dou and substitute wood supporting the lower gold purlin, with an ancient practice; the glazed main ridge ornaments and flower ridge on the roof are vividly shaped, built in the Ming dynasty.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 始建于明洪武十四年
  • 经清乾隆三十四年重修
  • 光绪二年至八年续修
  • 逐渐扩充为以大成殿为中心
  • 坐北面南的轴对称群体建筑
  • 占地面积约12000平方米
  • 建筑面积2100平方米
  • 是古县城内一处规模最大、保存最好的名胜古迹
  • 1996年公布为第四批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 府谷文庙形制为前庙后学
  • 南庙北学
  • 建筑空间布局以大成殿院落即庙的祭祀空间为整体建筑群落的核心
  • 与以明伦堂院落即学的内庭空间为次中心所产生的位置关系
  • 构成两组不同功能的院落空间形式
  • 府谷文庙整体采用中轴对称的布局方式

69. 蒲城文庙

Pucheng Confucian Temple
唐、宋、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 陕西省文物保护单位 蒲城县

蒲城文庙始建于唐贞观年间（公元627~649年），宋、元、明、清各代均有修葺。

现存大成殿、戟门、东西庑为明正德七年（1512年）重建，棂星门、六龙壁、石牌坊、泮池均建于明万历四十六年（1618年）。1992年公布为陕西省文物保护单位。

现存建筑中六龙壁高5米，长10米。正面塑有六条飞舞游动的龙，而这六条龙两个形成一组，中间以戏珠绾结整幅图，形成了六龙泳舞图。这个照壁最大的特点是它的壁身两端各建有一座石牌坊，这种建造制式在全国独此一处。在这两座牌坊上面悬挂有题写着文章祖、帝王师的两块匾额，这是时任山西巡抚的蒲城人樊东谟所题，意在赞扬孔子是文章的师祖、帝王的老师。

在石牌坊下面有两扇石门，每扇门上都刻有方孔九个，俗称石门九眼，相传只有中了状元的人才有资格从石门经过，其他人只能绕道而行。大殿面阔五间，进深三间，周回廊，1976年毁于火灾。现在的大成殿是2004年从渭南搬过来的一座真武大殿，建筑占原大成殿遗址的三分之二。

大成殿北面为明伦堂，是明三暗五的建筑形制。室内墙壁上有朱子《易系辞》和王进德所书忠、孝、节、义四个石刻大字，东西各有斋房五间，东为掌酒司，西为典库司。

— ENGLISH —

Pucheng Confucian Temple was first built during the Zhenguan period of Tang (627-649 AD), and was repaired in the Song, Yuan, Ming, and Qing dynasties.

The existing Dacheng Hall, Halberd Gate, and east-west corridors were rebuilt in the 7th year of Zhengde, Ming (1512); the Lingxing Gate, Six Dragon Wall, Stone Archway, and Pan Pool were all built in the 46th year of Wanli, Ming (1618).

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Cultural Heritage Site in 1992. Among the existing buildings, the Six Dragon Wall is 5 m high and 10 m long.

On the front, six flying and swimming dragons are molded, with these six dragons forming groups of two, and the entire picture tied together with a pearl-playing motif in the middle, forming a 'Six Dragons Swimming and Dancing' picture.

The biggest feature of this screen wall is that a stone archway is built at each end of the wall body, and this construction system is unique in the country.

On these two archways, two plaques inscribed with 'Ancestor of Literature' and 'Teacher of Emperors' are hung, which were inscribed by Fan Dongmo, a Pucheng native who was then the Governor of Shanxi, intending to praise Confucius as the ancestor of literature and the teacher of emperors.

Below the stone archways are two stone doors, with nine square holes carved on each door, commonly called 'Stone Door Nine Eyes.' According to legend, only those who passed the top imperial examination were qualified to pass through the stone door, while others could only take a detour.

The main hall is five bays wide, three bays deep, with surrounding corridors, and was destroyed by fire in 1976. The current Dacheng Hall is a Zhenwu Hall moved from Weinan in 2004, occupying two-thirds of the original Dacheng Hall site.

North of the Dacheng Hall is the Minglun Hall, with a building form of 'apparent three, hidden five.' On the interior walls are four large stone-carved characters 'Loyalty, Filial Piety, Integrity, Righteousness' written by Zhu Xi's 'Commentary on the Book of Changes' and Wang Jinde; there are five斋房 rooms on each side, with the Zhangjiu Department on the east and the Dianku Department on the west.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 始建于唐贞观年间
  • 宋、元、明、清各代均有修葺
  • 现存大成殿、戟门、东西庑为明正德七年重建
  • 棂星门、六龙壁、石牌坊、泮池均建于明万历四十六年
  • 1992年公布为陕西省文物保护单位
  • 现存建筑中六龙壁高5米
  • 长10米
  • 正面塑有六条飞舞游动的龙
  • 而这六条龙两个形成一组
  • 中间以戏珠绾结整幅图
  • 形成了六龙泳舞图
  • 这个照壁最大的特点是它的壁身两端各建有一座石牌坊
  • 这种建造制式在全国独此一处
  • 在这两座牌坊上面悬挂有题写着文章祖、帝王师的两块匾额
  • 这是时任山西巡抚的蒲城人樊东谟所题

70. 韩城司马迁祠

Hancheng Sima Qian Shrine
西晋、宋、元、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 韩城市南约10公里处的梁山坡岗上

司马迁，字子长，夏阳（今陕西韩城市南）人。生于汉景帝中元五年（公元前145年），卒年不详。他收集编纂了中国第一部纪传体史书——《史记》，对后世史学与文学具有深远的影响。鲁迅先生誉之为：史家之绝唱，无韵之离骚。司马迁祠始建年代已不可考。

以《水经注》所记，西晋永嘉四年（公元310年）的一次修葺为最早记录。据现存碑记记载，在北宋宣和七年（1125年）增修扩建，此后又经金、元、明三代有一次小修（年代不详）。

明代虽曾三次修葺（1438、1536、1606年），但规模都不大。

清代则记有三次大修：第一次是康熙六十一年（1722年），第二次是乾隆十四年（1749年），相去27年，第三次是光绪十二年（1886年）。

以后历经多次修葺，成为今天的规模。1982年公布为第二批全国重点文物保护单位。司马迁祠建于韩城市南约10公里处的梁山坡岗上。祠庙东临黄河，西枕高冈，凭高俯下，选地绝胜。

凭借天然的陡峻地势和雄伟的河山景色，意图创造一种引人崇敬的肃穆气氛，高山仰止，构祠以祀，借此体现司马迁的高风亮节。建筑群坐西朝东，顺着山势分四层而建，层层升高。各层之间以石阶相接。第一层位于梁山第一层台地上，有木牌坊一座，上书高山仰止四个大字。第二层修于梁山第二层台地上，原有砖砌山门一座，上书龙门才子故里六字。现今建筑已毁，仅留遗址。第三层建在梁山第三层台地上，有砖砌牌坊一座，上书河山之阳四字。

第四层建筑位于全祠最高台地上，也是最主要的建筑组群，有献殿、寝殿和墓冢等。献殿为敞厅，与寝殿前檐仅隔一线天沟，留出前院，周以垣墙，围成一座完整院落。院内两株苍劲古柏更增添肃穆气氛。墓冢在祠院之后，冢头古柏虬蟠，权桠垂曲。司马迁祠建筑风格简朴，一式灰瓦悬山顶，作博风、悬鱼、惹草。木作不施彩绘，规模、体量也不大。

其气势之雄伟，并不在于建筑本身，而在于周围的自然环境和所处的地势，也在于建筑与自然环境和地势的结合。祠院现存建筑中，以山门和寝殿较为古老，其大木结构仍保留某些宋代的做法特征，为陕西省内一处很有价值的古建筑。山门：面阔一间，进深两椽；构架为前后搭牵，分心用三柱；屋坡举折平缓，约为1:4；前后檐斗拱四铺作出单抄，补间用一朵，于顺脊串上施单材襻间。

寝殿：面阔三间，进深四椽，带前廊；构架为搭牵对三椽栿用三柱；三椽栿上用驼峰，平梁上用叉手、蜀柱，安丁华抹颏拱，于柱头缝施单材襻间；角柱做生起、侧脚；柱为梭柱；前檐柱头斗拱四铺作出单抄，补间用一朵；当心间、补间四铺作外插昂，昂下刻作华头子；内檐出单抄，耍头后尾起挑斡向上交于平槫下，具有宋《营造法式》的做法特征；又于令拱左右出琴面昂，属于一种地方手法。

— ENGLISH —

Sima Qian, courtesy name Zichang, was a native of Xiayang (south of present-day Hancheng City, Shaanxi). He was born in the 5th year of Zhongyuan of Emperor Jing of Han (145 BC), and the year of his death is unknown.

He collected and compiled China's first biographical history book - 'Records of the Grand Historian' (Shiji), which had a profound influence on later historiography and literature. Mr.

Lu Xun praised it as: 'the swan song of historians, the Li Sao without rhyme.' The founding date of the Sima Qian Shrine is no longer verifiable. According to the 'Commentary on the Water Classic,' a repair in the 4th year of Yongjia, Western Jin (310 AD) is the earliest record.

According to existing stele records, it was expanded in the 7th year of Xuanhe, Northern Song (1125), and thereafter underwent one minor repair (date unknown) during the Jin, Yuan, and Ming dynasties.

Although it was repaired three times in the Ming dynasty (1438, 1536, 1606), the scale was not large.

In the Qing dynasty, three major repairs are recorded: the first in the 61st year of Kangxi (1722), the second in the 14th year of Qianlong (1749), 27 years apart, and the third in the 12th year of Guangxu (1886). After multiple subsequent repairs, it reached its current scale.

It was designated as the second batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 1982. The Sima Qian Shrine is built on the slope of Liang Mountain, about 10 km south of Hancheng City.

The shrine faces the Yellow River to the east, rests on a high ridge to the west, overlooking from a height, with an unrivaled site selection.

Relying on the natural steep terrain and majestic river and mountain scenery, it intends to create a solemn atmosphere that inspires reverence, 'looking up to the high mountain, building a shrine to worship,' thereby expressing Sima Qian's noble integrity.

The building complex faces east from west, built in four layers along the mountain slope, rising layer by layer. Each layer is connected by stone steps.

The first layer is located on the first terrace of Liang Mountain, with a wooden archway inscribed with the four characters 'Gao Shan Yang Zhi' (Looking Up to the High Mountain).

The second layer was built on the second terrace of Liang Mountain, originally with a brick mountain gate inscribed with the six characters 'Long Men Cai Zi Gu Li' (Hometown of the Talented Scholar of Longmen). The current building has been destroyed, leaving only the site.

The third layer is built on the third terrace of Liang Mountain, with a brick archway inscribed with the four characters 'He Shan Zhi Yang' (South of the Yellow River and North of the Mountains).

The fourth layer of buildings is located on the highest terrace of the entire shrine, and is also the most important building group, including the offering hall, sleeping hall, and tomb mound.

The offering hall is an open hall, separated from the front eave of the sleeping hall by only a narrow sky gutter, leaving a front courtyard surrounded by walls to form a complete courtyard. Two vigorous ancient cypresses in the courtyard further enhance the solemn atmosphere.

The tomb mound is behind the shrine courtyard, with ancient cypresses on the mound twisting and curling, with branches drooping and curving.

The architectural style of the Sima Qian Shrine is simple, with uniform gray tile overhanging gable roofs, with gable boards, suspended fish, and grass ornaments. The woodwork is not painted, and the scale and volume are not large.

Its majestic momentum does not lie in the building itself, but in the surrounding natural environment and its terrain, as well as in the combination of the building with the natural environment and terrain.

Among the existing buildings in the shrine courtyard, the mountain gate and sleeping hall are relatively ancient, and their large wooden structures still retain some Song dynasty practice characteristics, making it a very valuable ancient building in Shaanxi Province.

Mountain Gate: one bay wide, two rafters deep; the frame is front-rear daqian (tie beams), with a central column dividing the space; the roof pitch is gentle, about 1:4; the front and rear eave dougong are four-puzuo single chao, with one set in the intermediate bay, with single-cai panjian on the shunji chuan.

Sleeping Hall: three bays wide, four rafters deep, with a front porch; the frame is daqian paired with three-rafter beam using three columns; humpback blocks (tuofeng) are used on the three-rafter beam, diagonal braces (chashou) and short columns (shuzhu) are used on the flat beam, with dinghua moke dougong installed, and single-cai panjian on the column head joint; corner columns have shengqi (rise) and cejiao (inclination); columns are suozhu (tapered columns); the front eave column-head dougong is four-puzuo single chao, with one set in the intermediate bay; in the central bay, the intermediate four-puzuo has an externally inserted ang, with the lower part of the ang carved as a huazi head; the inner eave has single chao, with the rear tail of the shua-tou rising as a tiaowo upward intersecting under the flat purlin, having the practice characteristics of the Song 'Yingzao Fashi'; additionally, qinmian ang (lute-face ang) extends left and right from the ling gong, belonging to a local technique.

71. 留坝留侯祠（张良庙）

Liuba Marquis of Liu Shrine (Zhang Liang Temple)
西汉、隋、唐、宋、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 汉中市留坝县留侯镇庙台子街

张良，字子房，汉代城父（今河南郏县）人，生年不详，卒于公元前186年。张良辅佐刘邦建立西汉，曾封为留城侯，其祠庙故名留侯祠，俗称张良庙。

祠庙坐落于秦岭南坡的紫柏山麓，南距汉中101公里，北邻凤州76公里，距汉中留坝县城17公里处的庙台子街上。

紫柏山为汉水支流褒河的发源地之一，张良庙在褒河支流两条溪涧交汇处，周围群山环抱，松柏密布，现为道教主流全真派十方丛林，成为陕南道教的重要圣地。张良庙始建于西汉，始建年无考。有说汉高祖修留侯庙；

又有说东汉末年（约公元210年）张良的十代孙，汉中王张鲁为祀其先祖修建了汉张留侯祠。隋、唐、宋、明均曾重建、扩建。庙毁于明末战乱，经清康熙二十二年（1683年）重建及道光年间扩建，遂成今日所见的面貌。

留侯祠占地1.42公顷，六进院落曲折相连，156间楼殿亭舍鳞次栉比，摩崖题字50余块，碑刻匾额100余面。2006年公布为第六批国家重点文物保护单位。

留侯祠背靠秦岭山脉的柴关岭，而紫柏山又是柴关岭的主峰，层峦叠嶂。另外，经庙西南流往东北的和庙北由西向东流的两股清流在庙东北处交汇，有如两条游龙，环庙东、南、北，而西方是紫柏山主峰的余脉，恰似一只卧虎，整个建筑群可谓是依山就势，自然天成，变集中为分散。张良庙既有宗教活动功能，又有祭祀功能。

建筑总体布局将两种功能巧妙结合，由两条既相独立又相连接的轴线，将两组建筑连接在一起。照壁、大门、进履桥、保安观、灵官殿、三清殿，组成南北向轴线，严谨对称布置道教建筑；二山门、张良拜殿、大殿，形成东西向轴线，依山势安排祭祀建筑。张良庙建筑，为一式青砖、灰陶小瓦，小式大木结构；规模大者三间，小者一间；硬山顶居多，歇山收山大，近于一间，形制简朴。

唯屋顶镂空花脊、柱间木雕花板、檐枋彩画，题材多为道教神话典故，鱼禽鸟兽、二龙戏珠、双凤朝阳、仙鹤翔云、野鹿摘菩提果、山虎栖卧梧桐……光怪斑斓，饶有意趣，表现出陕南地方风格和道教建筑特色。三清殿：面阔三间，共12米。

进深九檩六椽，共13米，前出抱厦，单檐歇山顶。和三清殿两头搭接相连的是居东的三官殿与居西的三法殿。面阔各三间，单檐悬山建筑与三清殿排成一字形。

— ENGLISH —

Zhang Liang, courtesy name Zifang, was a native of Chengfu, Han dynasty (present-day Jia County, Henan), with unknown birth year and died in 186 BC.

Zhang Liang assisted Liu Bang in establishing the Western Han dynasty and was once enfeoffed as the Marquis of Liucheng, hence his shrine is named the Marquis of Liu Shrine, commonly called the Zhang Liang Temple.

The shrine is located at the foot of Zibai Mountain on the southern slope of the Qinling Mountains, 101 km south of Hanzhong, 76 km north of Fengzhou, on Miaotaizi Street, 17 km from the county seat of Liuba County, Hanzhong.

Zibai Mountain is one of the sources of the Bao River, a tributary of the Han River. The Zhang Liang Temple is at the confluence of two streams that are tributaries of the Bao River, surrounded by mountains and dense with pines and cypresses.

It is now a Ten Directions Jungle of the Quanzhen School, the mainstream of Taoism, becoming an important sacred site of Taoism in southern Shaanxi. The Zhang Liang Temple was first built in the Western Han dynasty, with the exact founding year unknown.

Some say Emperor Gaozu of Han built the Marquis of Liu Temple; others say that in the late Eastern Han dynasty (about 210 AD), Zhang Lu, the King of Hanzhong and the tenth-generation grandson of Zhang Liang, built the Han Marquis of Liu Shrine to worship his ancestor.

It was rebuilt and expanded in the Sui, Tang, Song, and Ming dynasties. The temple was destroyed in the wars at the end of the Ming dynasty, and after reconstruction in the 22nd year of Kangxi, Qing (1683) and expansion during the Daoguang period, it reached its present appearance.

The Marquis of Liu Shrine covers 1.42 hectares, with six courtyards connected in twists and turns, 156 buildings including towers, halls, pavilions, and rooms arranged in close succession, more than 50 cliff inscriptions, and more than 100 stele carvings and plaques.

It was designated as the sixth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites in 2006. The Marquis of Liu Shrine backs onto Chaiguan Ridge of the Qinling Mountains, and Zibai Mountain is the main peak of Chaiguan Ridge, with layer upon layer of peaks.

In addition, two clear streams, one flowing from southwest to northeast of the temple and the other from west to east north of the temple, converge at the northeast of the temple, like two swimming dragons surrounding the temple on the east, south, and north, while to the west is the secondary range of the main peak of Zibai Mountain, just like a crouching tiger.

The entire building complex can be said to follow the mountain terrain, naturally formed, changing from concentrated to dispersed. The Zhang Liang Temple has both religious activity functions and sacrificial functions.

The overall architectural layout cleverly combines the two functions, connecting two groups of buildings through two axes that are both independent and connected.

The screen wall, main gate, Jinlv Bridge, Bao'an Temple, Lingguan Hall, and Sanqing Hall form a north-south axis, with Taoist buildings arranged in rigorous symmetry; the second mountain gate, Zhang Liang worship hall, and main hall form an east-west axis, with sacrificial buildings arranged according to the mountain terrain.

The buildings of the Zhang Liang Temple are uniformly made of blue bricks and small gray ceramic tiles, with small-style large wooden structures; the larger ones are three bays and the smaller ones one bay; most have flush gable roofs, with hip-and-gable roofs having large收山, nearly one bay, with a simple form.

Only the hollow flower ridges on the roofs, carved wooden panels between columns, and painted eave beams have themes mostly from Taoist mythological allusions, such as fish, birds, and beasts, two dragons playing with a pearl, two phoenixes facing the sun, cranes soaring in clouds, wild deer picking bodhi fruit, and mountain tigers resting on parasol trees...

bizarre and colorful, full of interest, showing the local style of southern Shaanxi and the characteristics of Taoist architecture. Sanqing Hall: three bays wide, totaling 12 m.

Nine purlins and six rafters deep, totaling 13 m, with a front抱厦, single-eave hip-and-gable roof. Connected to both ends of the Sanqing Hall are the Sanguan Hall to the east and the Sanfa Hall to the west.

Each is three bays wide, with single-eave overhanging gable roofs, arranged in a straight line with the Sanqing Hall.

  • 张良辅佐刘邦建立西汉

  • 俗称张良庙
  • 祠庙坐落于秦岭南坡的紫柏山麓
  • 南距汉中101公里
  • 北邻凤州76公里
  • 距汉中留坝县城17公里处的庙台子街上
  • 紫柏山为汉水支流褒河的发源地之一
  • 张良庙在褒河支流两条溪涧交汇处

72. 勉县武侯祠

Mian County Wuhou Shrine
三国、魏、唐、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 勉县旧城东

三国时，诸葛亮辅佐刘备建立蜀汉，生前官至丞相，封武乡侯，死后谥忠武侯。据《三国志》载：蜀汉景耀六年（公元263年）春，诏为亮立庙于沔阳（今勉县）。祠庙坐南向北，背靠汉江，面向中原，取意于诸葛亮北定中原、收复汉室的遗志。

据史料记载：蜀汉景耀六年（公元263年）春，后主刘禅诏为亮立庙于沔阳（今勉县），祠庙初建于定军山下的武侯墓附近。当时祠建于定军山下，北魏时已崩褫难识，唐代重修。

明正德八年（1513年）都御使兰璋认为，定军山下的武侯祠离城较远，且与勉县老城分距汉江南北两岸，有汉水阻隔，前往祭祀很不方便，遂立重新庙于沔城（勉县旧城）东，距武侯墓5公里，即为今庙。

我国许多地方都建有纪念诸葛亮的武侯祠，而勉县武侯祠收藏有唐贞元十一年（公元795年）的武侯新庙碑，因此被证明是我国最早的武侯祠。

2013年公布为国家重点文物保护单位。勉县武侯祠坐南向北，占地30余亩。建筑群南北轴线长200米，东西宽约120米，周筑垣墙，是汉中地区最大的古建筑群之一。

整座建筑群可分为前后两大部分，祠庙南北中轴线上按照前庙后寝，布置了祠庙主要的祭祀性殿宇，由19座单体建筑组成七进院落。祠内现存明清时期古建筑19座80余间（建筑面积约1785平方米）。

主要有山门、乐楼、东西辕门、牌楼、琴楼、戟门、钟鼓楼、东西过门、东西厢房、东西配殿、拜殿、大殿和崇圣祠、琴台等建筑物。拜殿：又称献殿，是大殿前的附属建筑，厅堂三间，为卷棚式硬山建筑，为拜谒、祭祀诸葛亮的地方。面阔三间，四檩卷棚式硬山建筑。

建筑总高6.58米，明间面阔4.7米，施平身科斗拱三攒，次间稍窄，面阔3.95米，施平身科斗拱三攒。檐柱高5.6米，圆柱，柱身收分不明显，柱下用平石作柱础。梁架为彻上露明造。正身部分梁架，用四架梁，梁下有随梁枋。

上立两根脊瓜柱，并辅以角背。大殿：单檐歇山顶，面阔五间。明间面阔4.08米，次间稍窄，面阔2.65米，梢间面阔2.46米，明间施平身科斗拱一攒，次间和梢间也各施平身科斗拱一攒。

檐柱高4.70米，圆柱，柱身收分不明显，柱下用圆形石作柱础。梁架为抬梁式，彻上露明造。用九架梁，梁下有随梁枋，上立圆形瓜柱，并辅以角背。

崇圣祠（寝殿）：单檐悬山顶，总高6.79米，明间面阔3.70米，次间稍窄，面阔3.10米，梢间面阔2.85米，廊深1.62米。

檐柱高3.21米，圆柱，柱身收分不明显，柱下方形石作柱础。梁架结构为小式大木结构，梁架为彻上露明造。

— ENGLISH —

During the Three Kingdoms period, Zhuge Liang assisted Liu Bei in establishing the Shu Han dynasty, serving as Prime Minister before his death, enfeoffed as the Marquis of Wuxiang, and posthumously titled the Loyal and Martial Marquis.

According to the 'Records of the Three Kingdoms': in the spring of the 6th year of Jingyao, Shu Han (263 AD), an edict was issued to establish a temple for Liang in Mianyang (present-day Mian County).

The shrine faces north from south, with its back to the Han River and facing the Central Plains, symbolizing Zhuge Liang's behest of pacifying the north and recovering the Han dynasty.

According to historical records: in the spring of the 6th year of Jingyao, Shu Han (263 AD), Emperor Liu Shan issued an edict to establish a temple for Liang in Mianyang (present-day Mian County), and the shrine was initially built near the Wuhou Tomb at the foot of Dingjun Mountain.

At that time, the shrine was built at the foot of Dingjun Mountain; by the Northern Wei Dynasty it had collapsed and become difficult to recognize, and it was rebuilt in the Tang dynasty.

In the 8th year of Zhengde, Ming (1513), Censor Lan Zhang believed that the Wuhou Shrine at the foot of Dingjun Mountain was far from the city, and separated from the old city of Mian County by the north and south banks of the Han River, with the Han River as a barrier, making it inconvenient to go and worship; therefore, a new temple was established east of Mian City (old city of Mian County), 5 km from the Wuhou Tomb, which is the present temple.

Wuhou Shrines commemorating Zhuge Liang have been built in many places in China, and the Mian County Wuhou Shrine houses the 'New Wuhou Temple Stele' from the 11th year of Zhenyuan, Tang (795 AD), thus proving it to be the earliest Wuhou Shrine in China.

It was designated as a National Key Cultural Heritage Site in 2013. Mian County Wuhou Shrine faces north from south, covering more than 30 mu.

The north-south axis of the building complex is 200 m long, about 120 m wide east-west, surrounded by walls, and is one of the largest ancient building complexes in the Hanzhong region.

The entire building complex can be divided into two major parts, front and rear; on the north-south central axis of the shrine, according to the principle of 'front temple, rear sleeping quarters,' the main sacrificial halls of the shrine are arranged, consisting of 19 individual buildings forming seven courtyards.

There are currently 19 ancient buildings from the Ming and Qing periods with more than 80 bays in the shrine (building area about 1,785 square meters).

The main buildings include the mountain gate, opera tower, east and west yuan gates, archway, qin tower, halberd gate, bell and drum towers, east and west passage gates, east and west wing rooms, east and west side halls, worship hall, main hall, Chongsheng Shrine, and qin platform.

Worship Hall: also called the Offering Hall, is an附属 building in front of the main hall, with three bays, a rolling-roof flush gable building, serving as a place for paying respects to and sacrificing to Zhuge Liang.

Three bays wide, four-purlin rolling-roof flush gable building.

The total building height is 6.58 m; the central bay is 4.7 m wide, with three sets of pingjian-ke dougong; the secondary bays are slightly narrower, 3.95 m wide, with three sets of pingjian-ke dougong.

The eave columns are 5.6 m high, cylindrical, with unobvious column tapering, with flat stones as column bases. The beam frame is exposed (cheshang luming zao). The main part of the beam frame uses four-frame beams, with following beam beams under the beams.

Two ridge melon columns are erected above, supplemented by corner backs. Main Hall: single-eave hip-and-gable roof, five bays wide.

The central bay is 4.08 m wide, the secondary bays are slightly narrower at 2.65 m wide, and the end bays are 2.46 m wide; the central bay has one set of pingjian-ke dougong, and the secondary and end bays each have one set of pingjian-ke dougong.

The eave columns are 4.70 m high, cylindrical, with unobvious column tapering, with round stones as column bases. The beam frame is of抬梁 type, exposed (cheshang luming zao).

Nine-frame beams are used, with following beam beams under the beams, and round melon columns erected above, supplemented by corner backs.

Chongsheng Shrine (Sleeping Hall): single-eave overhanging gable roof, total height 6.79 m; the central bay is 3.70 m wide, the secondary bays are slightly narrower at 3.10 m wide, and the end bays are 2.85 m wide, with a corridor depth of 1.62 m.

The eave columns are 3.21 m high, cylindrical, with unobvious column tapering, with square stones as column bases. The beam frame structure is small-style large wooden structure, with the beam frame exposed (cheshang luming zao).

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 三国时诸葛亮辅佐刘备建立蜀汉
  • 生前官至丞相
  • 封武乡侯
  • 死后谥忠武侯
  • 据三国志载
  • 蜀汉景耀六年春
  • 后主刘禅诏为亮立庙于沔阳今勉县
  • 祠庙坐南向北
  • 背靠汉江
  • 面向中原
  • 取意于诸葛亮北定中原、收复汉室的遗志
  • 据史料记载
  • 蜀汉景耀六年春
  • 后主刘禅诏为亮立庙于沔阳今勉县
  • 祠庙初建于定军山下的武侯墓附近

73. 白水仓颉庙

Baishui Cangjie Temple
东汉、明、清 祠庙建筑 全国重点文物保护单位 白水县东北35公里处的黄龙山南麓史官乡

仓颉，传说为黄帝时史官，发明文字，是中国古代四圣（孔子孔圣、关羽关圣、苍颉苍圣、周公旦元圣）之一。其原籍在今白水县史官村。仓颉庙位于白水县东北35公里处的黄龙山南麓史官乡，东汉延熹年间庙宇已初具规模，历代有维修和扩建。

庙内尚存东汉延熹五年（公元162年）的仓颉庙碑，说明至迟在东汉时期即已有此庙，现存建筑多为明清以后所建。白水仓颉庙是国内唯一仅存的纪念文字发明创造的庙宇。

2001年被国务院批准为第五批全国重点文物保护单位，是全国同类遗迹中唯一的国保单位。庙后有墓冢，墓冢周长50米左右，总面积17亩。

主体建筑有前殿、中殿、后殿、戏楼、钟鼓楼等。庙内保存石碑16通。其中仓圣鸟迹书碑、孔子弟子题字碑、东汉延熹五年（公元162年）的仓颉庙碑等都是金石学上较名贵的碑刻。庙门向南，门前有砖砌照壁一通。

入庙门为第一进，门为过街楼式，五间三券洞，现仅存砖券台座，门楼已毁。紧贴庙门东西各有戏楼一座，高于庙门，均为硬山顶，称为东、西戏台。现存的东台创建于清嘉庆二年（1797年），民国时重修。

通面宽三间8.9米，通进深两间8.6米，台基高1.54米。两侧山墙前面向外倾斜，形成八字形墙。八字墙下面各饰以照壁，上面则高出屋面，其墀头和顶部雕刻均非常精致。西台创建于清咸丰元年（1856年）。

脊梁上有题记：咸丰元年岁次辛亥桐月上浣十日卯时上梁大吉合社创建。该戏台的形式和东台形似，通面宽三间8.22米，进深8.31米，台基高1.37米。梁架多用扁方木，结构富有逻辑性。前台两侧设有八字影壁。西台上下场门之上侧的两幅壁画的人物形象质朴憨厚，有较高的艺术价值。

这种以高夹低的组合形式，在古建筑中颇为少见，也可能是两次修建所形成。庙门北为前殿，面阔五间，进深仅一间，无门窗。殿后为第二进院落，左右为钟、鼓楼。往北为中殿，面阔五间，进深三间，殿前有报厅及东西厢，再北为寝殿，是庙内保存较好、年代较早的建筑。殿阶较高，面阔五间，带前廊，两梢间为穿堂。前廊檐柱为石柱，侧脚、生起明显。斗拱用材并不大，做法合于法式规范，显得形制古朴。

经穿堂通至后院，紧邻后殿为墓冢，围以八角墙垣。

— ENGLISH —

Cangjie, legendarily the historian of the Yellow Emperor, invented writing and is one of the Four Sages of ancient China (Confucius the Sage of Kong, Guan Yu the Sage of Guan, Cangjie the Sage of Cang, and Zhou Gongdan the Sage of Yuan).

His ancestral home is in present-day Shiguan Village, Baishui County. The Cangjie Temple is located in Shiguan Township, at the southern foot of Huanglong Mountain, 35 km northeast of Baishui County.

The temple had already taken initial shape during the Yanxi period of Eastern Han, and has been maintained and expanded through successive dynasties.

The temple still preserves the Cangjie Temple Stele from the 5th year of Yanxi, Eastern Han (162 AD), indicating that the temple existed at the latest during the Eastern Han period; the existing buildings were mostly built after the Ming and Qing dynasties.

The Baishui Cangjie Temple is the only existing temple in China commemorating the invention of writing.

In 2001, it was approved by the State Council as the fifth batch of National Key Cultural Heritage Sites, and is the only national-level protected unit among similar relics nationwide.

Behind the temple is a tomb mound, with a circumference of about 50 m and a total area of 17 mu. The main buildings include the front hall, middle hall, rear hall, opera tower, and bell and drum towers. The temple preserves 16 stone steles.

Among them, the Cang Sheng Niao Ji Shu Stele, the Confucius Disciples Inscription Stele, and the Cangjie Temple Stele from the 5th year of Yanxi, Eastern Han (162 AD) are all relatively precious steles in epigraphy. The temple gate faces south, with a brick screen wall in front.

Entering the temple gate is the first courtyard; the gate is in the style of a street-crossing tower, with five bays and three arched openings; now only the brick arched platform base remains, and the gate tower has been destroyed.

Closely attached to the east and west of the temple gate are one opera tower each, higher than the temple gate, both with flush gable roofs, called the East and West Opera Stages.

The existing East Stage was founded in the 2nd year of Jiaqing, Qing (1797) and rebuilt during the Republic period. It has a total width of three bays, 8.9 m, a total depth of two bays, 8.6 m, and a platform base height of 1.54 m.

The front of the gable walls on both sides slopes outward, forming a八字-shaped wall. Below the八字 walls are decorated with screen walls each, and above they rise above the roof, with the chi tou (gable ornament) and top carvings all very exquisite.

The West Stage was founded in the 1st year of Xianfeng, Qing (1856).

There is an inscription on the main beam: 'On the auspicious day of the 10th day of the first ten days of the 3rd month in the Xinhai year of Xianfeng, the main beam was placed at the auspicious hour, built jointly by the community.' The form of this opera stage is similar to the East Stage, with a total width of three bays, 8.22 m, a depth of 8.31 m, and a platform base height of 1.37 m.

The beam frames mostly use flat square timber, with a logical structure. On both sides of the front stage are八字 screen walls. The two murals above the entrance and exit doors of the West Stage depict figures with simple and honest images, having relatively high artistic value.

This combination form of high sandwiching low is quite rare in ancient buildings, and may also have been formed by two constructions. North of the temple gate is the front hall, five bays wide, only one bay deep, without doors or windows.

Behind the hall is the second courtyard, with the bell and drum towers on the left and right.

Further north is the middle hall, five bays wide, three bays deep, with a reporting hall and east-west wings in front of the hall; further north is the sleeping hall, which is a better-preserved and earlier building in the temple.

The hall steps are relatively high, five bays wide, with a front porch, and the two end bays are passage halls. The front porch eave columns are stone columns, with obvious cejiao (inclination) and shengqi (rise).

The dougong timber is not large, and its practice conforms to the norms of the Yingzao Fashi, appearing simple and ancient in form. Through the passage hall to the rear courtyard, adjacent to the rear hall is the tomb mound, surrounded by an octagonal wall.

  • 发明文字

  • 其原籍在今白水县史官村
  • 仓颉庙位于白水县东北35公里处的黄龙山南麓史官乡

  • 历代有维修和扩建

  • 白水仓颉庙是国内唯一仅存的纪念文字发明创造的庙宇
  • 2001年被国务院批准为第五批全国重点文物保护单位
  • 是全国同类遗迹中唯一的国保单位
  • 庙后有墓冢
  • 墓冢周长50米左右

74. 城固韩氏祠堂

Chenggu Han Clan Ancestral Hall
明、清 祠庙建筑 城固县人民政府重点文物保护单位 城固县原公镇西坝村西北300米处

韩氏祠堂位于城固县原公镇西坝村西北300米处，始建于明代。清嘉庆年间进行了扩修，之后屡有修葺。韩氏祠堂整体气势宏伟，内部结构精妙，集雕、塑、铸、绘于一身，工艺讲究，装饰华丽，堪称陕西民间建筑装饰艺术的典范。

2011年公布为城固县人民政府重点文物保护单位。建筑布局为坐北朝南的五进院落，总占地面积1170平方米。从南至北依次为照壁、大门、牌楼、中厅、献殿、过厅、大殿，各个空间功能明确，空间秩序、尊卑等级分明，在视觉和心理上则形成有序的空间序列。

韩氏祠堂照壁坐落在韩巷南部，与山门相对，为双耳砖雕，高大雄伟。山门为硬山顶，据传为仿江苏无锡市衙式样建造，门槛下汉白玉质抱鼓石，图案纹样精美，雕刻刀法细腻，形象生动。

二进为牌楼，又称进士楼，重檐歇山顶，楼高三层，面阔三间，前檐牌匾楷书奉直大夫，牌匾四周雕刻文房四宝、如意等装饰图案，落款韩叙典、韩法、韩履宠爷孙三人。后檐牌匾楷书进士，西侧门楣下挂落，深浮雕百官朝圣图，人物造型生动，栩栩如生。原前檐下曾竖写诰封匾额，左右明柱分别挂有乡举孝廉、明经进士匾，另有透雕云龙门牙等精美饰件，现在都已毁坏。

该楼因是标榜政绩地位的纪念性建筑，故而建造得非常气魄，雕梁画栋，做工精细，七踩斗拱，飞檐翘角，构思奇妙，给人以平步青云之感。三进中厅，硬山顶建筑，起过渡作用。四进献殿，悬山顶建筑，为周期性祭祀、族人议事用。五进大殿为明代所建，面阔三间，进深一间，硬山瓦顶，供奉韩家历代先祖神位。

其中，献殿与大殿之间，间隔较近，巧妙地用过桥的方法连接起来，形成过厅，设计精巧，别具匠心。

— ENGLISH —

The Han Clan Ancestral Hall is located 300 m northwest of Xiba Village, Yuangong Town, Chenggu County, and was first built in the Ming dynasty. It was expanded during the Jiaqing period of Qing and subsequently repaired multiple times.

The Han Clan Ancestral Hall has a magnificent overall momentum and exquisite internal structure, integrating carving, sculpture, casting, and painting in one, with exquisite craftsmanship and gorgeous decoration, which can be called a model of Shaanxi folk architectural decorative art.

In 2011, it was designated as a Key Cultural Heritage Site by the People's Government of Chenggu County. The building layout is a five-courtyard complex facing south from north, with a total area of 1,170 square meters.

From south to north, it sequentially includes the screen wall, main gate, archway, middle hall, offering hall, passage hall, and main hall; each space has clear functions, with distinct spatial order and hierarchy of superiority and inferiority, forming an orderly spatial sequence visually and psychologically.

The screen wall of the Han Clan Ancestral Hall is located in the south of Han Lane, opposite the mountain gate, and is a double-ear brick carving, tall and majestic.

The mountain gate has a flush gable roof, reportedly built in imitation of the style of the Wuxi County Yamen in Jiangsu; below the threshold are white marble drum-shaped bearing stones, with exquisite patterns and fine carving刀法, vivid images.

The second courtyard is the archway, also called the Jinshi (Advanced Scholar) Tower, with a double-eave hip-and-gable roof, three stories high, three bays wide; the front eave plaque has the regular script 'Feng Zhi Da Fu' (Honored Official), with decorative patterns such as the four treasures of the study and ruyi carved around the plaque, signed by Han Xudian, Han Fa, and Han Luchong, grandfather, father, and grandson.

The rear eave plaque has the regular script 'Jinshi' (Advanced Scholar); below the west door lintel is a hanging ornament with a deep relief of 'Hundred Officials Pilgrimage to the Sage,' with vivid and lifelike figure modeling.

Originally, under the front eave there was a vertically written 'Gao Feng' (Imperial Grant) plaque, and the left and right central columns respectively hung 'Xiang Ju Xiao Lian' (County-recommended Filial and Incorrupt) and 'Ming Jing Jin Shi' (Classics-examined Advanced Scholar) plaques, plus exquisite ornaments such as openwork carved cloud-dragon door teeth, all of which have now been destroyed.

Because this tower was a commemorative building to advertise political achievements and status, it was built very imposingly, with carved beams and painted rafters, fine workmanship, seven-cai dougong, flying eaves and upturned corners, and ingenious design, giving people a feeling of rising to the clouds.

The third courtyard middle hall is a flush gable roof building, serving as a transition. The fourth courtyard offering hall is an overhanging gable roof building, used for periodic sacrifices and clan meetings.

The fifth courtyard main hall was built in the Ming dynasty, three bays wide, one bay deep, with a flush gable tile roof, enshrining the ancestral spirit tablets of the Han family through the ages.

Among them, between the offering hall and the main hall, the interval is relatively close, and they are cleverly connected by a bridge method, forming a passage hall, with exquisite design and originality.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 始建于明代
  • 清嘉庆年间进行了扩修
  • 之后屡有修葺
  • 韩氏祠堂整体气势宏伟
  • 内部结构精妙
  • 集雕、塑、铸、绘于一身
  • 工艺讲究
  • 装饰华丽
  • 堪称陕西民间建筑装饰艺术的典范
  • 2011年公布为城固县人民政府重点文物保护单位
  • 建筑布局为坐北朝南的五进院落
  • 总占地面积1170平方米
  • 从南至北依次为照壁、大门、牌楼、中厅、献殿、过厅、大殿
  • 各个空间功能明确
  • 空间秩序、尊卑等级分明

75. 神木二郎山庙群

Shenmu Erlang Mountain Temple Complex
明、清 祠庙建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 榆林市神木县城西1公里处

神木二郎山庙群位于榆林市神木县城西1公里处，窟野河与芹河交汇处的二郎山上。神木县志记载了些许关于二郎山的来源，二郎山初次发现是于明正统八年（1443年），山上部分建筑在嘉靖二十四年（1545年）时进行了重新修正。

在诸神殿顶梁上有大明嘉靖二十四年岁次乙卯五月十七日立字样。现在为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

二郎山由山脚石窟地藏阁起，至北山顶山神庙为止，在前后相距1公里多的山脊上，依山就势，错落有致地建成集儒、道、释三教合一的各类建筑30余处，大小庙宇72座，其中13处是石窟洞庭，塑像320尊，碑文石刻40余块。

这100余间（孔）建筑，殿、庙、亭、阁、窟疏密相间，错落有致，其规模颇为宏大，各具特色。二郎山寺庙群由于山脊用地有限，不仅以院落为基本结构，还有很多单体建筑存在，大部分建筑随山势布局，没有明显的中轴线，随山势的变化形成多条与自然遥相呼应的轴线。

从山底到山坡、崖壁一直到达山顶，运用大小、形式、种类各异的建筑，形成多样的院落和空间组织，同时在不同的寺庙院落中又有不同的神灵供奉于不同的视线方向。庙群主体建筑有八仙洞、地藏洞、浩然亭、二郎庙、关帝庙、诸神殿、祖师庙、玉皇阁、娘娘庙等，可分为砖石窑洞带廊檐和单檐硬山式两种类型。庙、殿现存明清壁画18幅，多为工笔重彩，间有国画。

还有明清及民国年间碑石60余通，明代及清嘉庆元年（1796年）摩崖题刻7方。其中，明代照壁，正面石刻虎啸图，背面石刻九龙戏水图，栩栩如生；明代石牌楼，造型古朴；

地藏洞里的石窟藻井，雕刻细腻，均有较高的艺术及历史价值。

— ENGLISH —

The Shenmu Erlang Mountain Temple Complex is located 1 km west of Shenmu County town, Yulin City, on Erlang Mountain at the confluence of the Kuye River and Qin River.

The Shenmu County Chronicle records some information about the origin of Erlang Mountain; Erlang Mountain was first discovered in the 8th year of Zhengtong, Ming (1443), and some buildings on the mountain were renovated in the 24th year of Jiajing (1545).

On the top beam of the Zhushen Hall, there is an inscription reading 'Erected on the 17th day of the 5th month of the Yimao year, the 24th year of Jiajing of the Great Ming.' It is now a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site.

Starting from the Dizang Pavilion rock cave at the foot of the mountain to the Mountain God Temple at the northern mountain top, on a ridge more than 1 km apart front to back, more than 30 various buildings integrating Confucianism, Taoism, and Buddhism were built according to the mountain terrain, scattered in an orderly manner, with 72 temples of various sizes, of which 13 are rock cave halls, 320 statues, and more than 40 stele inscriptions and stone carvings.

These more than 100 bays (openings) of buildings, including halls, temples, pavilions, towers, and caves, are densely and sparsely interspersed, scattered in an orderly manner, with a quite grand scale and distinctive features.

Due to the limited land on the ridge, the Erlang Mountain temple complex not only uses courtyards as the basic structure but also has many individual buildings; most buildings are laid out according to the mountain terrain, without an obvious central axis, forming multiple axes echoing with nature along with the changes of the mountain terrain.

From the mountain foot to the slopes, cliffs, and all the way to the mountain top, buildings of various sizes, forms, and types are used to form diverse courtyards and spatial organizations, while different temple courtyards have different deities enshrined in different viewing directions.

The main buildings of the temple complex include the Baxian Cave, Dizang Cave, Haoran Pavilion, Erlang Temple, Guandi Temple, Zhushen Hall, Zushi Temple, Yuhuang Pavilion, and Niangniang Temple, which can be divided into two types: brick-stone cave dwellings with corridor eaves and single-eave flush gable style.

There are 18 existing Ming-Qing murals in the temples and halls, mostly fine-brush heavy color, interspersed with traditional Chinese painting.

There are also more than 60 stone steles from the Ming, Qing, and Republican periods, and 7 cliff inscriptions from the Ming and the 1st year of Jiaqing, Qing (1796).

Among them, the Ming dynasty screen wall has a stone-carved 'Tiger Roaring' picture on the front and a 'Nine Dragons Playing in Water' picture on the back, vivid and lifelike; the Ming dynasty stone archway has a simple and unsophisticated shape; the rock cave caisson ceiling in the Dizang Cave is exquisitely carved, all having relatively high artistic and historical value.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 位于榆林市神木县城西1公里处
  • 窟野河与芹河交汇处的二郎山上
  • 神木县志记载了些许关于二郎山的来源
  • 二郎山初次发现是于明正统八年
  • 山上部分建筑在嘉靖二十四年时进行了重新修正
  • 在诸神殿顶梁上有大明嘉靖二十四年岁次乙卯五月十七日立字样
  • 现在为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 二郎山由山脚石窟地藏阁起
  • 至北山顶山神庙为止
  • 在前后相距1公里多的山脊上
  • 依山就势
  • 错落有致地建成集儒、道、释三教合一的各类建筑30余处
  • 大小庙宇72座
  • 其中13处是石窟洞庭
  • 塑像320尊

76. 略阳江神庙

Lueyang River God Temple
明末清初 祠庙建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 汉中市略阳县城环城西路嘉陵江边

略阳江神庙位于汉中市略阳县城环城西路嘉陵江边，又名王爷庙、龙王庙。不仅是过去汉中境内独一无二的船帮用以祭祀、聚会的会馆，也是长江流域保存最完整的具有氐羌文化建筑风格的古代戏院建筑群。现在为陕西省重点文物保护单位。江神庙始建何时，尚无佐证。据清道光《略阳县志·艺文志》载：王爷庙岁己亥庚子间重加丹垩，金碧辉煌。

又据重修文昌庙碑记载，应为道光二十一年（1841年）重修。因此，最晚在明末清初就应有此建筑。新中国成立初，为县公安局驻地，1959年该局搬迁新址后，由居民占住。

文化大革命中，戏楼翼角斜撑、戏楼台前兽类木雕等，毁坏严重。1992年公布为省级文物保护单位。

— ENGLISH —

The Lueyang River God Temple is located by the Jialing River on Huancheng West Road, Lueyang County town, Hanzhong City, also known as the Prince Temple or Dragon King Temple.

It was not only the unique guild hall in the past Hanzhong region used by boatmen for sacrifices and gatherings, but also the best-preserved ancient theater building complex with Di-Qiang cultural architectural style in the Yangtze River basin.

It is now a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site. There is no evidence as to when the River God Temple was first built.

According to the 'Art and Literature Chronicle' of the 'Lueyang County Chronicle' from the Daoguang period of Qing: 'The Prince Temple was repainted during the Jihai-Gengzi years, resplendent and magnificent.' According to the record on the stele of the rebuilt Wenchang Temple, it should have been rebuilt in the 21st year of Daoguang (1841).

Therefore, this building should have existed at the latest in the late Ming and early Qing dynasties. At the beginning of the founding of New China, it was the residence of the County Public Security Bureau; after the bureau moved to a new site in 1959, it was occupied by residents.

During the Cultural Revolution, the diagonal braces of the opera tower wing corners and the animal wood carvings in front of the opera tower stage were severely damaged. It was designated as a provincial-level cultural heritage site in 1992.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 又名王爷庙、龙王庙
  • 不仅是过去汉中境内独一无二的船帮用以祭祀、聚会的会馆
  • 也是长江流域保存最完整的具有氐羌文化建筑风格的古代戏院建筑群
  • 现在为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 江神庙始建何时
  • 尚无佐证
  • 据清道光略阳县志艺文志载
  • 王爷庙岁己亥庚子间重加丹垩
  • 金碧辉煌
  • 又据重修文昌庙碑记载
  • 应为道光二十一年重修
  • 因此
  • 最晚在明末清初就应有此建筑
  • 新中国成立初
  • 为县公安局驻地

第六章 书院、考院与会馆

Chapter 6 Academies, Examination Halls and Guild Halls

77. 关中书院

Guanzhong Academy
明、清 书院建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 西安市城南门内书院门街

关中书院位于现陕西省西安市城南门内书院门街，为西北四大书院之首，是明、清两代陕西的最高学府，虽历经400年风雨，仍比较完整地保留了空间院落形态及建筑单体的形制，是一座研究我国传统建筑文化不可多得的活的书院。

1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

据《咸宁县志》记载，关中书院始建于明万历三十七年（1609年），盖因陕西著名学者冯从吾讲学的宝庆寺难以容纳，故将其东邻小悉园改建为书院。

创建之初，书院坐北面南，延绥抚台涂宗浚捐建讲堂六间，并提名为允执堂。三年后，书院又修建了斯道中天阁，专门祭祀孔子，由此形成了前学后庙的书院典型平面布局。明天启六年（1626年）书院遭毁。

清康熙、乾隆时重修，至光绪初年，书院初具规模，光绪二十九年（1903年）改为陕西第一师范学堂，宣统元年（1909年）改为陕西师范大学堂。以后又改为陕西省第一师范学校。

民国二十三年（1934年），重修关中书院斯道中天阁，并将第一师范改为陕西省立西安师范学校。

现已经恢复关中书院的形制，校舍由单层变为双层，基本保持了原有风貌。关中书院总体布局严格遵循儒家的礼制思想，采用中轴对称模式，通过轴线上大小不同的各个院落，明显区分出了尊卑、主次的关系。

书院中轴线上共有五重院落：牌坊大门外为第一重；大门至二门为第二重；二门至三门为第三重；三门至允执堂为第四重；泽园为第五重。其中，前三重院落规模较小，成为整个空间高潮部分的前奏，起到明显的心理诱导作用。

第三重院落为书院的核心部分，院落由左右学生斋舍外的长廊围合而成，平面进深约80米，庭院尺寸23米×38米，建筑均为单层，高约10米，空间尺度宜人，氛围宁静、悠远。

第四重院落以允执堂为核心的讲学区是关中书院内最大、也是最重要的院落，院落平面尺寸28米×73米，远远望去，位于轴线尽端的允执堂给人以悠远、神圣的感觉。

第五重院落为书院的祭祀区，位于书院的末端，院落由精一堂与两侧厢房围合而成，主要功能为祭祀书院先贤。泽园独立为院，满足了祭祀所要求的静穆、安祥的环境要求。这也成为其与书院内其他院落的最大区别。

允执堂为关中书院的讲堂，是建筑群整体核心。允执出自《中庸》中的允执厥中一词，意为遵循儒家的中庸之道，并暗合关中的中。

现存允执堂为1990年代初依照原貌翻建而成，其面阔五间，进深五开间，面积889平方米。建筑为一座硬山和一座卷棚顶勾连搭而成，前檐出一开间歇山抱厦，后檐出三开间歇山抱厦，呈凸字形平面。檐口轮廓线变化作幅度较大的处理，翼角的处理较其他建筑更为灵活，建筑风格矜持、庄重。

精一堂为关中书院祭殿，书院不可缺少的要素，用以供奉和纪念学派宗师和建院功臣，起顾书院之建，必崇祀先贤，以正学统功用。其与左右厢房单独成院，名曰泽园，位于书院中轴线的最末端。

建筑为后院之中心，尺度高大、形制宏伟。其面阔五开间，南北长10.2米、东西宽17.8米，面积约182平方米，硬山顶。房屋内正中设道统神主，两侧为正学、理学名臣神主。

— ENGLISH —

Guanzhong academy is located on Shuyuanmen Street, inside the South Gate of Xi'an City, Shaanxi Province. It is the foremost of the four major academies in the northwest and was the highest institution of learning in Shaanxi during the Ming and Qing dynasties.

Despite 400 years of wind and rain, it still relatively completely preserves the spatial courtyard form and the form of individual buildings, and is a rare living academy for studying China's traditional architectural culture.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992.

According to the 'Xianning County Chronicle,' Guanzhong academy was first built in the 37th year of Wanli, Ming (1609), because the Baoqing Temple where the famous Shaanxi scholar Feng Congwu lectured could no longer accommodate the students, so its eastern neighbor, Xiaoxi Garden, was converted into an academy.

At the beginning of its creation, the academy faced south from north; Tu Zongjun, the Governor of Yansui, donated to build six lecture halls and named them Yunzhi Hall.

Three years later, the academy also built the Sidao Zhongtian Pavilion, specifically for sacrificing to Confucius, thus forming the typical academy plane layout of 'front learning, rear temple.' In the 6th year of Tianqi, Ming (1626), the academy was destroyed.

It was rebuilt during the Kangxi and Qianlong periods of Qing, and by the early Guangxu period, the academy had taken initial shape; in the 29th year of Guangxu (1903), it was changed to the Shaanxi First Normal School, and in the 1st year of Xuantong (1909), it was changed to the Shaanxi Normal University.

Later it was changed to the Shaanxi Provincial First Normal School. In the 23rd year of the Republic (1934), the Sidao Zhongtian Pavilion of Guanzhong academy was rebuilt, and the First Normal School was changed to the Shaanxi Provincial Xi'an Normal School.

The form of Guanzhong academy has now been restored, with school buildings changed from single-story to double-story, basically maintaining the original style.

The overall layout of Guanzhong academy strictly follows the Confucian ritual system, adopting a central-axis symmetric mode, through various courtyards of different sizes on the axis, clearly distinguishing the relationships of superiority/inferiority and primary/secondary.

There are five courtyards on the central axis of the academy: outside the archway gate is the first courtyard; from the main gate to the second gate is the second courtyard; from the second gate to the third gate is the third courtyard; from the third gate to Yunzhi Hall is the fourth courtyard; and Zeyuan is the fifth courtyard.

Among them, the first three courtyards are smaller in scale, serving as a prelude to the climax of the entire space, playing an obvious psychological induction role.

The third courtyard is the core part of the academy, enclosed by long corridors outside the left and right student dormitories, with a plane depth of about 80 m, a courtyard size of 23 m × 38 m, all buildings are single-story, about 10 m high, with pleasant spatial scale and a tranquil, distant atmosphere.

The fourth courtyard, with the Yunzhi Hall as the core lecture area, is the largest and most important courtyard in Guanzhong academy, with a plane size of 28 m × 73 m; viewed from afar, the Yunzhi Hall at the end of the axis gives a distant and sacred feeling.

The fifth courtyard is the sacrificial area of the academy, located at the end of the academy, enclosed by Jingyi Hall and side rooms on both sides, with the main function of sacrificing to the academy's former sages.

Zeyuan is an independent courtyard, meeting the quiet and serene environmental requirements for sacrifice. This also becomes its biggest difference from other courtyards in the academy.

Yunzhi Hall is the lecture hall of Guanzhong academy and the overall core of the building complex.

'Yunzhi' comes from the phrase 'Yunzhi Juezhong' in the 'Doctrine of the Mean,' meaning following the Confucian doctrine of the mean, and also暗合 the 'Zhong' (center) of Guanzhong.

The existing Yunzhi Hall was rebuilt according to its original appearance in the early 1990s; it is five bays wide and five bays deep, with an area of 889 square meters.

The building is formed by a flush gable roof and a rolling-roof goudianda (interlocking roof), with a one-bay hip-and-gable抱厦 protruding from the front eave and a three-bay hip-and-gable抱厦 protruding from the rear eave, presenting a凸-shaped plane.

The eave outline is treated with a relatively large amplitude of change, and the wing corner treatment is more flexible than other buildings, with a restrained and solemn architectural style.

Jingyi Hall is the sacrificial hall of Guanzhong academy, an indispensable element of the academy, used to enshrine and commemorate the school's masters and the founders of the academy, serving the function of 'looking back at the academy's founding, must worship former sages, to rectify the learning lineage.' It forms an independent courtyard with the left and right side rooms, called Zeyuan, located at the very end of the academy's central axis.

The building is the center of the rear courtyard, with a large scale and magnificent form. It is five bays wide, 10.2 m long north-south and 17.8 m wide east-west, with an area of about 182 square meters, with a flush gable roof.

In the center of the house, the Daotong (orthodox lineage) spirit tablet is set, and on both sides are the spirit tablets of famous ministers of Zhengxue (orthodox learning) and Lixue (Neo-Confucianism).

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 关中书院位于现陕西省西安市城南门内书院门街
  • 为西北四大书院之首
  • 是明、清两代陕西的最高学府
  • 虽历经400年风雨
  • 仍比较完整地保留了空间院落形态及建筑单体的形制
  • 是一座研究我国传统建筑文化不可多得的活的书院
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 据咸宁县志记载
  • 关中书院始建于明万历三十七年
  • 盖因陕西著名学者冯从吾讲学的宝庆寺难以容纳
  • 故将其东邻小悉园改建为书院
  • 创建之初
  • 书院坐北面南
  • 延绥抚台涂宗浚捐建讲堂六间
  • 并提名为允执堂

78. 眉县横渠书院

Mei County Hengqu Academy
元、明、清 书院建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 宝鸡市眉县城东23公里处的横渠镇

横渠书院位于陕西省宝鸡市眉县城东23公里处的横渠镇。书院南靠太白山国家森林公园，北临佛教圣地法门寺，东与道教源地楼观台相连，西与名胜诸葛亮庙、钓鱼台、周公庙、金台观遥遥相望。

1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。横渠书院又称张载祠，其前身为崇寿院。张载是北宋著名的哲学家、思想家、教育家，理学的奠基人之一，关学的创始人及领袖。其哲学思想对中国近千年的影响极为深远。

张载在38岁中进士前一直研读和讲学于崇寿院，晚年辞官隐居在此兴馆设教。他死后，人们为了纪念这位关学宗师，将崇寿院改为横渠书院。

元代元贞元年（1295年）在书院的旧址上修建了张载祠，为后祠前书院式格局。

泰定四年（1327年）诏建横渠书院，文礼恺有记。

明洪武十四年（1381年），眉令林思正修书院，万历三年（1575年）知县姚继先重修书院。

清康熙二十三年（1684年），眉令陈石麟重修书院。康熙五十一年（1712年）、光绪十一年（1885年），曾两次重修书院。

光绪三十二年（1905年），改书院为眉县第一高等小学堂。宣统元年（1909年），书院改为眉县第二高等小学堂。

民国时改为中山街小学，中华人民共和国成立后，名称几变，直至1982年更名为眉县实验小学。现为张载纪念馆。横渠书院坐北向南，南北长82米，东西宽37.5米。建筑以轴线为中心对称排列。

主要建筑有献殿、东西厢房、山门、后殿、学堂等。建筑风格以宋式为主，兼有明清建筑特色。院内现存清康熙皇帝御匾学达性天、院印、族谱、明清版本《张子全书》和印版（木质印版等原物）等文物，以及北宋以来文人墨客留下的碑石50余块，大殿内立有塑像以及长达60米的巨幅壁画。

献殿为横渠书院主体建筑之一，坐北向南，东西长16.5米，南北宽7.5米。面阔五间，进深三间。

柱径0.36米，柱高4.07米，台基高0.25米，廊柱径0.3米，高3.5米，廊深1.6米，砖包土坯墙，无斗拱。

硬山屋顶，施用布板筒瓦，有勾头滴水，蔓草雕花脊，门窗已改造。

— ENGLISH —

Hengqu academy is located in Hengqu Town, 23 km east of Mei County town, Baoji City, Shaanxi Province.

The academy backs onto the Taibaishan National Forest Park to the south, faces the Buddhist sacred site Famen Temple to the north, connects with the Taoist source Louguantai to the east, and faces the scenic Zhuge Liang Temple, Diaoyutai, Zhougong Temple, and Jintaiguan across the distance to the west.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992. Hengqu academy is also called the Zhang Zai Shrine, and its predecessor was the Chongshou Yuan.

Zhang Zai was a famous philosopher, thinker, and educator of the Northern Song dynasty, one of the founders of Neo-Confucianism, and the founder and leader of the Guanxue (Guanzhong Learning) school.

His philosophical thought had an extremely profound influence on China for nearly a thousand years.

Before passing the imperial examination at the age of 38, Zhang Zai had been studying and lecturing at the Chongshou Yuan; in his later years, he resigned from office and lived in seclusion here, establishing halls and teaching.

After his death, people renamed the Chongshou Yuan to Hengqu academy to commemorate this master of Guanxue.

In the 1st year of Yuanzhen, Yuan (1295), the Zhang Zai Shrine was built on the site of the academy, in a layout of 'rear shrine, front academy.' In the 4th year of Taiding (1327), an edict was issued to build Hengqu academy, with a record by Wen Likai.

In the 14th year of Hongwu, Ming (1381), Lin Sizheng, the county magistrate of Mei, repaired the academy; in the 3rd year of Wanli (1575), County Magistrate Yao Jixian rebuilt the academy.

In the 23rd year of Kangxi, Qing (1684), Chen Shilin, the county magistrate of Mei, rebuilt the academy. In the 51st year of Kangxi (1712) and the 11th year of Guangxu (1885), the academy was rebuilt twice.

In the 32nd year of Guangxu (1905), the academy was changed to the Mei County First Higher Primary School. In the 1st year of Xuantong (1909), the academy was changed to the Mei County Second Higher Primary School.

During the Republic period, it was changed to Zhongshan Street Primary School; after the founding of the People's Republic of China, its name changed several times, until it was renamed Mei County Experimental Primary School in 1982. It is now the Zhang Zai Memorial Hall.

Hengqu academy faces south from north, 82 m long north-south and 37.5 m wide east-west. The buildings are symmetrically arranged with the axis as the center. The main buildings include the offering hall, east and west wing rooms, mountain gate, rear hall, and school rooms.

The architectural style is mainly Song-style, with Ming-Qing architectural features.

In the courtyard, there are existing cultural relics such as the imperial plaque 'Xue Da Xing Tian' (Learning Reaches Human Nature) by Emperor Kangxi of Qing, academy seals, genealogy, Ming-Qing editions of 'Zhangzi Quanshu' (Complete Works of Master Zhang) and printing plates (wooden printing plates and other original objects), as well as more than 50 stone steles left by literati since the Northern Song dynasty; in the main hall, there are statues and a huge mural 60 m long.

The offering hall is one of the main buildings of Hengqu academy, facing south from north, 16.5 m long east-west and 7.5 m wide north-south. Five bays wide, three bays deep.

Column diameter 0.36 m, column height 4.07 m, platform base height 0.25 m, corridor column diameter 0.3 m, height 3.5 m, corridor depth 1.6 m, brick-clad adobe walls, without dougong.

flush gable roof, using cloth board and tube tiles, with gutou (tile cap) and dishui (drip tile), vine-flower carved ridge, with doors and windows already modified.

关键特征 / Key Features

  • 书院南靠太白山国家森林公园
  • 北临佛教圣地法门寺
  • 东与道教源地楼观台相连
  • 西与名胜诸葛亮庙、钓鱼台、周公庙、金台观遥遥相望
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 横渠书院又称张载祠
  • 其前身为崇寿院
  • 张载是北宋著名的哲学家、思想家、教育家
  • 理学的奠基人之一
  • 关学的创始人及领袖
  • 其哲学思想对中国近千年的影响极为深远
  • 张载在38岁中进士前一直研读和讲学于崇寿院
  • 晚年辞官隐居在此兴馆设教
  • 他死后
  • 人们为了纪念这位关学宗师

79. 蒲城考场

Pucheng Examination Hall
清 考院建筑 陕西省重点文物保护单位 渭南市蒲城县城东槐院巷17号

蒲城考场位于渭南市蒲城县城东槐院巷17号，是陕西省唯一一座保存完好的清代科举考试场馆。1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位。

考场始建于清光绪二十一年（1895年），光绪三十二年（1906年）废除科举，考院改为蒲城县高等小学，后改为东槐院小学，1999年东槐院小学迁出，遂恢复原貌。

蒲城考场坐北向南，结构严谨，主体为两进规整的四合院组成，占地约6200平方米。建筑临街大影壁两边各有一道辕门，门内两侧曾建有小型鼓楼、钟楼，今已不存。

大门面阔三间，高约4米，两侧八字屏墙。屏墙上雕刻有金鸡报晓、凤凰展翅图案。大门与二门之间有一道可关启的木板影壁，大门以内为考场大院，中间为一条砖铺甬道，两边有对称的24间号舍。

两侧为狭长庭院，甬道尽头为月亮门，月亮门上方，南北两面各有一砖刻匾额，南额书腾蛟起凤，北额书紫电青霜，其后为考官阅卷、面阔五间的伦秀堂。伦秀堂南两侧各有三间房屋，旧称官厅，在考试期间作为监考考官的临时休息场所。旧时檐下3米处各设有一焚纸楼，作弊考生试卷都在此焚烧。向西是十间全形房屋，西头间内有一口井，称井房，是人们盥洗沐浴的场所，旧时称为浴室院。

清时称蒲城县为同州府，一州设三县，各县应试的童生都要考前净身，一是作为对孔圣人的崇敬；二是监考官届时可详细查验所有学生的衣物，查验是否藏有夹带。堂后有东西厢房各五间，内室三间，厢房是考院工作人员平日居住之处；内室是省巡视人员来时接待居住之用。蒲城考场建筑均为青砖灰瓦、硬山顶，小式大木结构。

建筑上石、木、砖雕十分精致，使整座建筑表现出一种庄重素雅的风格。

— ENGLISH —

Pucheng examination hall is located at No. 17 Donghuaiyuan Lane, east of Pucheng County town, Weinan City. It is the only well-preserved Qing dynasty imperial examination venue in Shaanxi Province.

It was designated as a Shaanxi Provincial Key Cultural Heritage Site in 1992.

The examination hall was first built in the 21st year of Guangxu, Qing (1895); in the 32nd year of Guangxu (1906), the imperial examination system was abolished, and the examination hall was changed to Pucheng County Higher Primary School, later changed to Donghuaiyuan Primary School; in 1999, Donghuaiyuan Primary School moved out, and the original appearance was restored.

Pucheng examination hall faces south from north, with a rigorous structure; the main body consists of two regular siheyuan (courtyard) complexes, covering about 6,200 square meters.

On both sides of the large screen wall facing the street, there is one yuan gate each; inside the gate, small drum towers and bell towers were once built on both sides, which no longer exist.

The main gate is three bays wide, about 4 m high, with八字 screen walls on both sides. On the screen walls, patterns of 'Golden Chicken Announcing Dawn' and 'Phoenix Spreading Wings' are carved.

Between the main gate and the second gate, there is a closable wooden board screen wall; inside the main gate is the examination hall courtyard, with a brick-paved path in the middle and 24 symmetric examination cells on both sides.

On both sides are narrow courtyards; at the end of the path is a moon gate, above the moon gate, on both the north and south sides, there is one brick-carved plaque each, with the south plaque inscribed 'Teng Jiao Qi Feng' (Soaring Dragon and Rising Phoenix) and the north plaque inscribed 'Zi Dian Qing Shuang' (Purple Lightning and Blue Frost); behind it is the Lunxiu Hall, five bays wide, where examiners graded papers.

On both sides south of the Lunxiu Hall, there are three rooms each, formerly called official halls, serving as temporary rest places for invigilators during examinations.

In old times, at 3 m under the eaves, there was one paper-burning tower each, where the examination papers of cheating candidates were burned.

To the west are ten complete rooms; in the westernmost room, there is a well, called the well room, a place for people to wash and bathe, formerly called the bathroom courtyard.

In the Qing dynasty, Pucheng County was called Tongzhou Prefecture, with one prefecture and three counties; the child candidates from each county had to purify themselves before the examination, first as a reverence for Confucius, and second so that invigilators could carefully inspect all students' clothing to check whether they had hidden cheat sheets.

Behind the hall, there are five east and west wing rooms each, and three inner rooms; the wing rooms are the daily residences of the examination hall staff; the inner rooms are for receiving and accommodating provincial inspectors when they come.

The buildings of Pucheng examination hall are all blue bricks and gray tiles, with flush gable roofs and small-style large wooden structures.

The stone, wood, and brick carvings on the buildings are very exquisite, giving the entire building a solemn and elegant style.

  • 是陕西省唯一一座保存完好的清代科举考试场馆
  • 1992年公布为陕西省重点文物保护单位
  • 考场始建于清光绪二十一年
  • 光绪三十二年废除科举
  • 考院改为蒲城县高等小学
  • 后改为东槐院小学
  • 1999年东槐院小学迁出
  • 遂恢复原貌
  • 蒲城考场坐北向南
  • 结构严谨
  • 主体为两进规整的四合院组成
